ARM A -- BOUNDING THE PRIZE FROM REPLACING THE TERRAIN DRAW PATH
================================================================
Date        2026-08-19
Box         AMD Radeon RX 7800 XT, D3D12, SM6, UE 5.8
Harness     tools/voxel-run-gpu-arm.ps1 (frame distribution + deferred ProfileGPU in ONE leg)
Site        -VoxelSpawnAt=-61440,-61440 (METRES), -Flight static, sun pinned 12:00 03-20, TimeScale 0
Output res  2560x1440 (TSR-upscaled from 1552x873 internal)
Worldgen    v28 C++ / v28 .ush (mirror bumped this session), assets ON

WHY NOT THE HISTORICAL SPAWN. -84480,53760 needs fine tile (-6,3); the current
provider terrain-diffusion-unlabeled-80b9ca451a23eae4-b19d281fd holds 15 tiles,
none at positive Y. VoxelFineTileStreamer.cpp:891 is FATAL on an unattended run
by design, so the leg dies rather than degrading. These numbers therefore CANNOT
be compared against the 2026-07-28 baselines -- different site, and a world that
has since gained assets and a biome rebalance. A0 vs A3 is a within-session A/B
and that is the comparison being made.

THE ARMS
--------
A0  shipped defaults                                    (control)
A3  voxel.Stream.GPUCullDebugDrawNothing 3              submit NOTHING (camera+shadow),
                                                        cull walk and range emit still run

RESULT
------
  arm                                     p50        p95      frames   hitches
  A0  control                           18.99 ms   21.52 ms    11,465     201
  A3  mask 3 -- submit nothing,          4.08 ms    5.03 ms    52,140     170
      cull walk + range emit still run
  A3  mask 7 -- pool does nothing        3.55 ms    4.85 ms    59,678     147

FULL DECOMPOSITION OF AN 18.99 ms FRAME
  GPU raster + submission   A0 - mask3   = 14.91 ms   78.5%   <- what a marcher replaces
  render-thread walk + emit mask3 - mask7=  0.53 ms    2.8%   <- what a marcher deletes
  everything else           mask7        =  3.55 ms   18.7%   <- the floor it cannot beat
                                           -------
  total terrain prize                      15.44 ms   81.3%

A verify leg after the 14.82 GB tile-cache cleanup reproduced the control at
p50 18.81 ms (0.9% from 18.99, inside noise), 0 gate leaks, 50,554 chunks --
so the cleanup is not in any of these numbers.

WHAT THE MARCHER MUST BEAT
  < 14.91 ms   to be a win at all
  ~  7 ms      to roughly double the frame rate (3.55 + 7 = 10.5 ms, ~95 fps)
  ~  2-4 ms    would give 5.5-7.5 ms, i.e. 133-180 fps -- 2.5-3.4x
  The floor is 3.55 ms and no marcher can go below it.

A0 GPU CAPTURE (deferred ProfileGPU, mid-run, frame 6348)
---------------------------------------------------------
  GPU frame            17.514 ms   12,138 draws   43,681,258 prims   130,860,882 verts
    SceneRender        17.449
      PrePass DDM       7.703      <-- 44.0% of the GPU frame
      BasePass          7.542      <-- 43.1%
      PostProcessing    1.405      (TSR 1.276)
    WorldTick           0.042      <-- meshing is ~nothing: settled static pose

  PrePass + BasePass = 15.245 ms = 87.0% of the GPU frame.

  Cull census: runs=50485 runQuads=46,798,900 visibleQuads=10,885,999 (23.3%)
               ranges=5571 frustum-rejected=39,520  shadowGather=0

MASK 7 GPU CAPTURE -- AND AN INDEPENDENT CROSS-CHECK
---------------------------------------------------
  GPU frame             2.164 ms   (A0: 17.514 ms)
    SceneRender         2.142
      PostProcessing    1.327      <-- now the LARGEST pass
        TSR             1.208
      PrePass/BasePass  do not appear among the top passes at all

  The frame is still rendering: post-processing and TSR cost what they always
  cost. This is not a blank or skipped frame, which is the obvious way a result
  this large could be an artifact.

  TWO INDEPENDENT INSTRUMENTS AGREE TO 0.1 ms:
    CPU  post-warmup p50   18.99 -> 3.55  = 15.44 ms removed
    GPU  ProfileGPU frame  17.51 -> 2.16  = 15.35 ms removed
  They are measured by different mechanisms on different threads. Agreement at
  this scale is the strongest available evidence that the suppression does what
  it claims and that the prize is real.

  MARCHER GPU BUDGET: the floor is 2.16 ms of GPU, of which 1.33 ms is
  post/TSR. Everything a marcher spends is ON TOP of 2.16 ms.

WHAT THIS SETTLES
-----------------
1. THE GATE PASSES, and not marginally. The plan's kill criterion was
   A0-A3 < 3 ms; the "proceed unconditionally" threshold was > 8 ms.
   Measured: 14.91 ms.

2. THE RESIDUAL FLOOR IS 4.08 ms, NOT ~9.5 ms. backlog.md 0.1b attributes
   ~9.5 ms of the 13.7 ms render thread to "not the voxel pool" --
   AVoxelClipmapActor, water and its pool, sky, base/post. At this site, with
   the terrain draw removed, the WHOLE frame is 4.08 ms. So the non-terrain
   floor is far lower than the July attribution implied, and the marcher's
   ceiling is correspondingly higher. The plan's "render thread becomes the
   bound at ~9.5 ms, i.e. ~100 fps" is too pessimistic HERE.
   (Caveat: static settled pose, no streaming; and A3 still pays the cull
   walk + range emit, so 4.08 ms is an UPPER bound on the floor.)

3. shadowGather=0 AGAIN, at a second site, at noon, with a live sun. The
   18.4 ms July frame and this 17.5 ms one both contain NO terrain shadow
   casting through the gather path. Cause still unconfirmed -- Virtual Shadow
   Map page caching is the leading hypothesis (Nanite on => VSM on; sun frozen;
   VSM's dynamic path does not route through GetDynamicMeshElementsShadowCullFrustum,
   which is exactly the predicate the census counts). Falsifier, one run, no
   code: re-capture with r.Shadow.Virtual.Enable 0.

4. Meshing is NOT the frame here. WorldTick 0.042 ms on a settled pose. The
   21-28% figure from July was a flight leg with terrain arriving.

MASK 7's FIRST ATTEMPT VOIDED -- AND IT WAS FLAKY, NOT THE CODE
The first mask-7 leg exited abruptly 51 s in with no fatal error, no exit reason
and NO CRASH DUMP (the newest dump in ue-project/Saved/Crashes is from an earlier
fine-tile gate failure). The harness only force-kills at 600 s, so it
self-terminated. Re-run unchanged: 270 s, clean, p50 3.55 ms. RecordGather is
self-contained (atomics + logging; nothing it reads is set up by the walk it now
skips), so the bit-4 path is safe by inspection as well as by re-run. GPU-mesh
readback timeouts and poolSize=0 appear in ALL legs including every one that
succeeded, so neither was ever the cause.

NOTE ON READING THE CENSUS UNDER MASK 3
The `drawnQuads=` field is printed from inside BuildCulledRanges, so under mask 3
it reports what the cull INTENDED to draw -- the walk still runs. Submitted
geometry is zero; the 14.91 ms is the proof. Under mask 7 the walk is skipped and
the line does not print at all.

INSTRUMENT NOTE
---------------
voxel.Stream.GPUCullDebugDrawNothing is new (VoxelGpuPoolComponent.cpp), a
bitmask: 1 = camera gathers, 2 = shadow gathers, 4 = also skip the cull walk and
range emit. Suppressed gathers are still RECORDED at zero, mirroring the
AllCapped path, so an absent stat and a measured zero stay distinguishable. The
census line's drawnQuads still reports what the cull WOULD have drawn under
mask 3 (the walk runs); submitted geometry is zero, which the 14.91 ms confirms.

================================================================
GATE 0-b -- IS RESOLUTION STILL FREE?  (2026-08-19, same site/pose)
================================================================
  1600x900    p50 18.95 ms   p95 21.49 ms
  2560x1440   p50 18.99 ms   p95 21.52 ms
  -> 2.5x the INTERNAL pixels (~0.536M vs 1.355M after TSR's ~61%) for 0.2%.

YES, STILL FREE. The 2026-07-28 finding (30.61 ms @900p vs 30.59 @1440p) carried
a standing warning to re-check it after any change, and the world has since
gained environment assets, a biome rebalance, and 2.5x the quad density. It
survives all of that. The renderer is still ENTIRELY geometry- and
submission-bound.

TWO CONSEQUENCES, PULLING OPPOSITE WAYS:

1. GOOD: every 900p-derived conclusion in docs/measurements/ is still valid, and
   900p remains a legitimate way to run cheaper sweeps. That was in doubt.

2. BAD, AND IT IS THE MARCHER'S ONE REAL REGRESSION: this property EXISTS ONLY
   BECAUSE THE FRAME IS GEOMETRY-BOUND. A ray marcher's cost is O(pixels) by
   construction, so it TRADES this away. After the marcher, resolution stops
   being free and becomes roughly linear. Today 2.5x the pixels costs 0.04 ms;
   afterwards it costs 2.5x the march.

   That makes the ~61% internal render (r.ScreenPercentage default) load-bearing
   TWICE: DefaultEngine.ini:70-73 already keeps it for TSR stability, and it now
   also caps the marcher's bill. And it makes dynamic resolution a real lever on
   this project for the first time -- there was no point in it while pixels were
   free.

   It also means the march spike MUST be measured at more than one resolution.
   The scaling curve is the deliverable, not the single number.

================================================================
SHADOWS -- CONFIRMED ABSENT, AND VSM IS NOT THE REASON
================================================================
Arm: A0 defaults + `r.Shadow.Virtual.Enable 0`, same site/pose, sun at +38.07 deg.

  census[window]: shadowGathers=0 MEASURED (not absent) over 60 camera gathers
  cumulative:     camGathers=3840  shadowGathers=0  camQuads=3.84e10  shadowQuads=0

So the pooled terrain contributes ZERO shadow-cascade draws, cumulatively, over
3,840 camera gathers, with Virtual Shadow Maps disabled. The VSM-page-caching
hypothesis is dead. The proxy DOES report shadow relevance
(`Result.bShadowRelevance = IsShadowCast(View)`, VoxelGpuPoolComponent.cpp:873),
so relevance is not the gate either. Mechanism still unidentified.

A TRAP THAT NEARLY COST A WRONG CONCLUSION, RECORDED SO IT DOES NOT AGAIN:
  the per-line field `shadowGather=%d` in the `VoxelGpuPool cull:` log is
  `bShadowGather ? 1 : 0` -- a BOOLEAN describing the ONE sampled gather, and
  that line only prints every 601st gather. `shadowGather=0` on it means "the
  gather that happened to be sampled was a camera gather". It is NOT a count and
  must never be quoted as evidence that no shadow gathers occurred.
  The CENSUS (`voxel.Stream.GPUCullStatsPeriod`) is the evidence: it accumulates
  into separate camera/shadow counters and says so explicitly, including the
  words "MEASURED (not absent)" -- which exists precisely because an absent stat
  and a measured zero must not look alike.

WHAT IT MEANS FOR THIS PROJECT
  The 18.99 ms baseline, and July's 18.4 ms, are BEST CASES: neither contains
  any terrain shadow cost. If terrain shadow casting is ever switched on, the
  raster path pays up to four more cascade passes over ~44M primitives, while a
  marcher pays one wave-uniform secondary ray. So the marcher's advantage is
  UNDERSTATED by every number in this file.
  Quote the speedup twice, and say which: "against the measured shadowless
  frame" and "against the same scene with shadows live".

WHETHER IT IS A BUG IS A SEPARATE QUESTION and is not answered here. Terrain
that casts no dynamic shadow is a visual gap, not just a perf saving. Flagged,
not chased.

Every one of these is the same scene and pose under a change that should not
move the frame, and none of them moves it. The Arm A delta is 14.91 ms --
about 80x the spread. No conclusion in this file rests on a difference near
the noise floor.

  VSM OFF COSTS NOTHING: 18.96 vs 18.99, and the GPU split is unchanged
  (PrePass 7.783 / BasePass 7.501 against 7.703 / 7.542). NO ShadowDepths pass
  appears in either capture. Terrain shadow casting is absent by some mechanism
  other than VSM, and disabling VSM neither reveals it nor costs anything.

================================================================
ARM A-ring -- IS THE CHEAPER HEIGHTFIELD PROJECT THE BETTER MOVE?
================================================================
July's session summary proposed replacing the DISTANT rings (L3-L5, sub-pixel
voxels) with a heightfield instead of building a marcher -- far less work. That
is only the better move if the cost actually lives out there, so it is measured
rather than argued. -VoxelMaxRingLevel= against the 6-ring control, same site
and pose. (voxel-run-gpu-arm.ps1 gained -ExtraArgs for this: ring composition is
read before the first RecomputeDesiredSet, so it cannot be a cvar.)

QUADS RESIDENT PER LEVEL at the control (VoxelGpuPool `pool:` line):
  L0 8,596,214 (18.4%)   L1 7,082,957 (15.1%)   L2 8,774,966 (18.8%)
  L3 8,236,142 (17.6%)   L4 7,302,611 (15.6%)   L5 6,806,010 (14.5%)
  total 46,798,900

  NEARLY UNIFORM. No ring exceeds 18.8%. This confirms gpu-g0-sizing.md's
  "ring counts are flat by construction" at the quad level, and it CORRECTS an
  earlier draft of the ray-marching plan which claimed R0 was ~80% of the
  resident set. R0 is 18.4%.

  Consequence for "finer voxels": taking R0 from 10 cm to 5 cm multiplies R0's
  share by ~4 and the WHOLE WORLD by only ~1.55x. Much cheaper than assumed.

FRAME COST BY RING (floor = 3.55 ms, from mask 7)
  arm      rings   quads        p50        draw cost (p50 - 3.55)
  ring0    L0      8.60M  18.4%  6.94 ms    3.39 ms   22% of the draw
  ring2    L0-L2  24.45M  52.3%  (pending)
  ring4    L0-L4  39.99M  85.5%  (pending)
  A0       L0-L5  46.80M 100.0% 18.99 ms   15.44 ms  100%

  R0 carries 18.4% of the quads and 22% of the draw cost -- cost tracks quad
  count close to linearly, which is what a submission-bound renderer should do.

CONTAMINATION DISCLOSURE -- three legs shared the box
================================================================
This project voids BOTH sides of an overlap, so these are named rather than
quietly kept.

  09:50      UBT build (8 actions, 6 cores, ~40 s)  -> overlaps p0-vsm-off-r1's tail
  ~09:53     UBT build (4 actions, ~33 s)           -> overlaps p0-ring0-r1
  ~09:58     vxc_volumeprobe --radius 32 (CPU)      -> overlaps p0-ring2-r1

  CONTAMINATED (re-run before quoting): p0-vsm-off-r1, p0-ring0-r1, p0-ring2-r1
  CLEAN: armA-A0-control, armA-A3-mask3, armA-A3-mask7b, armA-A0-verify,
         p0-gate0b-900p-r1

  Both build overlaps were a subagent building the G1 spike; the probe overlap
  was mine. Neither result looks moved -- vsm-off 18.96 sits inside the 0.18 ms
  spread of the four clean A0-equivalents, and ring0/ring2 sit on a straight
  line through the control -- but "looks unmoved" is not the standard this
  project uses, and the headline numbers (14.91 / 0.53 / 3.55) all come from
  CLEAN legs.

  The rule that catches this after the fact is tools/voxel-audit-leg-overlap.ps1,
  which rebuilds each leg's span from log timestamps. It only sees LEGS, though
  -- a build or a bench probe is invisible to it. Anything CPU-heavy counts.

RING ABLATION -- COMPLETE
  arm      rings   quads          p50       draw (p50-3.55)   draw share
  ring0    L0       8.60M  18.4%   6.94 ms    3.39 ms          22.0%
  ring2    L0-L2   24.45M  52.3%  11.25 ms    7.70 ms          49.9%
  ring4    L0-L4   36.55M  78.1%  16.02 ms   12.47 ms          80.8%
  A0       L0-L5   46.80M 100.0%  18.99 ms   15.44 ms         100.0%

  Draw cost tracks QUAD COUNT almost exactly (22.0 vs 18.4, 49.9 vs 52.3,
  80.8 vs 78.1). That is what a submission-bound renderer should do, and it
  means no ring is disproportionately expensive: there is no cheap subset to
  attack.

THE VERDICT ON THE COMPETING ARCHITECTURE
  ring2 IS July's proposal measured: keep true voxels to 512 m, draw L3-L5 as
  heightfield. Its FLOOR is 11.25 ms (89 fps) -- floor, because a heightfield
  is not free and this arm draws nothing at all out there.

    do nothing                18.99 ms    53 fps
    heightfield distant rings >= 11.25 ms  <= 89 fps   1.69x   (weeks)
    ray marcher                3.55 + march ms                 (months)

  So the marcher must land the march UNDER 7.70 ms merely to TIE the cheaper
  project, and well under it to justify the difference in cost. That is a far
  tighter bar than "under the 14.91 ms it replaces", and it is the number the
  G1 spike is now being measured against.

  What the heightfield does NOT do, and should weigh alongside the 1.69x:
    - it does not fix the pool saturation (34,937 chunks refused today)
    - it does not unblock the 42 authored species (palette wiring)
    - it does not enable finer voxels anywhere
    - it gives up true voxel geometry beyond 512 m permanently

================================================================
G1 SPIKE -- THE SUBSTRATE ASSUMPTION WAS WRONG
================================================================
The plan (and my brief to the G1 agent) said FVoxelFluidOccupancy is "a
ready-made 51.2 m test volume a marcher spike can consume TODAY with zero new
producer work". The first spike leg says otherwise:

  Fluid perf [idle: no readback yet] alive=0 spawned=0 ... marchGpuMs=off
                                      occupancy=0/0

  marchGpuMs prints "off" because RenderStats.MarchStepBudget is 0, and that is
  0 because the pass never ran, and the pass never ran because the extension's
  spike clause requires State->OccupancyKeepAlive.IsValid().

WHY: the occupancy volume holds TERRAIN bits, but its REGION is driven by the
fluid sim's active area. With no particles spawned there is no active region,
so the volume is never allocated -- `occupancy=0/0`. The data is terrain; the
placement is fluid's. The brief conflated the two, and so did the plan.

  NOTE THE INSTRUMENT FLAW THIS EXPOSES: the field prints "off", and its own
  comment says *"off is only ever voxel.Marcher.Spike 0"*. Here the cvar was 1
  and it still printed "off". An armed-but-never-ran spike is exactly the
  "pending" case the field was built to distinguish, and it lands in the wrong
  bucket. Worth fixing before anyone reads a spike result.

TWO WAYS FORWARD
  (a) `voxel.Fluid.Spawn <n>` seeds a dam-break above the camera, which creates
      the active region and builds the volume. Zero code. Cost: the SOLVER now
      runs beside the march, so simGpuMs is non-zero and the frame is not the
      one the other arms were measured on. Separable (the march has its own
      timestamp ring) but not clean.
  (b) A mode that centres the occupancy volume on the CAMERA regardless of
      fluid. Small change, and it is what the spike actually wants: terrain
      occupancy around the viewer, no solver.

  (a) is being tried first because it needs no code.

  WORKAROUND (a) WORKS. `voxel.DeferExec 70 voxel.Fluid.Spawn 40000` creates the
  active region; the volume then packs over ~60 s and the field walks
  off -> pending -> a number:

    occupancy=2/510    marchGpuMs=pending
    occupancy=297/215  marchGpuMs=4.38(budget=886,nofetch=0)
    occupancy=343/169  marchGpuMs=4.67
    occupancy=512/0    marchGpuMs=5.14 .. 5.19   <- CONVERGED

  THE COST RISES AS THE VOLUME FILLS, exactly as the G1 agent warned: a
  half-packed volume stops rays at the fill frontier and reports a cheap march
  for the wrong reason. 4.38 would have been a 15% understatement. ANY spike
  number taken before `occupancy=N/0` is void.

FIRST G1 NUMBER: 5.19 ms, budget 886, 2560x1440 output (1552x873 internal)
  and it does NOT clear the bar comfortably.

  bar to beat the heightfield project      7.70 ms
  measured march, dense 51.2 m volume      5.19 ms
  headroom                                 2.51 ms (33%)

  AND THE MEASURED NUMBER IS FLATTERED IN ONE BIG WAY AND PENALISED IN ANOTHER:
    flattered -- reach is 51.2 m of volume, ~25.6 m from the camera at centre.
                 The real marcher must cover 4 km. The G1 agent's own estimate
                 for the real thing is 1.4-1.6x the spike (palette fetch at the
                 hit, sparse indirection, ring transitions). 1.4 x 5.19 = 7.3 ms
                 -- AT THE BAR, before any of the 4 km is paid for.
    penalised -- dense single-level volume, no empty-space skipping, no mip
                 pyramid. Worst case per metre, best case per unit range. A
                 brick pyramid is exactly what would fix this, and it is
                 unmeasured.
    confound  -- the fluid solver was running 40,000 particles beside the march
                 (the workaround's cost). The march has its own timestamp ring,
                 but the two contend for the GPU.

  So the honest reading is NOT "the marcher wins". It is: the march is the same
  ORDER as the bar, and which side of it the real thing lands on depends
  entirely on whether the acceleration structure buys back what 4 km costs.
  The decomposition arms (S1 overhead, S5 no-fetch, S2/S3 step scaling) decide
  whether that is plausible, because they say whether the 5.19 ms is memory
  (fixable by a pyramid) or steps/ALU (not).

  POSE CHECK -- the arms above ARE marching terrain, not air.
  The static pose puts the camera at 219,258 UU with the path centre at
  221,758 UU, i.e. ~60 m above terrain (-VoxelPerfDepth default). The
  fluid-anchored occupancy box is GROUND-anchored (~13 m below surface to
  ~38 m above), so rays leave the camera, cross ~22 m of air, enter the box
  from above and hit ground inside it. Terrain is in the box.

  THIS MATTERS FOR THE NEXT ROUND. The G1 agent has since added
  `voxel.Marcher.SpikeVolume 1`, which is solver-free (better) but anchors Z to
  the CAMERA (worse at this pose): at ground+60 m the box would span roughly
  ground+34 to ground+86 and be almost entirely AIR. Marching nothing is cheap
  and the timer would be honest about a meaningless scene.
  => the solver-free re-run must also pass `-VoxelPerfDepth=2` (or similar) so
  the camera sits at a realistic eye height and the box straddles the surface.

G1 STEP-BUDGET CURVE (converged volume, occupancy=512/0, fluid-anchored box)
  budget      marchGpuMs
       1        0.13      <- pass overhead: dispatch, ray setup, full-screen quad
      64        0.87
     886        5.19      <- full 51.2 m box diagonal
   (256, no-fetch, 900p pending)

  THE CURVE IS STRONGLY SUBLINEAR, and that is the most useful thing it says.
  A linear fit through b1->b64 (0.0117 ms/step) predicts 10.5 ms at 886; the
  measured value is 5.19. Marginal cost per step FALLS from 0.0117 (1->64) to
  0.00525 (64->886).

  Meaning: most rays terminate on a hit early, and the total is dominated by the
  MINORITY of rays that travel far -- sky rays and grazing rays. That is exactly
  the population empty-space skipping and a mip pyramid target, and the census
  independently says 91-96% of the flat index is skippable (occupancy 9.2% at R0
  falling to 3.8% at R5, 19.0 bricks per brick-column at R0 falling to 2.0 at R5).

  SO THE ENCOURAGING READING IS AVAILABLE -- an acceleration structure attacks
  precisely the rays that cost the most -- AND IT IS STILL A PREDICTION. This
  project's standing rule is that a predicted saving does not go on a plan until
  an experiment removes the work and measures it. The spike has no pyramid, so
  it cannot measure this. See the Phase 0 verdict note.

================================================================
RING COMPOSITION IS STRONGLY BIOME-DEPENDENT -- and this site is LIGHT
================================================================
Read off the `VoxelGpuPool pool:` line of the 2026-08-19 biome vista captures.

  pose                       L0     L1     L2     L3     L4     L5    total
  VS_tundra                15.8%  12.9%  17.5%  18.9%  19.2%  15.7%   54.9M
  THIS SITE (-61440,-61440) 18.4%  15.1%  18.8%  17.6%  15.6%  14.5%   46.8M
  VS_desert                31.4%  18.7%  15.4%  14.3%  11.6%   8.6%   67.8M
  VS_rainforest            32.4%  19.1%  15.2%  14.1%  10.8%   8.4%   89.0M
  VS_temperate             51.6%  36.2%  11.0%   1.1%  0.04%  0.02%  200.0M SATURATED

TWO CONSEQUENCES, BOTH IMPORTANT.

1. THE HEIGHTFIELD PROJECT DOES NOT FIX THE SATURATION. At the pose that
   actually saturates, L3-L5 is 1.2% of the pool -- dropping it frees ~2.4M of
   200M quads. The refusals are logged "no room ... at level 5": L0+L1 consumed
   175M of 200M and the OUTER RINGS WERE STARVED. So temperate's near-empty
   outer rings are a SYMPTOM of saturation, not evidence about where geometry
   lives. The saturation is a NEAR-FIELD problem (dense vegetation + composed
   assets inside 256 m) and the heightfield addresses the far field.

2. THE 7.70 ms BAR DOES NOT GENERALISE. It came from ring2 at THIS site, where
   L0-L2 is 52.3% of quads. At temperate L0-L2 is 98.8%, so a heightfield over
   L3-L5 saves ~1% of the draw. The heightfield is worth ~1.69x where the FAR
   field dominates (tundra, alpine, desert) and ~nothing where the NEAR field
   does. The bar is a RANGE, ~7.7 ms to ~18 ms, and the marcher clears it
   comfortably in the case that hurts.

AND TWO CAVEATS ON EVERY FRAME NUMBER IN THIS FILE -- the second is the bigger.

  (i) SITE. This site carries 46.8M quads against temperate's 200M+, roughly a
      QUARTER of the worst case, with a flat ring mix where the worst case is
      88% near-field.

  (ii) NO ASSETS. Every leg in this file ran WITHOUT -VoxelAssetDir. All four
      biome captures ran WITH it. Assets are shipping content composed into
      terrain chunks, and in a dense biome they are most of the geometry:
      tundra-with-assets is 54.9M (close to this site's asset-free 46.8M,
      because tundra is bare) while temperate-with-assets is 200M. So the gap
      is biome density AND assets together, and this file measures neither.

  14.91 ms / 3.55 ms / 11.25 ms are all measured on a LIGHT, ASSET-FREE scene
  and must not be quoted as the worst case. The re-measure must pass
  -VoxelAssetDir=D:/voxelsim/asset-forge/out/engine and -VoxelSpawnAltM=30 to
  match the captures. It is runnable -- the captures needed
  -VoxelPoolCapacityQuads=200000000 to get that far, and even then refused
  34,937 chunks.

G1 STEP-BUDGET CURVE, FILLED IN
  budget      marchGpuMs     marginal ms/step over previous point
       1        0.13         (pass overhead: dispatch, ray setup, screen quad)
      64        0.86         0.0116
     256        3.18         0.0121
     886        5.19         0.0032

  READ THE MARGINALS, NOT THE TOTALS. Cost is essentially LINEAR in steps out to
  256, then the marginal cost collapses by ~4x. 256 steps at 10 cm is 25.6 m --
  exactly the box half-diagonal from a centred camera. So almost every ray is
  still marching at 25.6 m and almost none survives much past it.

  What that says about the real marcher: the expensive population is the rays
  that cross the near field WITHOUT hitting anything -- air between the eye and
  the surface, and grazing rays. The spike pays full price for that air because
  it has NO empty-space skipping at any level. A brick pyramid removes exactly
  that cost, and the census independently measures 91-96% of the flat index as
  skippable.

  It also says the reverse, and this is the part to be careful about: marching
  the near ~25 m ALONE costs 3.18 ms against a bar that is 7.70 ms in
  far-field-dominated scenes. The real marcher must do that near field AND
  everything out to 4 km. If the pyramid does not recover most of the 3.18 ms,
  there is no budget left for the other 3,975 m.

================================================================
G1 DECOMPOSITION -- the march is ~half memory, ~half ALU
================================================================
  b886, fetch      5.21 ms
  b886, no-fetch   2.88 ms   (identical loop and trip count; verified by
                              disassembly to contain zero dx.op.bufferLoad)
  b1               0.13 ms

  memory / "asking the volume"   5.21 - 2.88 = 2.33 ms   45%
  DDA loop itself (ALU)          2.88 - 0.13 = 2.75 ms   53%
  pass overhead                                0.13 ms    2%

  CAVEAT THE G1 AGENT STATED AND IT MATTERS: the no-fetch permutation also
  deletes the ADDRESSING MATHS that feeds the load (toroidal wrap, word index,
  bit mask). So 2.33 ms is "the cost of asking the volume a question", not pure
  bandwidth. Do not quote it as a bandwidth figure.

THE COST MODEL THIS GIVES
  Both halves scale with STEP COUNT -- the no-fetch arm still walks all 886
  steps. So, at 1552x873 internal, on a dense single-level volume:

      march_ms  ~=  0.13  +  steps x 5.9 us
                            (of which 2.6 us is the fetch)

  That is the first extrapolable thing the spike has produced, and it reframes
  the whole question. The marcher's viability is now ONE unknown:

      HOW MANY STEPS DOES A PYRAMID-ACCELERATED 4 km MARCH TAKE?

      200 steps -> 1.3 ms   comfortable
      500 steps -> 3.1 ms   fine
    1,000 steps -> 6.0 ms   marginal
    2,000 steps -> 12  ms   dead

  The spike's 886 steps buy 51.2 m with NO skipping. The census says 91-96% of
  the flat index is skippable (occupancy 9.2% at R0 -> 3.8% at R5) and that a
  brick column holds 19.0 bricks at R0 falling to 2.0 at R5. If a brick-level
  skip plus six mip levels gets a ray to 4 km in a few hundred steps, the
  marcher is comfortable. If it needs thousands, it is not.

  NOBODY HAS THAT NUMBER, and the spike cannot produce it -- it has no pyramid.
  It is measurable cheaply though: a step-count histogram over a real brick
  pyramid is a P1/P2 deliverable, not a months-long commitment.

================================================================
G1 RESOLUTION SCALING -- AND IT OVERTURNS THE "MARCHER GIVES UP FREE
RESOLUTION" CLAIM
================================================================
Same pose, same budget 886, both converged (occupancy=512/0):

  output      internal        internal px   marchGpuMs
  1600x900    ~976x549          0.536M        4.96
  2560x1440   1552x873          1.355M        5.21

  2.53x THE PIXELS FOR 5% MORE COST.

I previously wrote, off the GATE 0-b result, that "resolution is free only
because the frame is geometry-bound, and a marcher trades that away -- cost
becomes ~linear in pixels". THAT IS WRONG between these two resolutions, and
measured wrong rather than argued wrong.

WHY, and it matters for the cost model: the march is LATENCY-bound, not
throughput-bound. Each DDA step's address depends on the previous step's
result, so a wave spends most of its life waiting on dependent loads. At 0.536M
pixels the GPU is not saturated; the extra 0.8M pixels at 1440p largely fill
lanes that were already idle. This is consistent with the ALU/memory split
(2.75 / 2.33 ms) -- neither half is near a hardware limit.

CONSEQUENCES
  - The marcher does NOT obviously give up free resolution. The plan's stated
    "one real regression" is not supported at 900p->1440p.
  - `march_ms ~= 0.13 + steps x 5.9 us` is therefore NOT a per-pixel model. It
    is a per-frame model at ~1.3M internal pixels, dominated by the longest
    waves. Do not scale it by pixel count.
  - It WILL stop being free somewhere. 4K internal (8.3M px) is 6x 1440p and is
    unmeasured. The claim proved here is bounded: 0.54M -> 1.36M internal.
  - It also means the 61% internal render (r.ScreenPercentage default) is NOT
    load-bearing for the marcher's cost the way I claimed -- only for TSR
    stability, which is what DefaultEngine.ini:70-73 always said.

================================================================
G1 SPIKE -- CONSOLIDATED
================================================================
All arms: site -61440,-61440 m, static, camera ~60 m above terrain, converged
volume (occupancy=512/0), fluid-anchored 512^3 box (51.2 m), solver running
40,000 particles (the workaround's cost; the march has its own timestamp ring).

  arm                              output       marchGpuMs
  budget 1        (overhead)      2560x1440        0.13
  budget 64                       2560x1440        0.86
  budget 256      (25.6 m)        2560x1440        3.20
  budget 886      (full diagonal) 2560x1440        5.21
  budget 886, NO-FETCH            2560x1440        2.88
  budget 886                      1600x900         4.96

DERIVED
  memory / "asking the volume"     2.33 ms   45%
  DDA loop (ALU)                   2.75 ms   53%
  pass overhead                    0.13 ms    2%
  cost model (per FRAME, ~1.3M internal px, NOT per pixel):
                                   0.13 + steps x 5.9 us
  resolution 0.54M -> 1.36M px:    +5% for 2.53x the pixels  => latency-bound

WHAT THE SPIKE DOES AND DOES NOT SETTLE
  SETTLES:  the march is latency-bound, roughly half memory / half loop, and
            close to resolution-independent in the measured range. It does NOT
            give up free resolution, contrary to what this plan claimed.
  DOES NOT: whether a 4 km march is affordable. The spike has no pyramid, no
            empty-space skipping, no sparse indirection and 25.6 m of reach.
            Everything that makes 4 km possible is absent by construction.

  THE ONE NUMBER STILL MISSING is the step count for a pyramid-accelerated 4 km
  march. Feed it into the model above and the project decides itself:
      200 steps -> 1.3 ms   500 -> 3.1 ms   1,000 -> 6.0 ms   2,000 -> 12 ms
  It is cheaply measurable (a coarse mip over the volume that already exists +
  a hierarchical march + a step histogram) and is queued as P0.5.

================================================================
G1 CROSS-CHECK -- the march timer is trustworthy AND the march is SERIAL
================================================================
The spike arms also moved the whole-frame p50, and by almost exactly the amount
the march's own GPU timestamp ring reported:

  arm         frame p50   d(frame) vs b1   d(marchGpuMs) vs b1
  b1            19.65        --                --
  b64           20.49       +0.84             +0.73
  b256          22.96       +3.31             +3.07
  b886          25.14       +5.49             +5.08
  no-fetch      22.59       +2.94             +2.75
  b886 @900p    24.90       +5.25             +4.83

  Two independent instruments -- a per-pass timestamp ring and the frame
  distribution -- agree to within ~0.4 ms across a 5 ms range.

WHY THIS IS MORE THAN A SANITY CHECK ON THIS PROJECT.
  The standing lesson here is that work REMOVED from a subsystem does not
  reliably become frame time: game-thread publication fell 2.94 -> 0.055 ms and
  p50 did not move; speculative GPU dispatches were cut 46% for 0.08 ms. That
  is why every saving must be measured on the FRAME.
  Here the converse holds and is measured: work ADDED to the GPU becomes frame
  time essentially 1:1. The march does not overlap with anything -- it is
  serial in the frame. So `march_ms` can be added to a frame budget directly,
  which is exactly what the cost model needs and exactly what could not be
  assumed.

  (Frame p50s here also carry the fluid solver's ~0.5-0.65 ms: b1's 19.65 ms
  against the solver-free A0's 18.99 ms. That is the workaround's cost and it
  cancels in every delta above.)

  NOTE ON RUNNING THE TEMPERATE POSE. The first attempt VOIDED at 23 s on the
  fine-tier gate: spawn -115200,-176640 needs fine tile (-8,-12), which the
  current provider (...-b19d281fd, 15 tiles) does not hold.
  THE BIOME VISTA CAPTURES AVOID THIS BY PASSING `-VoxelFineTileDir=` --
  present-but-empty, which beats the ini and disables the fine tier entirely.
  That is how all four 2026-08-19 captures ran, and it is why they reached
  200M quads without ever touching a fine tile. Any leg at a vista pose must
  pass the same empty switch.
  (Checked deliberately: the 2026-08-19 tile-cache cleanup did NOT break these
  poses. They never depended on the deleted namespaces.)
  Coarse-only also means no placement channels, so riparian species refuse --
  the asset set is slightly reduced from a fine-tier run. Same configuration as
  the captures, so the comparison holds.

  THE TEMPERATE POSE IS NOT CURRENTLY RUNNABLE AS A LEG, and the reason is
  worth recording because it looks like a config error and is not one.
  Spawn -115200,-176640 needs fine tile (-8,-12). The configured provider
  (...-b19d281fd) does not hold it; before the 2026-08-19 cleanup it existed
  only under a DIFFERENT terrain-diffusion base (71e2b362...), which the gate
  correctly refuses as an identity mismatch rather than silently loading.
  The 02:10 captures at that pose ran with the same ini and did NOT trip the
  gate, and I could not reconcile that -- provider id is unchanged in git, and
  their command line carries no fine-tier switch. Left unexplained rather than
  guessed at.
  Attempting `-VoxelFineTileDir=` from PowerShell does not work either: the
  empty value arrives QUOTED (`VoxelFineTileDir=""`), which is a path of two
  quote characters, not the "present-but-empty" switch the ini doc describes.

  SO THE ASSET QUESTION IS ISOLATED AT A COVERED SITE INSTEAD. Re-running
  Arm A at -61440,-61440 WITH -VoxelAssetDir answers the variable that actually
  matters -- assets were the 4x multiplier between this site and temperate --
  without needing a tile that is not baked. The biome question stays open and
  needs a dense-biome tile baked into the current provider.

================================================================
ASSET-ENABLED LEG -- the 35x regression reproduces, live
================================================================
Arm A re-run at the covered site WITH -VoxelAssetDir, to isolate the asset
variable (assets were the suspected 4x multiplier between this site and the
temperate capture).

  Observed during preflight: the 5 s perf line goes SILENT for 60+ seconds at a
  time while the game thread composes assets, and the pool sits at ~2,244 quads
  where the asset-free leg reached 46.8M. Log shows
  "2348 crown chunks CPU-meshed", "7 DISTINCT species requested",
  top species alpine-krummholz 95%.

  CORRECTION, taken a few minutes later: IT ACCELERATES. The pool reached
  32,840,408 quads by the end of preflight. The stall is an initial
  asset-composition warm-up, not a permanent wall -- so a 300 s leg CAN get a
  usable world, contrary to what the paragraph above assumed from the first
  60 seconds. docs/asset-streaming-perf-plan.md's "~17 chunks/s and never
  finishes" describes a different configuration (a fine-tier flight leg), not
  this static coarse one. Do not read the two as the same measurement.

TWO CONSEQUENCES
  1. The "with assets" prize CANNOT be measured by a settle-then-measure leg
     until the asset streaming regression is fixed. Both arms share the stall so
     an A0-vs-mask7 DELTA is still meaningful, but it is a delta on a nearly
     empty world and says little about the loaded case.
  2. ASSETS DO MATTER HERE -- and I called this wrong TWICE from mid-stream
     readings before it settled. Final, settled:

                      quads        p50        p95
       asset-free   46,798,900   18.99 ms   21.52 ms
       WITH assets  66,394,419   27.54 ms   55.76 ms
                       +42%        +45%      +159%

     Cost tracks quad count near-linearly once more (+42% geometry -> +45%
     frame). The p95 blows out far worse than the p50, which is the asset
     streaming hitching, not the draw.

     METHOD NOTE, because I made the same mistake twice in one hour: I read
     2,244 quads at t+60s and wrote "the world does not stream"; I read
     46,684,025 at t+~200s and wrote "assets add nothing". Both were mid-stream.
     ONLY QUOTE A SETTLED POOL. The `loaded=`/`quads=` pair must stop growing
     first -- the same discipline the occupancy=N/0 rule enforces for the spike.

     This is still an ALPINE site (alpine-krummholz 95%). Temperate's 200M is a
     denser world again, so +42% is a FLOOR on what assets cost, not a typical
     figure. The dense-biome question still needs a tile baked into the current
     provider.

  So the honest position stands: every frame number in this file is from a
  light, asset-free scene, and the loaded case is UNMEASURED rather than
  measured-and-similar.

================================================================
ASSET-LOADED ARM A -- THE PRIZE VANISHES, AND THE REASON MATTERS
================================================================
Same site, same pose, WITH -VoxelAssetDir, both arms settled to the same pool:

  arm                       p50       p95      quads
  asset-A0                27.54 ms  55.76 ms  66,394,419
  asset-mask7 (no draw)   26.79 ms  59.69 ms  66,372,475
                           -0.75 ms

  Against the asset-free pair: A0 18.99 -> mask7 3.55, a 15.44 ms prize.
  WITH ASSETS THE SAME SUPPRESSION IS WORTH 0.75 ms.

THIS IS NOT "the marcher does not help with assets". It is the classic shape
this project already documented: work removed from a subsystem does not become
frame time when something ELSE is the binding constraint. Here the asset legs
never stop streaming -- the pool is still growing at leg end, p95 blows out to
56-60 ms against 21 ms asset-free, and the frame is bound by asset
streaming/composition on the CPU rather than by the GPU draw. Under a CPU-bound
frame the GPU draw hides completely, so removing it shows nothing.

WHAT IT DOES ESTABLISH
  * The terrain draw dominates ONLY ONCE THE WORLD HAS SETTLED. While streaming
    -- which is most of the time when a player is moving -- the binding
    constraint is elsewhere. Both facts are real and they apply to different
    moments of play.
  * The asset-loaded draw-path prize is therefore STILL UNMEASURED. It needs a
    settled asset world, and asset streaming does not settle inside a 330 s leg
    at this site.
  * It is independent corroboration that the asset streaming regression
    (docs/asset-streaming-perf-plan.md) is the more urgent problem of the two:
    it is what is actually costing frames right now, at 27.5 ms p50 / 56 ms p95
    against 19.0 / 21.5 without assets.

DO NOT quote 0.75 ms as evidence against the marcher, and do not quote 15.44 ms
as the loaded-case prize. Neither is a measurement of the other's condition.

  BUILD NOTE FOR P0.5: the census + mip-skip C++ arrived unbuilt (the agent
  correctly refused to run a 6-core build against a live leg) and did not
  compile: `RecentreField` undeclared at VoxelFluidSubsystem.cpp:2672 -- the
  edit that added the `pending(...,volume=...)` branch had deleted the
  RecentreField block that the same UE_LOG still referenced. Restored verbatim
  from HEAD; builds clean. Worth noting because it is the failure mode of
  handing over shader-verified-but-uncompiled C++: every shader permutation was
  proven by disassembly while the C++ around it had never seen a compiler.

================================================================
P0.5 -- STEP CENSUS, and the ray population that costs the money
================================================================
`voxel.Marcher.SpikeCount 1` builds an exact per-step histogram in a second
compute pass (so it must NOT be read as a timing run -- the GPU marches twice).
Converged volume (occupancy=512/0), budget 886, camera ~60 m above terrain.

  skip=0 (flat control)
    rays 1,354,896   counted 1,354,896 (ok)     <- ran-flag: outcomes sum to pixels
    hit        559,035  41.3%   mean 221.2 steps
    miss       795,861  58.7%   mean 442.4 steps
    exhausted        0   0.0%
    overall mean 351.1 steps, p95 605

  1,354,896 rays is exactly 1552x873 -- the internal resolution. The dispatch is
  what it claims to be.

THE SHAPE THIS REVEALS
  The MISSES dominate: 59% of rays, at TWICE the step count of the hits. Those
  are rays that cross the whole 51.2 m box without ever meeting a surface --
  sky above the horizon and air between the eye and the ground, at a pose 60 m
  up. They pay full price per step and return nothing.

  That is precisely the population a mip pyramid deletes, and it is why the
  step-budget curve was so sublinear: hits terminate at ~221 steps, misses run
  to ~442, so raising the budget past ~256 only feeds the misses.

  ZERO rays exhausted the 886 budget, so the box diagonal is fully covered and
  none of the above is a budget artifact.

  (Reading the earlier partial-fill census is instructive as a counter-example:
  at occupancy=119/393 it reported hit=100.0%, mean 213.9 -- every ray stopping
  at the fill frontier, which looks like a CHEAPER and TIDIER world than the
  real one. Anything read before occupancy=N/0 is not merely noisy, it is
  systematically wrong in the flattering direction.)

================================================================
THE 4 km EXTRAPOLATION -- why the skip ratio is the whole project
================================================================
Steps a HORIZONTAL ray needs to reach 4 km, with the ring cascade's coarsening
already included (each ring doubles voxel size, so the far field is cheap per
metre before any skipping):

  ring        span_m   voxel_m   steps
     0-128      128      0.10     1280
   128-256      128      0.20      640
   256-512      256      0.40      640
   512-1024     512      0.80      640
  1024-2048    1024      1.60      640
  2048-4096    2048      3.20      640
                        TOTAL     4480

Fed through the measured model (march_ms = 0.13 + steps x 5.9 us) and added to
the measured 3.55 ms floor:

  skip ratio    steps    march      frame        fps    vs today (18.99 ms)
      1x (none)  4480    26.56 ms   30.11 ms      33    WORSE THAN TODAY
      3x         1493     8.94 ms   12.49 ms      80    1.5x
      5x          896     5.42 ms    8.97 ms     112    2.1x
     10x          448     2.77 ms    6.32 ms     158    3.0x
     20x          224     1.45 ms    5.00 ms     200    3.8x

  WITHOUT EMPTY-SPACE SKIPPING THE MARCHER IS SLOWER THAN THE RENDERER IT
  REPLACES. Every optimistic number in this programme is really a bet on the
  skip ratio, and until P0.5 lands nobody has measured it.

CAVEATS, both directions
  - 5.9 us/step was measured on a DENSE uniform 10 cm volume. Coarse rings have
    a much smaller working set per unit distance, so us/step at range is
    probably LOWER -- the table is pessimistic there.
  - A horizontal ray to 4 km is the worst case. The flat census says only 58.7%
    of rays miss, and hits terminate at ~221 steps, so the MEAN ray is far
    cheaper than this table's ray.
  - Against that: the table assumes a ray that never re-enters fine rings, and
    ignores the cost of crossing ring boundaries.
  - The census independently measures 91-96% of the flat index as skippable,
    which is where a 10-20x ratio would have to come from.

P0.5 skip=1 -- A 6.6x REDUCTION, AND THE VERIFIER REFUSES TO CERTIFY IT
  skip=1(vs-flat DISAGREES -- RATIO INVALID: 52 mismatch / 1354896 compared)
    hit  559,035 41.3%  mean  48.6   (flat: 221.2)
    miss 795,861 58.7%  mean  56.5   (flat: 442.4)
    overall mean 53.3, p95 83        (flat: 351.1, p95 605)

  ratio 351.1 / 53.3 = 6.6x overall; 7.8x on the misses, 4.6x on the hits.
  The hit/miss SPLIT is identical to the flat control to the ray (559,035 /
  795,861 both arms), so the skip walk is finding the same surfaces -- it is
  only the step counts that differ, which is what a correct skip should do.

  BUT 52 RAYS OUT OF 1,354,896 (0.0038%) DISAGREE WITH THE FLAT WALK, and the
  instrument prints RATIO INVALID rather than a number. That guard exists for a
  specific reason the G1 agent stated up front: the skip walk's characteristic
  failure -- skipping one cell too many -- LOWERS the step count and still looks
  like terrain. It reads as a better result, not as a bug. So a disagreement of
  any size invalidates the ratio in the direction that flatters it.

  => 6.6x IS PROVISIONAL AND MUST NOT BE QUOTED AS MEASURED. If it survives the
  fix, the extrapolation gives:
       4480 / 6.6 = 679 steps -> march 4.14 ms -> frame 7.69 ms -> ~130 fps
       i.e. ~2.5x today's 53 fps.
  0.0038% of rays cannot move a mean by much, so the corrected figure is
  unlikely to move far -- but "unlikely to move far" is not the standard this
  project uses, and the whole point of the verifier is that the error is
  systematically in the flattering direction.

P0.5 COMPLETE -- SKIP RATIO, AND A DESIGN FINDING THAT WAS NOT THE POINT
  All arms converged (occupancy=512/0), budget 886, same pose, 1,354,896 rays,
  counted=ok in every arm, hit/miss split byte-identical across all three.

    arm       overall   hitMean   missMean    p95    ratio vs flat
    flat        351.1     221.2      442.4    605      --
    skip=1       53.3      48.6       56.5     83     6.59x
    skip=2       48.8      68.9       34.6    118     7.19x

  BOTH ARMS: "DISAGREES -- RATIO INVALID: 52 mismatch / 1354896 compared".

THE 52 ARE THE SAME 52 IN BOTH ARMS, and that is the diagnostic. skip=1 and
skip=2 traverse different level counts, take different step counts and produce
different distributions, yet disagree on EXACTLY the same number of rays. That
is not a per-level bug; it is something shared by every skip path and absent
from the flat one -- box entry/exit clip, initial coarse-cell seeding, or the
hand-off back to VoxelFluidWalkVoxelLine at a span boundary. 52 of 1.35M is
about the order of the rays that graze the volume boundary.

DESIGN FINDING (not what the experiment was for, and worth more than the ratio)
  TWO MIP LEVELS IS NOT STRAIGHTFORWARDLY BETTER THAN ONE.
    skip=2 wins on MISSES   56.5 -> 34.6
    skip=2 LOSES on HITS    48.6 -> 68.9
    and its p95 is worse    83   -> 118
  A ray that ends on a surface is by definition near occupied space, so
  descending two coarse levels costs steps that advance it barely at all, while
  a ray crossing open air gets the full coarse stride. => the real marcher wants
  the coarse level used ADAPTIVELY (coarse while far from occupancy, fine once
  close), not a fixed hierarchy depth. A fixed depth-2 hierarchy would make the
  common case -- looking at the ground -- worse.

WHAT IT IS WORTH (provisional on the 52)
  4,480 steps to 4 km / ratio, through march_ms = 0.13 + steps x 5.9 us,
  plus the measured 3.55 ms floor:
    6.59x -> 680 steps -> 4.14 ms march -> 7.70 ms frame -> 130 fps
    7.19x -> 623 steps -> 3.81 ms march -> 7.35 ms frame -> 136 fps
    today                                  18.99 ms frame ->  53 fps
  => ~2.5x, and NOT CERTIFIED until the 52 are explained.

  THE STEP CENSUS HAS ZERO MEASUREMENT NOISE. Every census line within a leg
  reports the identical mean and p95 -- 351.1/605 in the flat arm, 53.3/83 in
  skip=1 -- repeated exactly, frame after frame. That is what a pinned static
  pose over a converged volume should give: the geometry is identical every
  frame and the histogram is exact rather than sampled.

  CONSEQUENCE: replicates are unnecessary for the ratio, and the ONLY
  uncertainty in it is the correctness of the skip walk. There is no noise to
  hide behind and no spread to appeal to -- which is precisely why 52 rays out
  of 1.35M are worth chasing rather than absorbing. A 0.004% disagreement in a
  measurement with 0.000% noise is a bug, not variance.

  FRAME TIMES FROM THE P0.5 LEGS ARE NOT USABLE AS TIMINGS. voxel.Marcher.
  SpikeCount 1 adds a second compute pass that marches every ray again, so the
  GPU does the work twice. The legs reported p50 30.35 / 26.92 / 26.44 ms for
  flat / skip=1 / skip=2. The DIRECTION corroborates the census (skipping
  helps), the MAGNITUDE is meaningless. Timing arms and counting arms must be
  separate runs -- which is why the cvar is separate.

================================================================
A COORDINATION HAZARD WORTH WRITING DOWN: .usf FILES ARE LIVE INPUT
================================================================
Two legs voided at boot with:

  VoxelMarchSpike.usf(826,15): error: conditional operator only supports
  results with numeric scalar, vector, or matrix types.
      return bDone ? H : Miss;      (H and Miss are structs; HLSL ?: cannot)

Cause: a subagent was mid-edit on the shader. Nothing was wrong with the leg.

THE ASYMMETRY THAT MAKES THIS BITE. A half-finished .CPP edit is inert until
someone builds. A half-finished .USF edit is NOT: the editor compiles global
shaders at boot, so a broken shader in the working tree kills the next leg that
starts, with a fatal error that looks nothing like "somebody is editing".
Whoever runs legs and whoever edits shaders are therefore coupled even when
they touch no common file otherwise.

ALSO NOTE the error fired only on PERMUTATION 2. A single-permutation offline
dxc check passes it. Verifying a shader by disassembling one permutation is not
verifying the shader.

RULE ADOPTED: treat a boot-time global-shader compile error as "the tree is
mid-edit", not "the leg failed" -- do not chase it as a regression, and do not
count the leg as evidence of anything.

================================================================
A CAVEAT ON EVERY SKIP NUMBER SO FAR -- THE POSE
================================================================
Every ratio measured today comes from a pose 60 m ABOVE terrain, where the
census says 58.7% of rays MISS. Skipping helps the misses most (7.8x) and the
hits least (4.6x), so a pose with more sky flatters the ratio.

A player's camera is usually near eye height, where far more rays hit ground
quickly and the miss population -- the one skipping rescues -- is much smaller.
THE RATIO MAY BE MATERIALLY WORSE AT EYE HEIGHT, AND THAT IS THE POSE THE GAME
ACTUALLY RENDERS. -VoxelPerfDepth=2 against the same arms is queued; until it
runs, 6.6-7.2x is an OVERHEAD-POSE figure and should be quoted as such.

  SHADER FIXED and the box is productive again -- the struct ternary is gone and
  legs boot clean (0 fatal, census reached). The eye-height pose arms
  (-VoxelPerfDepth=2) are running against the same skip=0/skip=1 pair.

  -VoxelPerfDepth DOES NOT MOVE THE STATIC POSE. An eye-height attempt with
  -VoxelPerfDepth=2 logged "depth=2m" in the config line and then
  "path centered at (-6144000,-6144000), height 221758" -- IDENTICAL to the
  60 m run -- and produced a byte-identical census (hit 559,035 / 41.3%,
  mean 351.1, p95 605). The scene never changed. Depth applies to the flight
  path, not to a pinned static pose.
  THE KNOB IS -VoxelSpawnAltM (metres above ground), which is what the biome
  captures use (-VoxelSpawnAltM=30). Re-run the pose experiment with that.
  Useful accident: because the pose did not change, this leg is an exact
  reproduction of the earlier flat control on a NEWER BINARY -- and it
  reproduces to the ray, which independently confirms the census is
  deterministic across builds.

================================================================
THE 52-RAY FIX -- one class eliminated, a second class exposed
================================================================
The G1 agent diagnosed v1's skip walk as advancing by a scalar t-epsilon and
re-flooring the cell index: near a cell EDGE or CORNER that crosses two or three
planes at once and lands in the DIAGONAL neighbour, omitting the intermediate
cell the flat walk visits. Fixed with a true Amanatides & Woo coarse traversal
plus a half-voxel backoff so the fine walk is never seeded on a boundary.
The backoff costs 1-2 extra fine steps per occupied cell -- CONSERVATIVE, so it
can only make the ratio worse than truth.

RESULT, converged (45 identical census lines; the varying ones are all from the
fill period, where the frontier is jagged and mismatch runs 105-142):

  skip=1, post-fix:  mean 54.8  p95 86   mismatch 53 / 1,354,896
  skip=1, pre-fix:   mean 53.3  p95 83   mismatch 52 / 1,354,896
  flat control:      mean 351.1 p95 605

  ratio 351.1 / 54.8 = 6.41x   (pre-fix 6.59x -- the 2.7% drop IS the backoff,
                                 i.e. the number got more honest, not worse)

THE COUNT BARELY MOVED (52 -> 53) BUT THE CLASS CHANGED, and the samples prove
it. The instrument now dumps coordinates:

  flat=hit(-614327,-614336,21950)  skip=hit(-614326,-614336,21950)   x +/-1
  flat=hit(-614271,-614384,21961)  skip=hit(-614271,-614385,21961)   y +/-1
  flat=hit(-614271,-614377,21958)  skip=hit(-614271,-614378,21958)   y +/-1

  EVERY residual disagreement is an ADJACENT voxel differing on exactly ONE
  axis. That is the corner/edge tie class the agent predicted, and it is NOT the
  diagonal-neighbour class that was fixed -- a diagonal miss would differ on two
  or three axes and skip an intermediate cell. So the fix did what it claimed;
  what remains is a different, smaller-consequence question: when a ray passes
  exactly along the plane between two adjacent surface voxels, which one is
  "the" hit? Both walks find the same SURFACE at the same distance.

  => 6.41x is the working figure. Whether the verifier should tolerate the
  one-axis-adjacent class explicitly (rather than printing RATIO INVALID) is a
  judgement for the agent to make in code, with the tie rule stated -- not
  something to wave through by loosening a threshold.

  THE SHADER PRECHECK IS NOW A GATE, and it works. tools/voxel-check-march-shader.ps1
  compiles all ELEVEN permutations in ~2 s with no editor, and the leg chain
  refuses to start if any fail:
      ok pixel skip=0/1/2 x nofetch=0/1 | ok census skip=0/1/2 | ok mip L1/L2 reduce
  It was verified to FAIL, not merely to pass: the agent rebuilt the struct-ternary
  defect in a throwaway tree and it reported 3 of 11 failing, naming skip=1 and
  census skip=1 while skip=0 and skip=2 passed -- the one-permutation blind spot
  made visible. A precheck that has only ever been seen to pass is not a gate.

================================================================
THE POSE KNOB -- three switches tried, two were silent no-ops
================================================================
The skip ratio is measured at a pose where 58.7% of rays MISS, and skipping
helps misses 7.8x against 4.6x for hits, so the pose flatters the headline
number. Measuring eye height took three attempts:

  -VoxelPerfDepth=2   logs "depth=2m", leaves the pose at height 221758.
                      Flight-path depth; no effect on a PINNED STATIC pose.
  -VoxelSpawnAltM=2   spawn altitude -- overwritten on the first path-init tick
                      (CircleCenterUU = Pawn location, then FixedHeightUU =
                      surface + HeightAboveSurfaceUU). Same 221758.
  BOTH produced a BYTE-IDENTICAL census (hit 559,035 / 41.3%, mean 351.1,
  p95 605). A null result that reads exactly like a completed experiment.

  THE ACTUAL CAUSE: VoxelPerfRunSubsystem.h:114
      static constexpr double HeightAboveSurfaceUU = 3000.0;  // surface + 30m
  A COMPILE-TIME CONSTANT with no override. The static pose is always surface
  + 30 m. (The "depth=60m" in the run header is a different parameter and is
  what misled me into calling this a 60 m pose earlier -- it is 30 m.)

  FIXED: HeightAboveSurfaceUU is now a member and -VoxelPerfHeightM=<metres>
  sets it, mirroring the guarded-scalar shape of -VoxelPerfDepth/-VoxelPerfSpeed.
  The header records which two switches do NOT move it, because both silently
  produce the default pose and an identical census.

================================================================
THE POSE RISK WAS UNFOUNDED -- the camera was already at eye height
================================================================
-VoxelPerfHeightM=2 DID move the path centre (221758 -> 218958, exactly 2800 UU
= 28 m) and the census was STILL byte-identical. The reason is in the log:

    path centered at (-6144000,-6144000), height 218958      <- moved
    preflight warmup ... at spawn pose (-6144000,-6144000, 219258)   <- did NOT

  A STATIC flight never leaves the spawn pose. FixedHeightUU / HeightAboveSurfaceUU
  describe the CIRCLE/LINE path centre, which a pinned pose does not use. So all
  three switches were no-ops for this measurement, for three different reasons.

  ARITHMETIC THAT SETTLES IT:
    path centre at +30 m = 221758  =>  surface = 218758
    path centre at + 2 m = 218958  =>  surface = 218758   (consistent)
    spawn pose z         = 219258  =>  camera  = surface + 500 UU = +5 m

  THE CAMERA HAS BEEN 5 m ABOVE GROUND IN EVERY LEG -- i.e. already a realistic
  player-height pose, not the elevated one I assumed. The 58.7% miss rate is
  simply a horizontal view with sky filling half the frame, which is what a game
  camera sees.

  => THE "elevated pose flatters the skip ratio" RISK IS RETIRED. 6.41x was
  measured at a realistic pose all along. I raised this risk twice and pushed an
  agent to treat it as the headline; it was my error, from reading "depth=60m"
  in the run header (a different parameter) as the camera height.

  -VoxelPerfHeightM= is still worth keeping: it is correct, it makes the path
  centre controllable for LINE/CIRCLE flights, and its header now records which
  switches do not move a static pose. But it does not change a static leg, and
  no static measurement in this file needs re-running because of pose.

  TWO INSTRUMENT NOTES FROM THE TIE CHANGE, both generally useful:

  (a) THE CENSUS LINE IDENTIFIES ITS OWN BINARY. A `ties=` field present means
      the tie-exemption build; absent means the pre-tie build and the mismatch
      count is stale rather than a failure. No timestamp forensics needed --
      the log says which code wrote it.

  (b) SHADERS ARE LIVE-FROM-SOURCE, C++ IS NOT, so there is a window after a
      .usf edit and before the DLL relinks (here 11:33:40 -> 11:37:04) in which
      a leg pairs the NEW shader with the OLD C++. When the two disagree about a
      readback layout the damage is bounded but real: histogram-derived counts
      stay valid, per-ray SAMPLE COORDINATES do not. Counts from such a leg are
      usable; sample records are not.

  (c) VERIFY A BINARY BY CONTENT, NOT TIMESTAMP. The agent confirmed the tie
      change by searching the DLL for the UTF-16 literal `ties / %llu compared`
      (present) and the superseded `mismatch / %llu compared` (absent). Its own
      build log had been truncated to the last 25 lines and showed no Compile
      lines at all -- trusting that would have produced the opposite and wrong
      conclusion.

================================================================
THE TIE EXEMPTION LANDED, AND IT IMMEDIATELY SPLIT THE POPULATION
================================================================
Converged, stable across every census line:

  skip=1  DISAGREES -- RATIO INVALID: 20 mismatch / 33 ties / 1,354,896
          hit 559,035 (41.3%)  miss 795,861 (58.7%)
          mean 54.8  p95 86    hitMean 52.4  missMean 56.5

  Of the original 53: 33 were SHARED-FACE TIES (one-axis adjacent, <=0.5 UU
  apart along the ray, both voxels solid) -- excused, counted in their own
  column. 20 were NOT.

THE RESIDUAL 20 ARE DIAGONAL, i.e. still a real defect:

  flat=hit(-614178,-614216,21929)  skip=hit(-614179,-614215,21929)  dx=-1 dy=+1
  flat=hit(-614163,-614252,21917)  skip=hit(-614164,-614251,21917)  dx=-1 dy=+1

  TWO axes differ, z unchanged, and both samples share the same dx=-1/dy=+1
  signature -- a narrow consistent class, not scatter. That is the SAME
  signature as the original defect (a step crossing two planes at once and
  landing in the diagonal neighbour, omitting the intermediate cell), so the
  first fix reduced that class rather than eliminating it.

  The exemption refuses them correctly: it requires L1(delta voxel) == 1 and
  these are L1 == 2. The instrument is working; it is the walk that is not
  finished.

SCALE, BOTH DIRECTIONS
  20 of 1,354,896 is 0.0015% and cannot move a mean over 1.35M rays -- the
  ratio is 6.41x with or without them, so this does NOT block the number.
  But the census has zero measurement noise, and the error direction
  under-counts steps (flattering), so it is a bug rather than variance.
  DO NOT widen the tie bound to absorb them: L1==2 failing the test is the
  discrimination working, and widening it is how this instrument would stop
  being worth having.


================================================================
PHASE 0 -- WHERE IT LANDED
================================================================
MEASURED, and I would quote these:

  the settled frame               18.99 ms   53 fps
    terrain raster + submission   14.91 ms   78.5%
    render-thread walk + emit      0.53 ms    2.8%
    everything else (the floor)    3.55 ms   18.7%
  resolution is free for BOTH the raster and the march
  brick volume vs quads, one walk  404 MiB vs 702 MiB   1.74x
  march cost model                 0.13 + steps x 5.9 us  (per FRAME, not per pixel)
  the march is SERIAL              frame delta tracks march delta to 0.4 ms
  4 km ray, no skipping            4,480 steps -> 30 ms frame -> WORSE than today
  skip ratio, realistic pose       6.41x -> ~699 steps -> 7.8 ms -> ~128 fps -> 2.4x

NOT MEASURED, and must not be quoted as if they were:
  * the ASSET-LOADED prize. With assets the frame is streaming-bound, not
    draw-bound: suppressing the whole terrain draw is worth 0.75 ms against
    15.44 ms asset-free. Blocked on the asset streaming regression, which is a
    separate and more urgent project.
  * a DENSE-BIOME pose. Needs a tile baked into the current provider.
  * anything at 4K internal. The resolution claim is proved over 0.54M -> 1.36M
    internal pixels only.

OPEN DEFECT, not blocking: 20 rays of 1,354,896 still land on a diagonal
neighbour. Cannot move the ratio; is a bug rather than variance; being fixed.

THE CLAIMS OF MINE THAT MEASUREMENT OVERTURNED, kept because the corrections are
the most useful part of this file:
  1. "VRAM refund 3-14x"            -> 1.74x on identical ground.
  2. "the marcher gives up free resolution" -> it does not, 2.53x px for 5%.
  3. "R0 is ~80% of resident quads" -> 18.4%; the distribution is near-uniform.
  4. "a heightfield over L3-L5 also fixes the saturation" -> no; at the
     saturating pose L3-L5 is 1.2% and L0+L1 is 175M of 200M.
  5. "the 7.70 ms bar"              -> site-specific; it is a range, ~7.7 to ~18.
  6. "assets add nothing here"      -> +42% quads, +45% frame (read mid-stream).
  7. "the elevated pose flatters the skip ratio" -> the camera was at surface
     + 5 m the whole time; the risk never existed.
Seven, which is the same count this project falsified in a single day in July.
The rule that catches them is the same one: measure the thing, do not derive it.

================================================================
FINAL SKIP RATIOS, post-fix binary -- and skip=2 IS the configuration
================================================================
All arms converged, tie-exemption build (census carries a `ties=` field),
camera = surface + 5 m, 1,354,896 rays, counted=ok:

  arm      mean   hitMean  missMean  p95   mismatch/ties   ratio vs flat
  flat    351.1     221.2     442.4  605   (control)         --
  skip=1   54.8      52.4      56.5   86   20 / 33          6.41x
  skip=2   38.2      57.0      25.0   94   22 / 38          9.19x

  skip=2 was 48.8 on the PRE-fix binary; the true-A&W coarse traversal improved
  it to 38.2. So the correctness fix made the FAST arm faster, which is the
  opposite of the usual direction and worth noting: the old walk was not merely
  wrong at 52 rays, it was also taking unnecessary steps everywhere.

  THE EARLIER "two levels is not better than one" FINDING IS SUPERSEDED for the
  MEAN, and still true for the TAIL:
    skip=2 crushes MISSES  56.5 -> 25.0  (misses are 58.7% of rays, so it wins)
    skip=2 costs HITS      52.4 -> 57.0
    skip=2 has a WORSE p95    86 -> 94
  The adaptive-depth conclusion stands and is now better motivated: use the
  coarse level while far from occupancy, drop to fine when close, and both the
  mean AND the tail improve rather than trading against each other.

EXTRAPOLATION TO 4 km (4,480 steps unskipped; model 0.13 + steps x 5.9 us;
floor 3.55 ms):

  skip=1  6.41x ->  699 steps -> march 4.26 ms -> frame 7.81 ms -> 128 fps -> 2.43x
  skip=2  9.19x ->  487 steps -> march 3.01 ms -> frame 6.56 ms -> 153 fps -> 2.90x

  => THE HEADLINE IS 2.9x (about 153 fps against today's 53), using two mip
  levels. Caveats unchanged and still binding: the spike volume is single-level
  and uniformly 10 cm, so the ratio is a LOWER bound; the backoff over-counts
  steps by construction; 20-22 diagonal mismatches remain (0.0015%, cannot move
  a mean, still a bug).

  Frame p50s from these legs (30.37 / 27.59 / 29.23 ms) are COUNTING runs and
  are not timings -- the GPU marches twice. Note skip=2's frame is WORSE than
  skip=1's there despite fewer steps, which is the double-march plus the second
  mip level; another reason counting arms and timing arms must stay separate.

================================================================
THE RESIDUAL 20 ARE NOT A DEFECT -- they are the reference's own
float32 noise floor, and it was MEASURED rather than assumed
================================================================
An offline float32 mirror of both walks (tools/walkmodel/walks.c -- no GPU
needed) reproduced them at 1 in ~670k random rays, the same order as the
in-engine 1.5e-5. One traced case:

  [flat] axis=0 v=(50,49,32) t=0.236133948
  [flat] axis=2 v=(50,49,31) t=0.236133978    <- 3e-8 apart = ~2 float ULP

The ray crosses an x-plane and a z-plane SIMULTANEOUSLY to within two ULP. The
flat walk arrives with tMax accumulated over ~30 `+= tDelta` steps; the sub-walk
computes tMax fresh from its own start. Different arithmetic histories order the
two planes differently. Both resulting voxels are solid. NO CELL IS SKIPPED --
this is not the diagonal class that was fixed.

THE CONTROL THAT SETTLES IT (8,000,000 rays, same field):

  flat vs FLAT, entry nudged 1e-6 UU (same ray, same algorithm)   157   2.0e-5
  flat vs SKIP                                                     12   1.5e-6

  THE REFERENCE DISAGREES WITH A NEGLIGIBLY PERTURBED COPY OF ITSELF 13x MORE
  OFTEN THAN IT DISAGREES WITH THE SKIP WALK. Any two DDA instances with
  different arithmetic histories diverge at that rate, and a restart-based skip
  walk necessarily has a different history. No amount of care in the skip logic
  can go below the floor the comparison itself sits on. Our 20/1,354,896 =
  1.5e-5 is AT that floor.

  (The first control used a 0.5 UU nudge, which changes real coverage and was
  therefore unfair; driven down to 1e-6 UU, coverage change is ~3e-7 and
  essentially all of the residual is arithmetic.)

THE VERIFIER NOW MEASURES THE FLOOR INSTEAD OF ASSUMING ZERO. Each frame it
runs the flat walk a SECOND time over a segment scaled by 1-1e-7 -- the same ray
geometrically, one part in ten million of arithmetic difference, no new DDA --
and counts how often the reference disagrees with itself on the same rays in the
same frame:

  skip=1(vs-flat AT REFERENCE NOISE FLOOR: 20 mismatch / 27 refNoise / 33 ties
         / 1354896 compared)

  AGREES (0) -> AT REFERENCE NOISE FLOOR (<= refNoise) -> DISAGREES (above it).

  THIS IS CALIBRATION AGAINST A CONTROL DRAWN FROM THE SAME POPULATION, NOT A
  LOOSENED THRESHOLD. The floor is re-measured every frame, nothing about it can
  be tuned to hide a defect, and a real bug runs well clear of it -- the earlier
  diagonal defect ran 52 against a floor of this order, which is exactly why it
  fired. The tie bound was NOT widened; these cases still fail it correctly
  (hits ~1.6 voxels apart, nowhere near 0.5 UU).

  Conservative by construction: the scaled segment is ~1e-3 UU shorter at the
  far end, so a reference hit inside that sliver flips to a miss, INFLATING the
  floor slightly and making the verifier stricter about what counts as noise.

=> IF THE RE-RUN READS mismatch <= refNoise, 6.41x / 9.19x STAND WITH NO
   RESIDUAL DEFECT.

  RE-RUN RESULT: THE FLOOR HYPOTHESIS DOES NOT HOLD AT CONVERGENCE.
  Converged (42 of 47 census lines identical):

    skip=1(vs-flat DISAGREES -- RATIO INVALID: 20 mismatch / 7 refNoise
           / 33 ties / 1354896 compared)

  Mismatch 20 is ~3x the in-engine measured floor of 7. The verdict is
  DISAGREES, not AT REFERENCE NOISE FLOOR.

  I QUOTED THE OPPOSITE FROM A MID-FILL LINE -- "AT REFERENCE NOISE FLOOR:
  7 mismatch / 11 refNoise" -- which is from the fill period, where the jagged
  frontier INFLATES refNoise and SUPPRESSES mismatch, flipping the comparison.
  Third time today I have drawn a conclusion from an unsettled reading (after
  the asset quad count twice). The rule is already written down in this file
  and I broke it anyway: NOTHING IS READ BEFORE occupancy=N/0.

  THE OFFLINE MODEL AND THE IN-ENGINE CONTROL DISAGREE, and that is the open
  question, not a detail:
    offline (8M rays):  flat-vs-self 157   flat-vs-skip 12    self-noise HIGHER
    in-engine (converged): refNoise    7   mismatch     20    self-noise LOWER
  Opposite ordering. Either the in-engine control (segment scaled 1-1e-7) is a
  WEAKER perturbation than the offline one (entry nudged 1e-6 UU) and therefore
  understates the floor, or there is a genuine residual defect of ~13 rays above
  it. Both are testable; neither is established.

  IMPACT ON THE RATIO: none. 20 rays of 1,354,896 cannot move a mean over 1.35M
  samples, and the step counts are unchanged (mean 54.8, p95 86, identical to
  the pre-calibration run). What is NOT established is CERTIFICATION: the
  instrument declines to certify, and I should not overrule it.
  => 6.41x / 9.19x remain the working figures and remain UNCERTIFIED.

  BOTH ARMS AGREE ON THE FLOOR AND BOTH EXCEED IT, converged:

    skip=1   20 mismatch / 7 refNoise / 33 ties
    skip=2   22 mismatch / 7 refNoise / 38 ties

  refNoise is 7 in BOTH arms. That is a good sign for the control itself -- it
  is a flat-vs-flat comparison and should not depend on the skip level, and it
  does not. So the in-engine floor is being measured consistently; the question
  is whether it is measuring the RIGHT perturbation, not whether it is stable.
  ~13-15 rays sit above it in both arms, consistently.

  Frame p50s from this sweep (33.01 / 36.53 ms) are counting runs and are worse
  than the earlier counting runs (30.4 / 29.2) because the census now performs
  THREE walks per ray -- flat, skip, and the refNoise flat. Still not timings.

================================================================
THE OFFLINE 13x IS WITHDRAWN -- it was measured on the wrong world
================================================================
The G1 agent's offline model built a RANDOM-NOISE field (30% solid voxels
scattered through 35% of cells). That is not terrain. Re-run on terrain-like
geometry (solid below a smooth surface) the same probes move enormously:

  probe                    random noise    terrain-like
  flat vs skip                      12              10
  segment scale (shipped)         3227             134
  restart-only                    6954             230

  So the "reference disagrees with itself 13x more often" claim was a synthetic
  rate quoted as if it bounded a real one -- the exact flat-fallback trap this
  file already records for the 2026-07-27 perf numbers, committed again by a
  different route. THE ENGINE MEASUREMENT IS AUTHORITATIVE; the 13x is withdrawn
  as a RATE. What survives is the MECHANISM -- the traced two-ULP simultaneous
  plane crossing -- which is geometry-independent.

MY HYPOTHESIS WAS ALSO WRONG. I guessed the in-engine segment-scale control was
a WEAKER perturbation than an entry nudge. Offline it is the STRONGER one
(3227 vs 157). The real problem is that it measures the wrong quantity: it
bounds how sensitive the REFERENCE is to its own rounding, not how much a
RESTART-BASED walk must diverge from it. And it inverts against the engine in
both synthetic worlds, so the model cannot settle the question either.

THE REPLACEMENT CONTROL -- restartNoise -- is a direct one
  The SAME skip walk, same ray, with the mip FORCED SOLID. Identical code path,
  identical restart structure, and provably zero skipping because no cell is
  ever reported empty. Its disagreements with flat are restart-induced BY
  CONSTRUCTION rather than by argument.

  It restarts at EVERY cell where the real skip walk restarts only at OCCUPIED
  ones, so it sees more divergence opportunities: it is an UPPER BOUND. That
  makes the verdict deliberately asymmetric, and the verdict string says so:

    mismatch >  restartNoise  -> HARD RESULT, a real defect (not explainable
                                 by restarts)
    mismatch <= restartNoise  -> "WITHIN RESTART NOISE (not proof)" -- consistent
                                 with noise, explicitly NOT certification

  A permissive control cannot manufacture a pass it has not earned; the worst it
  can do is decline to convict. That asymmetry is the honest shape when the
  control is an upper bound, and it is why this one is worth more than the
  refNoise it replaces (refNoise still prints alongside -- it measures a real
  but different thing, and keeping both is how the discrepancy stayed visible).

RESTART-NOISE VERDICT, converged
  skip=1(vs-flat WITHIN RESTART NOISE (not proof):
         20 mismatch / 53 restartNoise / 7 refNoise / 33 ties / 1354896)

  20 <= 53, so the disagreement is fully accounted for by an UPPER BOUND on
  restart-induced divergence. Note restartNoise (53) is 7.6x refNoise (7),
  which is why the segment-scale control was the wrong instrument: restarting
  the walk perturbs it far more than perturbing the reference's own arithmetic.

THE 20 SAMPLES CONFIRM THE TRACED MECHANISM
  1/20 px(1496,192) flat(-614171,-614199,21942) skip(-614170,-614198,21943) dx+1 dy+1 dz+1
  2/20 px(1497,192) ... identical voxels
  3/20 px(1498,192) ... identical voxels
  4/20 px(1427,237) flat(-614173,-614218,21930) skip(-614174,-614217,21930) dx-1 dy+1
  5/20 px(1448,242) flat(-614178,-614216,21929) skip(-614179,-614215,21929) dx-1 dy+1
  6/20 px(1276,282) flat(-614163,-614252,21917) skip(-614164,-614251,21917) dx-1 dy+1

  Samples 1-3 differ on THREE axes -- a ray through a cell CORNER where three
  planes coincide -- and are three CONSECUTIVE PIXELS reporting identical
  voxels, i.e. a small cluster riding one geometric edge. The rest differ on two
  axes. That is the near-simultaneous multi-plane crossing signature, clustered
  on edges rather than scattered, and it is NOT a skipped-cell signature (no
  intermediate cell is omitted; both walks land on the same surface).

WHERE THIS LEAVES CERTIFICATION -- and the honest answer is "consistent, not
proven":
  * the disagreement is within a construction-based upper bound on restart noise
  * the samples match the traced float mechanism, on the geometry it predicts
  * 20 rays of 1,354,896 cannot move a mean; step counts are bit-identical
  * the verdict string itself says "(not proof)", and that wording is correct
  => 6.41x / 9.19x stand as WORKING FIGURES, consistent with float noise, with
     certification recorded as open rather than claimed.

================================================================
ADAPTIVE DEPTH -- MY CLAIM WAS WRONG, AND THE MODEL WAS VALIDATED
BEFORE BEING BELIEVED
================================================================
I suggested adaptive hierarchy depth "should win both mean AND tail". That was
an assertion, not a measurement. The G1 agent checked its offline model against
the three known engine numbers BEFORE using it to predict adaptive:

                        engine              model
  flat            351.1 / p95 605     234.9 / p95 647
  skip=1           54.8 / p95  86      40.4 / p95 109
  skip=2           48.8 / p95 118      41.0 / p95 161

  flat/skip=1 ratio        6.41 vs 5.81   within ~10%      VALIDATED
  skip=2 tail worse        +37%  vs +48%  reproduced       VALIDATED
  skip=2 MEAN better       -11%  vs +1.5% NOT reproduced   FAILS

  The failing row is precisely the fact that motivates adaptive, so the model is
