  NOT QUALIFIED to predict adaptive's benefit and was not quoted as one.

  INDICATIVE ONLY (model, outside its validated range): adaptive gave the best
  mean (34.0, -16% vs skip=1) and did NOT beat skip=1 on p95 (118 vs 109). So a
  first-cut adaptive scheme -- climb/descend one level per cell, paying a test
  at each level on the way down -- wins the mean and LOSES the tail, because the
  tail is dominated by descent overhead on rays near surfaces, which adaptive
  ADDS to rather than removes.

  => IF ADAPTIVE IS PURSUED, THE ARM TO READ IS p95, NOT MEAN. The mean is the
  easy half; the tail is where a 4 km budget is actually spent.

  THE MODEL'S VALIDATED RANGE, which belongs next to tools/walkmodel/: a
  MECHANISM FINDER, good for tail DIRECTION and the flat/skip ratio to ~10%,
  and explicitly NOT validated for cross-depth mean ordering. It has already
  been quoted out of range once (the withdrawn 13x on random-noise geometry).

A CAVEAT ON MY OWN EXTRAPOLATION, surfaced by the same discussion:
  `march_ms = 0.13 + steps x 5.9 us` was calibrated at the FLAT step
  distribution and is being applied to SKIPPED distributions whose shape differs
  (mean falls 9.2x while p95 falls only 7x). On SIMD hardware a wave costs its
  SLOWEST lane, not its mean, so a distribution with a relatively heavier tail
  costs more per mean-step than the calibration assumes. The 2.9x projection is
  therefore mildly optimistic in a way the arithmetic does not show. Bounded --
  the ratio of ratios is ~1.3x at worst -- but real, and it is another reason
  the p95 arm matters.

================================================================
NOTHING IN THE SCENE CASTS A DYNAMIC SHADOW -- five causes ruled out
================================================================
Prompted by the owner asking for dynamic shadows + GI in production. This is
now a live bug, not a curiosity, and it makes every number in this file a
BEST CASE that the shipping game will not get.

  THE GPU CAPTURE CONTAINS NO SHADOW PASS AT ALL. Searching the whole
  ProfileGPU tree for shadow-related passes returns only `VolumeCascade`
  (translucency lighting volume) and `ClearDepthStencil`. There is no
  ShadowDepths pass, no shadow projection. It is not that TERRAIN fails to
  cast -- NOTHING casts.

RULED OUT, each by measurement rather than reading:
  1. Virtual Shadow Maps      r.Shadow.Virtual.Enable 0 -> frame 18.96 vs 18.99,
                              GPU split unchanged, still no shadow pass.
  2. Scalability              the leg applies [ShadowQuality@3] and
                              [GlobalIlluminationQuality@3] from Scalability.ini.
  3. Light mobility           VoxelSkySubsystem.cpp:2081 SetMobility(Movable).
                              (A Stationary light would make SetCastShadows
                              early-out, which the file itself warns about.)
  4. Cascade count            -VoxelShadowCascades=4 applied and LOGGED
                              ("sun DynamicShadowCascades=4"); shadowGathers
                              stayed 0, frame 19.16 vs 19.20.
  5. The light being absent   the sun exists, is configured, and its properties
                              take. CastShadow=true on the pool component
                              (VoxelGpuPoolComponent.cpp:2387) and on every
                              batch (:1249); the proxy reports bShadowRelevance.

  AND IT USED TO WORK. VoxelSkySubsystem.cpp:2095-2103 records the 2026-07-27
  draw-path diagnosis measuring "the shadow gathers at ~4 per camera gather,
  each submitting against the whole pool". Something between then and now
  turned it off.

LEAD 6 -- DynamicShadowDistanceMovableLight -- TESTED AND WRONG.
  The new unconditional diagnostic prints, on a default leg:

    VoxelSky sun cascade setup: DynamicShadowCascades=4
      DynamicShadowDistanceMovableLight=40000 UU (400 m) CastShadows=1

  400 m, not zero. Four cascades. Casting enabled. THE LIGHT IS A CORRECTLY
  CONFIGURED MOVABLE SHADOW-CASTING DIRECTIONAL LIGHT and no shadow pass
  renders anyway. Six causes now ruled out by measurement rather than reading.

  The diagnostic stays: it costs one log line per leg and it converts this from
  "somebody has to suspect the right property" into "the state is on the page".

OLD LEAD TEXT, kept for the reasoning it records:
CURRENT LEAD: DynamicShadowDistanceMovableLight
  DynamicShadowCascades says how many cascades to slice;
  DynamicShadowDistanceMovableLight says how far they reach. AT ZERO THERE IS
  NOTHING TO SLICE, so the cascade set is skipped entirely -- no ShadowDepths
  pass, no primitive ever gathered -- while every other knob (cascade count,
  VSM, scalability, per-primitive CastShadow, mobility) reads as correctly
  configured. That failure shape matches all five negative results above.

  Added `-VoxelShadowDistanceM=<metres>` alongside the existing cascade
  override, plus an unconditional log of the sun's cascade setup so the default
  value is visible in EVERY leg rather than only when someone suspects it.
  Not yet tested -- the build is blocked on a concurrent agent's file.

WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE MARCHER, and it cuts one way:
  with shadows live the RASTER path pays up to four more cascade passes over
  ~44M primitives (July measured ~4 gathers per camera gather, each against the
  whole pool), while a marcher pays ONE wave-uniform secondary ray per hit.
  So enabling shadows -- which the owner wants -- widens the gap sharply rather
  than narrowing it. The 2.2-2.9x measured here is WITHOUT the feature.


================================================================================
P3 DEPTH GATE, FIRST RESULT -- AND THE BUG IT FLUSHED OUT SIDEWAYS (14:00-14:30)
================================================================================

THE RESULT, on a converged volume, mode 2, budget 886:

  marchMs=5.079  emitMs=0.074  scratchMs=0.080
  frames=5737    emitFrames=5737
  depth gate: FAIL | compared=510444 disagree=18691 (3.6617%, bar 0.5%)
    edge=18580 interior=111 (bar 0) | edgeMaskCovers=56.37% | maxDelta=594.646 vox
    buckets <0.25=471914 <0.5=19839 <1=5216 <2=8765 <4=3447 >=4=1263
    miss=844446 hitOnSky=6

WHAT STANDS:
  marchMs=5.079 against the independent spike's 5.21 at the same budget and
  pose. TWO SEPARATE IMPLEMENTATIONS, WRITTEN BY DIFFERENT AGENTS FROM THE SAME
  FORMAT CONTRACT, AGREEING TO 2.5%. That is the strongest corroboration the
  cost model has had, and it is worth more than either number alone.
  frames == emitFrames (5737): the NeverCull fix holds, so the timing brackets
  are measuring work that actually ran. This was a real risk -- RDG would
  otherwise have culled the whole marcher and reported near-zero ms for it.
  92.5% of compared pixels agree within a QUARTER VOXEL; 96.4% within half.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE HOOK HANDED THE EMIT 1x1 BLACK TEXTURES, AND HAD BEEN DOING SO ALL ALONG
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Surfaced as an RDG error that pointed at the WRONG object:

  Pass 'ClearRenderTarget(VoxelMarch.Scratch.SceneColor ...) 1x1 ClearAction'
  attempted to bind texture as a render target, but the texture has not been
  created with TexCreate_RenderTargetable.

Root cause is not a missing flag. GetOrCreateSceneTextureUniformBuffer IGNORES
the SetupMode argument whenever the frame already has scene textures -- it
returns SceneTextures->UniformBuffer verbatim (FXRenderingUtils.cpp:109-112)
and only honours SetupMode on the fallback path at :118. The renderer sets
SetupMode = ESceneTextureSetupMode::SceneDepth during the prepass
(DeferredShadingRenderer.cpp:2499). So at this hook it returned the 1x1 black
SYSTEM texture for SceneColor and for every GBuffer.

  ==> THE EMIT HAS NEVER WRITTEN A REAL GBUFFER, IN ANY MODE.

It bound 1x1 dummies and reported a perfectly plausible 0.074 ms for doing it.
The only reason this surfaced at all is that mode 2 COPIES the descriptor to
build its scratch set, so RDG complained about the copy -- an error about the
scratch that was really about the source. Both symptoms (no RenderTargetable,
and "1x1") are the same fact.

  emitMs=0.074           DISCARD. It is the cost of writing 1x1 dummies.
  the depth gate         STANDS. It reads MarchVis/MarchHitT from the march CS
                         and RenderTargets.DepthStencil, the hook's own
                         argument. It never touched the uniform buffer.
  marchMs=5.079          STANDS, same reason, and corroborated by the spike.

Fixed by taking colour targets from the RenderTargets argument -- the exact set
the base pass just drew through -- and mapping engine slots to the fixed
SV_Target order via FSceneTexturesConfig::Get().GBufferBindings[GBL_Default],
which is public and is the same table the renderer built its bindings from.
The uniform buffer is now out of the emit path entirely.

GENERALISES: a helper that silently ignores the argument you passed it, and
returns a VALID object of the right type, cannot be caught by a null check or
by reading the call site. What caught it was a downstream consumer with
stricter requirements than the original one. Ask of any engine getter: does it
have a mode argument, and is there a path where it ignores it?

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TWO CLAIMS OF MINE FROM THIS RESULT, BOTH WRONG
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. "maxDelta = 594.6 voxels (~59 m) is a population, not a stray ray -- a real
   second bug." WRONG, and the instrument was the problem. At a silhouette the
   two renderers do not disagree about the DEPTH OF ONE SURFACE; they disagree
   about WHICH SURFACE THE PIXEL BELONGS TO. Foreground at 5 m, background at
   65 m, delta 60 m. So that number is the SCENE'S DEPTH RANGE, not an error
   magnitude, and a max taken over all compared pixels cannot distinguish one
   broken ray from ordinary edge sampling. Consistent with this: 18,580 of the
   18,691 disagreements are edge-classified. The tail is where it belongs.
   FIX: max and buckets are now tracked a SECOND time over INTERIOR pixels
   only, printed first and labelled. That is the number that can answer the
   question; the run above could not.

2. "classified == total (21,340) means the tile classifier rejects nothing, so
   a 58% emit saving is available and untaken." WRONG. There is no separate
   classify stage. The march dispatches over the whole view rect -- correct,
   since the volume is centred on the camera -- and SELECTION HAPPENS AFTER IT,
   FROM REAL HITS. hit=8983 is what the indirect emit actually rasterises. The
   saving was ALREADY BEING TAKEN. TilesClassified was a field filled with
   TileTotal, which read exactly as I read it; removed rather than fixed, and
   the line now prints `tiles total=21340 drawn=8983`.

   NOTE THE SHAPE: a counter that is not wired to anything reports a plausible
   value and invites a confident wrong inference. Same family as the emitMs
   above and as the silent-counter failures already recorded on the water work.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
AUTOMATION: VoxelEarth.GpuPool 8/8 GREEN (was 6/8), AND BOTH FAILURES WERE TESTS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

BrickEviction FAIL -- the pool correctly refused a chunk of 1025 occ words
  against a 1024-word arena, which is EXACTLY WHAT THE TEST ASSERTS. Every
  assertion passed. It failed because the pool logs the refusal at Error
  severity and the automation framework promotes any Error logged during a
  test into a failure. Fixed with AddExpectedError rather than by lowering the
  production log level: in a live run "the brick pool refused a chunk" IS an
  error and must stay loud.

BrickFlushQueue FAIL -- "Expected 'something was evicted' to be true". The
  test's own comment said "three are resident, so two more force one out". Two
  were resident. Chunk 1 had been removed, and the re-add of key {0,0,0,0} is a
  REPLACEMENT, not an addition: AddChunkFromGpu finds a resident key, frees it
  and removes it before allocating (VoxelBrickPool.cpp:313-327). So 10 and 11
  filled the four-chunk capacity exactly and evicted nothing. It takes three.
  Fixed by adding chunk 12 and writing the count down in the comment.

  BOTH DIAGNOSES ARE THE CONVENIENT ONES -- "the pool is fine, the tests were
  wrong" -- so both were checked against the pool source before being accepted,
  not inferred from the test text. The refusal path and the replace-on-re-add
  path were read directly.

  Worth noticing: BrickFlushQueue's eviction assertion had been passing zero
  eviction scenarios. It was green-looking and testing nothing.


================================================================================
P1/P2 BYTE-EQUALITY GATE: PASS -- AND TWO DEFECTS THE SAME LEG EXPOSED (14:30)
================================================================================

Leg: p1-brickgate-r1, spawn -61440,-61440, static flight, 2560x1440.
This gate had NO RESULT EITHER WAY before now -- its first attempt died in a
breadcrumb assert before reporting. It now has one.

  voxel.GPU.VerifyBrickPack: PASS -- the resident volume is byte-identical to
  the CPU reference -- 16/16 chunks checked against packChunkBricksCanonical.
  scratchDescFail=0 poolDescFail=0 occFail=0 matFail=0 recordFail=0 guardFail=0
  failed=0 sizeMismatch=0 doubleDelivered=0.
  Pool: 16 resident, 7904 occ + 7086 mat dwords used, largest free run
  occ 13600 mat 140626, allocFail 0, evictions 0.

So the GPU brickpack kernel, the pool write, the rebase-by-real-allocation, the
32 B chunk record including allSolid, and the guard band over every unallocated
dword all agree with vxc::packChunkBricksCanonical to the byte. Together with
the earlier P1-A/P1-B cross-verification (1,024 bricks, 0 mismatches) the format
contract is proven on THREE independent implementations.

Also passed the same day: VoxelEarth.GpuPool automation, 8/8 (was 6/8).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DEFECT 1: THE GATE'S OWN CVAR FORCING IS A SILENT NO-OP
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The gate prints:
  "Forced voxel.GPU.BrickPack 1 (was 1) and voxel.GPU.BrickPackResident 0
   (was 1) for this run; both are restored afterwards."

The same log, same timestamps:
  LogConsoleManager: Warning: Setting the console variable
  'voxel.GPU.BrickPackResident' with 'SetByCode' was ignored as it is lower
  priority than the previous 'SetByConsole'. Value remains '1'

EVERY cvar on this project is set from the command line via -ExecCmds, which is
SetByConsole, and SetByCode cannot outrank it. So the force did nothing while
announcing that it had.

Does NOT invalidate this PASS: doubleDelivered=0 is precisely the counter that
would catch the private pool being fed twice, and it reads zero.

DOES invalidate any CONTROL ARM run this way. Forcing voxel.GPU.BrickPack 0 for
a control would be ignored identically, and the "control" would be a
byte-identical rerun of the direct arm -- a control that agrees with the thing
it is controlling, for the worst possible reason. Same family as every silent
no-op already recorded on this project.

RULE: a gate that cannot establish its own preconditions must REFUSE TO REPORT,
not report anyway. Read the cvar back after forcing; abort on mismatch.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DEFECT 2 (OPEN): THE BRICK VOLUME HOLDS 8.4% OF THE WORLD
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  world settled:  loaded=50560  quads=46843133  jobsInFlight=0  pendingJobs=0
  brick pool:     4272 chunks resident of 65536; resident 8.8 MiB,
                  committed 314.0 MiB

4,272 / 50,560 = 8.4%. The census predicted ~223 MiB for this cascade; this is
8.8 MiB. Capacity is not the limit (6.5% of chunk slots, allocFail 0,
evictions 0) and there are no pool refusals -- I misread 142 case-insensitive
"refused" matches as brick-pool refusals; they are unrelated tile telemetry and
every one of them reads refusedTiles=0.

Prime suspect from the same log:
  VoxelGpuMesh: job 4313 for chunk (-1167,-1197,44) L4 ended TimedOut --
  requeueing on the CPU worker path. readback not ready after 10.0 s
If the CPU worker fallback does not pack bricks, every chunk that ever falls
back is silently absent from the volume.

READING DISCIPLINE FAILURE, MINE, FIFTH TIME: both Brick.Stats reads fired at
18:34:58 and 18:35:48; the world did not reach jobsInFlight=0 until 18:36:36.
BOTH READS PRECEDE CONVERGENCE. They were identical to each other and I nearly
took that as proof of settling -- but two identical reads of a stalled counter
look exactly like two identical reads of a converged one. Agreement between two
reads establishes convergence ONLY IF the driving process is known to still be
running. Here it was: the world was still loading, so identical reads are
evidence the POOL stopped, not that the pool finished.
Re-running as p1-brickgate-r2 with both reads placed after jobsInFlight=0.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DEFECT 2 CONFIRMED ON A PROPERLY CONVERGED READ (p1-brickgate-r2)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

World converged at 18:39:28 (loaded=50507, jobsInFlight=0, pendingJobs=0) and
held. Both Brick.Stats reads placed well after it:

  18:45:05  4368 chunks resident of 65536; resident 8.6 MiB, committed 314.0 MiB
  18:46:05  4368 chunks resident of 65536; resident 8.6 MiB, committed 314.0 MiB

  ==> 4,368 of 50,507 world chunks = 8.65%.

Stable across two legs (4,272 then 4,368; the r1 figure was depressed by the
verify run sharing the box). So the shortfall is REAL, not a convergence
artifact, and the previous leg's ambiguity is resolved: the pool had indeed
stopped, it had not finished.

Density where it IS packed: 8.6 MiB / 4,368 = 2.07 KiB per chunk. Scaled to all
50,507 chunks that projects to ~100 MiB -- an ESTIMATE, and one that assumes the
missing 91% resemble the present 9%, which is exactly the assumption a
selection bug would violate. Do not quote it as the volume size. It is recorded
only because it is well under the census's ~223 MiB and therefore does not
threaten the VRAM case whichever way the gap resolves.

Cause under investigation; leading suspect is the CPU worker fallback path not
packing bricks. NOT yet established as bug vs by-design.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
CORRECTION TO THE ABOVE: MY "READING DISCIPLINE FAILURE" WAS ITSELF A MISREADING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I wrote that both p1-brickgate-r1 Brick.Stats reads preceded convergence. They
did not. The world reached loaded=50560 / jobsInFlight=0 / pendingJobs=0 at
**18:31:31** and held there. The reads at 18:34:58 and 18:35:48 are 3.5 and 4.3
minutes AFTER convergence. The original reads were valid all along.

The mistake: I took the LAST streaming census line in the file (18:36:36) as the
convergence time, when it is merely the last line printed. The right question is
when jobsInFlight first reached 0 and stayed there, which is a `grep | head -1`,
not a `tail`. Guarding against reading-too-early made me misidentify WHEN too-early
ended -- an over-correction, and one that cost an 8-minute leg.

The re-run was not wasted: it is an independent converged read that AGREES
(4,368 / 8.6 MiB against 4,272 / 8.8 MiB), and it was taken against a stated
prediction rather than after the fact.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DEFECT 2 ROOT CAUSE: THE BRICK POOL IS FED BY A FORK THAT CARRIES 5% OF TRAFFIC
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PREDICTED BEFORE THE READ, then confirmed: 4,000-5,000 chunks, 8-11 MiB.
MEASURED: 4,368 chunks, 8.6 MiB.

The accounting from p1-brickgate-r1:

  chunks tracked                            100,863
  chunks that got a mesh job                 88,151
    of those, produced geometry              50,560
    of those, meshed to ZERO quads           37,539
  jobs the GPU fork ever dispatched           4,564   <-- 5.2% of 88,151
    of those, timed out                          52
  chunks in the brick pool                    4,272

4,564 dispatched - 52 timed out = 4,512, against 4,272 resident, of which 16 are
the verify's own (they reached the global pool too, because the
BrickPackResident 0 force was ignored -- see Defect 1). Residual ~256 = exactly
one in-flight cap's worth of cancelled jobs. THE ACCOUNTING CLOSES.

  ==> THE BRICKPACK PATH IS WORKING AT ~100% OF WHAT IT IS OFFERED.
      Every GPU job returning Success packed a chunk. VoxelBrickPool and
      brickpack.ush are clean. The 8.6% is entirely upstream.

MECHANISM: the GPU fork's in-flight queue (cap 256, VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp:1800,
:10700) was saturated from the FIRST 5-second window -- streaming loaded 11,020
chunks in 5 s against a fork throughput of ~250/s decaying to ~25/s as
submitToDeliver climbed 1,065 ms -> 9,920 ms. Everything arriving while full went
to the CPU worker, and THE CPU WORKER HAS NO BRICK CODE AT ALL. The fork stopped
dispatching entirely at 18:31:31, a full minute before streaming finished.

So this is not a brick bug and not a capacity bug. It is a coverage bug: the new
volume is fed from one branch of a pre-existing fork, and that branch was never
the majority path.

CONDITIONS UNDER WHICH A LOADED CHUNK NEVER REACHES THE POOL (verdicts stated):
  GAP  1. GPU queue full -> CPU worker, which never packs   :10700 / :10740
          ~83,600 of 88,151 chunks. THIS IS THE WHOLE SHORTFALL.
  GAP  2. Timed-out job sets bNoGpuRetry permanently, CPU-only for the session
          :10203-10213. 52 chunks here -- the mechanism I suspected, but a
          rounding error next to #1.
  GAP  3. Edited/overlay chunks and every post-edit re-mesh are game-thread CPU
          only :10447-10451. ZERO impact on this leg (no edits) but it means
          THE BRICK VOLUME SILENTLY DIVERGES FROM THE WORLD AFTER ANY EDIT.
  GAP  4. Adopted parked geometry, unparked without a new job :8631-8692. Small.
  OK   5-11. Buried band (fired 0x), sky band (fired 96x), ring level above
          GpuMeshMaxLevel (default 5 excludes nothing), non-Success job status,
          brick readback copy failure, occ/mat overflow guard, water chunks.

MY HOMOGENEOUS-CHUNK HYPOTHESIS: WRONG, and it mattered. AddChunkFromGpu has NO
all-air/all-solid early return (VoxelBrickPool.cpp:293-405); OccWords==0 and
MatWords==0 are handled inline and the chunk is stored COLLAPSED (64 descriptors
+ a 32 B record). A zero-quad GPU job still returns Success and still packs. So
the 37,539 zero-quad chunks BELONG in the pool, and the census does not need
restating on a different basis.

THE RIGHT TARGET IS 88,151 CHUNKS, NOT 50,560, precisely because zero-quad
chunks pack. At the measured 2,153 B/chunk (512 desc + 776 occ + 833 mat + 32
record) that is ~181 MiB -- close enough to the census's 221.6 MiB that THE
CENSUS IS NOT THE THING THAT IS WRONG. My earlier ~100 MiB scaling was based on
50,507 chunks and is superseded.

TWO SIZING CONSEQUENCES TO DECIDE BEFORE FIXING COVERAGE:
  a) AT FULL COVERAGE THE POOL OVERFLOWS. 88,151 x 64 = 5.64M descriptor slots
     against 4.19M capacity, and 88,151 chunks against a 65,536 chunk capacity.
     Both 1.35x over. Sizing must move WITH coverage, in the same change.
  b) EVICTION IS FIFO, NOT LRU (VoxelBrickPool.cpp:260-291) and nothing in
     streaming calls RemoveChunk. Under pressure it evicts the chunks NEAREST
     THE PLAYER FIRST, because those loaded earliest. That is the worst possible
     order and it only becomes visible once (a) is hit.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A WASTED LEG, MINE: THE MARCHER'S SOURCE IS THE FLUID VOLUME, NOT THE BRICK POOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

p3-depthgate-r2 reported:
  Voxel march: mode=scratch | marchMs=never-ran | frames=0 emitFrames=0
  | tiles total=0 drawn=0 | declined noView=0 noVolume=0 noTextures=0 unsupported=0
  Voxel march depth gate: NOT RUN. This is not a pass.

Every declined counter zero AND frames=0 means the hook body never executed at
all -- the view extension was never registered. Confirmed: zero occurrences of
"view extension registered" in the log, against 2 in the leg that worked.

CAUSE, AND IT WAS MINE. I composed the cvar set by hand and dropped
`voxel.Fluid.Enable 1` and `voxel.DeferExec 70 voxel.Fluid.Spawn 40000`.
FVoxelMarchRenderExtension is registered by VoxelMarchPublishSource
(VoxelMarchRenderer.cpp:218), which is only ever called with a live
FVoxelFluidOccupancyVolume. No fluid, no volume, no publish, no registration --
and IsActiveThisFrame_Internal (:586) then declines before any counter can move,
which is why the failure prints as silence rather than as a decline.

THE STRUCTURAL POINT, WORTH MORE THAN THE LOST LEG:
THE MARCHER IS STILL FED BY FVoxelFluidOccupancy, NOT BY THE BRICK POOL.
That is the Phase 0 spike arrangement, chosen because it needed zero new
producer work -- a 512^3 / 51.2 m SINGLE-LEVEL 1-bit volume. So P1/P2 (the brick
volume, byte-proven) and P3 (the marcher, drawing) ARE TWO WORKSTREAMS THAT HAVE
NOT MET. Every depth-gate number to date describes the marcher reading the FLUID
volume. Wiring the marcher onto the brick pool is the integration step that
makes the cascade and the mip pyramid reachable at all, and it has not happened.

PROCEDURAL FIX: reconstruct a leg's cvars from the -ExecCmds of the leg that
worked (`grep -m1 "Command Line:" <log>`), never by hand. The harness records the
full command line in every log for exactly this reason.


================================================================================
P3 DEPTH GATE r3 -- THE 594-VOXEL MYSTERY WAS THE INSTRUMENT (19:00)
================================================================================

Leg p3-depthgate-r3, correct cvar set reconstructed from the working leg's own
recorded command line. Two reads 60 s apart:

  marchMs=5.058 emitMs=0.068 scratchMs=0.087 | frames=6295 emitFrames=6295
    | tiles total=21340 drawn=8246
  depth gate: FAIL | compared=213696 disagree=10891 (5.0965%, bar 0.5%)
    edge=10871 interior=20 | edgeMaskCovers=54.23%
    INTERIOR: max=0.642 vox | <0.25=97732 <0.5=65 <1=20 <2=0 <4=0 >=4=0

  marchMs=5.109 emitMs=0.072 scratchMs=0.087 | frames=8659 emitFrames=8659
    | tiles total=21340 drawn=8765
  depth gate: FAIL | compared=342105 disagree=15051 (4.3995%, bar 0.5%)
    edge=14934 interior=117 | edgeMaskCovers=62.25%
    INTERIOR: max=0.957 vox | <0.25=128962 <0.5=78 <1=117 <2=0 <4=0 >=4=0

THE ANSWER, AND IT WAS PREDICTED BEFORE THE RUN:
  INTERIOR max = 0.642 and 0.957 VOXELS. Interior buckets <2, <4 and >=4 are
  ALL ZERO on both reads. Not one interior pixel disagrees by as much as one
  voxel.

So maxDelta=594.646 from the first gate was NOT a second bug and NOT a
population of broken rays. It was the SCENE'S DEPTH RANGE leaking into an error
metric: at a silhouette the two renderers do not disagree about the depth of one
surface, they disagree about WHICH SURFACE the pixel belongs to -- foreground at
5 m, background at 65 m, difference 60 m. A max taken over all compared pixels
is dominated by legitimate edge sampling and cannot distinguish a broken ray
from normal behaviour. My reading of it ("a population, not a stray ray; a 59 m
error is a different bug from a one-voxel one") was wrong, and wrong in the
direction of alarm.

CROSS-CHECK ON marchMs: 5.058 and 5.109 here, 5.079 in the first gate, 5.21 from
the independently written spike. Four measurements, two implementations, two
authors, spread under 3%.

MY "58% FREE WIN" IS NOW VISIBLY DEAD ON THE CORRECTED COUNTER:
  tiles total=21340 drawn=8246 (38.6%) and 8765 (41.1%)
The selection was always happening; the old `classified` field was filled with
TileTotal and reported 21340/21340, which read as "rejects nothing".

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE GATE STILL SAYS FAIL, AND THE VERDICT IS THE THING THAT IS WRONG NOW
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

10,871 of 10,891 disagreements are edge-classified; interior is 20 of ~98k
interior pixels on the first read. The 0.5% bar is applied to a total DOMINATED
BY SILHOUETTE SAMPLING, which is expected behaviour, not defect. Meanwhile
edgeMaskCovers is only 54-62%, so a large share of genuine silhouette pixels are
not even being credited as edges.

This is the Phase 0 lesson arriving in a new place: CALIBRATE THE VERIFIER
AGAINST THE REFERENCE'S OWN NOISE FLOOR, NOT AGAINST ZERO. A gate whose pass
condition counts legitimate edge behaviour as failure cannot pass however
correct the marcher is, and "FAIL" from it carries no information.

NOT SETTLED, DO NOT QUOTE AS FINAL: the gate accumulates over frames
(compared 213,696 -> 342,105) and the interior count moved 20 -> 117, so the
interior RATE is not converged either (0.0094% -> 0.0342%). Both reads agree on
the thing that matters -- every interior disagreement is sub-voxel -- but the
counts themselves are still growing.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
CORRECTION: THE GATE NEVER ACCUMULATED. THERE WAS NO DEFINED SAMPLE AT ALL.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I recorded above that "the gate accumulates over frames (compared 213,696 ->
342,105)" and treated the rise as an unconverged running total. WRONG. The
counters buffer is cleared EVERY FRAME (AddClearUAVPass). Those two lines are
TWO DIFFERENT SINGLE FRAMES of a moving scene, handed back by whichever readback
slot happened to be free.

That is worse than accumulation, not better. An accumulating counter at least
converges; this had NO DEFINED SAMPLE. The interior rate moving 0.0094% ->
0.0342% is two arbitrary frames, not a trend, and neither figure had a
denominator worth quoting.

FIX: voxel.March.VerifyDepth is now a FRAME COUNT, not a flag. It sums that many
sampled frames before publishing and the result carries [N frames]. Default 64.
Two reads of a settled system become comparable; two reads of an unsettled one
visibly disagree, which is the honest outcome rather than a hidden one.

  GENERAL RULE THIS EARNS: before quoting any per-frame verifier, establish
  whether it reports ONE frame, N frames, or a running sum. "The number moved
  between reads" has three different meanings across those cases and I assumed
  the most benign one.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE CONTROL FOR THE DEPTH GATE, AND ITS DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Re-rasterising the scene at a jittered pose is not reachable from this seam, so
the control is: THE RASTER DEPTH COMPARED AGAINST A ONE-PIXEL-DISPLACED COPY OF
ITSELF, same pixels, same threshold, same edge mask. Both sides are then the
reference, so whatever it scores is what the instrument reports with the marcher
REMOVED.

It is an UPPER BOUND and is written as one: one pixel is coarser than the
sub-pixel difference that actually separates the two renderers (both carry the
same TAA jitter and differ only in point-sample vs rasterised coverage). Same
character and same wording as the spike's restartNoise control:
  marcher ABOVE it -> hard result
  marcher BELOW it -> WITHIN REFERENCE NOISE (not proof)

A bilinear half-pixel version was REJECTED even though it would be the tighter
bound: interpolating depth across a silhouette averages foreground and
background into a value NEITHER RENDERER WOULD EVER PRODUCE, understating the
floor and flattering the marcher. Choosing the looser bound because the tighter
one is unsound is the right call and is the same reasoning that made
restartNoise an upper bound in Phase 0.

GUARD: if the control scores ZERO while the marcher scores non-zero, the log
says so loudly. A control that never fires is not a denominator -- this project
has shipped probes that measured nothing and reported success.

PASS CONDITION (interior is the gate, edges informational beside their own
control), BOTH required:
  1. interior disagreement rate <= interior control rate
  2. interior max < 1.0 voxel
Condition 2 is NOT redundant and was not my idea: a rate can sit under the
control while a handful of pixels are wrong by METRES, which is a different
failure from sampling noise and the rate alone would not catch it. Today's data
already satisfies it (0.642 / 0.957 vox, <2 <4 >=4 all zero), so it costs
nothing now and catches a real class later.
NO NUMERIC BAR ON THE EDGE POPULATION until the control has run -- inventing one
now would repeat exactly the mistake that produced the uninformative 0.5%.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT THE OCCUPANCY-SOURCE NUMBERS MEAN FOR THE BRICK-SOURCE FUTURE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Nothing in the gate assumes the brick pyramid. VOXEL_MARCH_LEVEL_COUNT is 1
under the occupancy source, so level is always 0 and the threshold is always
0.5 x 10 cm = 5 cm -- THE STRICTEST THE GATE CAN EVER BE. Under the brick
source, coarse rings raise voxelSize(level) and the threshold correctly relaxes
with it. So today's numbers are a LOWER BOUND on how well it will score, not a
special case that stops applying.

Two things WILL change character at the swap, recorded now so they are not read
as regressions later:
  - `miss` collapses. A 4 km volume stops losing rays at 25.6 m, so `compared`
    rises sharply and the population being judged changes.
  - interior max in "voxels" starts mixing scales across levels, so it must be
    read per-level or in UU once rings are live.


================================================================================
COVERAGE FIX BUILT: BRICKS PACK ON THE CPU PATH TOO (19:20)
================================================================================

Build succeeded; VoxelEarth.GpuPool automation 9/9 green including the new
VoxelEarth.GpuPool.BrickCpuArm (CPU add bookkeeping, 64-alignment,
GPU-replaces-CPU-key taking no extra slots, malformed pack refused, and the
eviction ORDER -- near-added-first survives, far-added-later goes).

MY SIZING ARITHMETIC WAS DRAWN FROM A BIASED SAMPLE, AND THIS IS THE GOOD CATCH.
I sized the payload arenas as chunks x 2,153 B/chunk. But that 2,153 B mean came
from THE 4,368 CHUNKS THE FORK HAPPENED TO REACH -- the nearest and earliest
ones -- which is EXACTLY THE SELECTION THE COVERAGE BUG BIASED. Using it to
predict the other 84,000 assumes the missing chunks resemble the present ones,
which is the assumption the bug itself violates. The arenas are instead sized
from the CENSUS (56.0 occ, 142.5 mat MiB) at ~1.5x, because the 37,539 zero-quad
chunks store COLLAPSED and pay no payload at all.
  Sizing: PoolChunks 65536 -> 131072, PoolOccMiB 80 -> 96, PoolMatMiB 200 -> 224.
  Committed 314.0 -> 388.0 MiB.

TRUE LRU WAS CONSIDERED AND REJECTED FOR A REASON WORTH KEEPING: nothing reads a
chunk after add (there is no marcher on this volume yet), so every recency stamp
EQUALS the add stamp and LRU degenerates to precisely the FIFO it was meant to
replace. Eviction is instead farthest-from-focus, via SetEvictionFocusVoxel0 in
LEVEL-0 VOXEL UNITS -- a chunk centre is (Key*32)<<Level by construction, so
every ring is comparable without the pool learning voxel size, world origin, or
that a streaming subsystem exists. Rebuilt only when exhausted or when the focus
moves > 256 level-0 voxels (~1/s at 20 m/s), never per eviction.

GAP 2 CONFIRMED CLOSED BY READING, NOT ASSUMED: a timed-out job sets bNoGpuRetry
and releases the record; RecomputeDesiredSet still wants the chunk, it re-enters
the pending queue, and `bUseGpuMesh && !bNoGpuRetry` routes it to the CPU worker
-- which now packs.
GAP 4 closes CONDITIONALLY: nothing calls RemoveChunk, so a parked chunk's
bricks stay resident and adoption re-checks EditEpoch, meaning the surviving
entry is still correct -- BUT ONLY WHILE EVICTIONS ARE ZERO. If the pool ever
evicts an adopted chunk, no job re-produces it. The new sizing is what keeps
that true, which makes `evictions 0` a correctness property here, not just a
capacity comfort.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE PUSHBACK I ASKED FOR, AND IT IS A REAL ONE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

packChunkBricksCanonical is 32,768 sampler calls per chunk and CANNOT use the
brick skip the mesher uses (a buried brick emits no face but is still solid
rock). Against a level-0 mesh job's ~64,000 minus skips, the estimate is
**1-3 ms of worker time per level-0 chunk, a plausible 30-60% hit to chunks/s.**

That cost lands on THE VERY PATH THIS PROJECT EXISTS TO SPEED UP. If it is real
at that magnitude, raising the GPU queue cap becomes the better trade FOR
COVERAGE -- though it would still leave gap 3 (edited chunks are game-thread CPU
only, so the brick volume silently diverges from the world after any edit) wide
open, which is why the CPU arm was built as asked rather than instead.

voxel.Brick.PackOnCpu 0/1 is the control arm that settles it. NOT YET RUN.

PREDICTIONS, STATED BEFORE THE LEG:
  chunks resident    84,000-88,151, centre ~86,000 (below 88,151 because stale
                     and superseded results are discarded before publication and
                     re-meshes replace by key)
  resident MiB       165-200, centre ~180. Occupancy ~60-68 (census 56 and the
                     measured 776 B/chunk agree within 16%). MATERIAL IS THE
                     UNCERTAIN AXIS: measured-scaled says ~70 MiB, census says
                     142.5. If material lands near 142, total is ~250 MiB against
                     224 capacity -- tight-but-not-over, and the number to watch.
  allocFail 0, evictions 0. ANY eviction means the material sizing was wrong.
  added (gpu N, cpu M) with M >> N, roughly 95:5.

A TRAP RECORDED BEFORE IT COULD FIRE: voxel.GPU.BrickPack DEFAULTS TO 0 and the
CPU arm is subordinate to it, so a leg that forgets it packs nothing on EITHER
arm while looking like a valid run. Set it explicitly in every leg.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
COVERAGE RESULT: 8.6% -> 99.4%. EVERY PREDICTION LANDED.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Leg p2-cpupack-on-r1. Streaming converged 19:27:58; both Brick.Stats reads
(19:34:00, 19:35:00) are ~6 minutes later and byte-identical to each other.

  cpu:gpu roughly 95:5               91.7 : 8.3            close

COVERAGE 87,644 / 88,151 = 99.4%, from 8.6%. The 507 short are stale and
superseded results discarded before publication, exactly as predicted.

THE UNCERTAIN AXIS RESOLVED THE WAY THE AGENT CALLED IT. Material was the one
term where the census (142.5 MiB) and the measured-scaled estimate (~70 MiB)
disagreed by 2x, and it landed at 62.5 MiB -- the measured axis was right and
the census over-predicts material by ~2.3x. Occupancy landed 59.4 against a
census 56.0, agreeing within 6%. So the arena sizing holds with room:
occ 62%, mat 28%, desc 67% used, every arena still at ONE free run (no
fragmentation at all after 87,644 adds).

`evictions 0` matters beyond capacity comfort: nothing calls RemoveChunk, so
parked-chunk adoption stays correct ONLY while evictions are zero. It is a
correctness property here.

COST: 0.743 ms/chunk of worker time, 59,691 ms summed across threads over
80,328 chunks, 153.1 MiB uploaded. That is the LOW end of the predicted
1-3 ms/chunk.

COLD FILL, INDICATIVE ONLY -- NOT A CONTROL:
  p1-brickgate-r2 (no CPU arm in the build at all)  35.3 s to converge
  p2-cpupack-on-r1                                  48.1 s to converge  +36%
Inside the predicted 30-60%. But these are DIFFERENT BUILDS on different runs
with different OS cache state, and the streaming census only prints every 5 s,
so both ends carry +/-5 s. Do not quote +36% as the cost. The real control
(same build, voxel.Brick.PackOnCpu 0) is running as p2-cpupack-off-r1.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE CONTROL ARM, AND IT IS A GENUINE ONE THIS TIME
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

p2-cpupack-off-r1, same build, same params, same spawn, voxel.Brick.PackOnCpu 0:

  4477 chunks resident; resident 8.8 MiB, committed 388.0 MiB
  added 4477 (gpu 4477, cpu 0), evictions 0, allocFail 0, writesDropped 0
  cpu arm: 0 chunks packed ... voxel.Brick.PackOnCpu is 0.

`added (gpu 4477, cpu 0)` PROVES THE ARM WAS OFF. Contrast Defect 1 earlier
today, where a control was announced and silently ignored. This one reports its
own state and the state matches. It also reproduces the pre-fix coverage exactly
(4,477 against 4,272 and 4,368 measured before the change), which independently
confirms the diagnosis.

  arm            cold fill   chunks     MiB     producer split
  PackOnCpu 0      35.4 s      4,477     8.8    gpu 4477, cpu 0
  PackOnCpu 1      48.1 s     87,644   167.4    gpu 7316, cpu 80328
                    +36%       19.6x

The cross-build indicative figure (35.3 s) and the true control (35.4 s) agree
to 0.1 s, which retires the cache/build-difference worry I attached to it.

GAME THREAD IS NOT THE COST: brickFlush=0.3-0.4 ms per 5 s window. The entire
+36% is worker-thread throughput, exactly where the arm's author predicted a
regression would appear.

THE OPEN DECISION, STATED SO IT IS NOT QUIETLY BANKED EITHER WAY:
+36% on cold fill is a real regression on the path this project exists to speed
up. The transitional framing says it is temporary -- right now BOTH producers
run (quad mesher AND brick packer) for the same chunks, and Phase 5 retires
terrain quad meshing, at which point the CPU worker stops meshing quads and only
packs bricks, making this a reduction rather than an addition.

THAT FRAMING IS A PREDICTION, AND THE STANDING RULE FORBIDS BANKING IT: no
saving goes on the plan until an experiment removes the work and measures the
frame. The experiment that would settle it -- suppress terrain quad meshing,
leave brick packing on, measure cold fill -- is now the highest-value item, above
either micro-optimisation, because it decides whether the cost is REAL or merely
EARLY.

The marcher disagrees with the rasteriser SIX TIMES LESS than the rasteriser
disagrees with a one-pixel-displaced copy of ITSELF. That is the denominator the
earlier "FAIL, 4.4% disagree, bar 0.5%" never had -- and against it, 4.4% was
never a failure, it was a sixth of the instrument's own floor.

Interior max 0.964 against the control's 0.962: the largest interior
disagreement the marcher produces is the same size as the largest the reference
produces against itself, and both sit just under the 1.0-voxel bar.

THE VERDICT IS DELIBERATELY "NOT PROOF". The control is an UPPER bound -- one
pixel is coarser than the sub-pixel difference that actually separates the two
renderers. Above it would be a hard defect; below it is consistent with sampling
noise and nothing more. Same asymmetry as the spike's restartNoise control, and
for the same reason.

THE FRAME-COUNT FIX WORKED, WHICH IS ITSELF THE EVIDENCE: two reads now agree to
0.002 percentage points on the gate (0.0420 / 0.0440) and 0.009 on the control
(0.2723 / 0.2634). The previous instrument moved 0.0094% -> 0.0342% between
reads of the same settled scene, because each read was one arbitrary frame.

THE CONTROL FIRED: 18,442 of ~6.8M interior pixels. Not zero, so the
"a control that never fires is not a denominator" guard did not trip.

emitMs IS NOW A REAL NUMBER. The binding log confirms it:

  1552x880
Full resolution, not the 1x1 system texture. emitMs 0.069 / 0.046 against the
1x1 era's 0.074 -- essentially unchanged, exactly as predicted, because the pass
is OVERHEAD-bound (PSO set, indirect draw, ROP setup) and not bandwidth-bound.
A number that does not move after a fix is only reassuring once you know why it
should not have moved.

marchMs 5.077 / 5.114, consistent with 5.058, 5.079, 5.109 and the spike's 5.21.

NOT YET RUN, and the log now names it: the identity check
  mode2frame - mode0frame == marchMs + emitMs + scratchMs
needs a mode 0 arm to close. "If the identity does not close, something is being
paid that no bracket here contains." That is the next timing measurement, and it
