================================================================================
THE +36% ATTACKED THREE WAYS: ONE REAL, ONE FALSIFIED FROM THE RECORD, ONE ARM
================================================================================

LINE 1 -- STOP RE-SAMPLING WHAT THE MESHER ALREADY SAMPLED. REAL, IMPLEMENTED.

mesher.h:56-70 already materialises the brick PLUS its apron into
MaterialId mat[10^3] before any face scan, then discards it. Its own comment
gives the reason: the face scan reads every voxel six times plus nine per
visible face, so a live sampler was being re-evaluated ~10x per voxel. So the
dense array the packer needs was ALREADY BEING BUILT AND THROWN AWAY.

Per brick the mesher reads 1,000 and the packer needs 512 of them. Sharing takes
a chunk from ~96,800 sampler calls to ~64,000 -- A THIRD OF THE SAMPLING
REMOVED, the same order as the +36%.

Three pieces of the implementation worth recording:
  * A TEMPLATE SINK, NOT A POINTER. FNoVoxelSink with kRecords=false means every
    `if constexpr` compiles to nothing, so a mesh with no packer behind it is
    INSTRUCTION-identical, not merely byte-identical. A `const MaterialId*` with
    a null check would have cost a branch on 64,000 samples per chunk to buy
    nothing.
  * THE SKIPPED-BRICK CASE, which would have been silently wrong. The level-0
    skip means meshBrick never runs and never reads those cells -- but "no AIR
    in brick + apron" leaves a brick that is fully solid AND MIXED, whose
    palette only its 512 materials can supply. Skipped bricks now pay 512 direct
    samples instead of the 1,000 the skip saved, so the skip stays a net win.
  * A REFUSAL WORTH COPYING: the all-air proof was NOT special-cased, because
    the predicate returns bool and does not say WHICH proof fired. Inventing an
    answer from the wrong one is a hole in the world.

Guard: voxel.Brick.Stats now prints "N of those read the MESHER'S OWN voxels"
separately from total packs. Reading 0 there while packs are non-zero means the
reuse is off and the ms/chunk is the expensive form.

New tests VoxelEarth.BrickSink.* exist because this optimisation IS INVISIBLE
WHEN WRONG -- the mesh is untouched, the pool fills, every counter moves, and
the volume quietly describes different terrain:
  Coverage      all 32,768 interior cells written EXACTLY ONCE (twice means two
                bricks think they own a cell, which a value check misses when
                both writers agree), values equal the sampler cell by cell, and
                the quad stream byte-identical with and without a sink.
  SkippedBricks skips EVERY brick, so the dense array can only be right if the
                skip path fills it alone.
  Skirt         interior identical with and without a skirt AND the quad counts
                DIFFER -- otherwise the test would pass by the mask being
                ignored.
12/12 automation green (9 pool + 3 sink).

Expected 0.743 -> ~0.15-0.25 ms/chunk, cold fill 48.1 s -> ~37-39 s. MEASURING,
not taking.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LINE 2 -- RAISE THE GPU FORK'S IN-FLIGHT CAP: FALSIFIED, AND ALREADY ON RECORD
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Not a cvar: -VoxelGpuMeshInFlight=<N>, resolved once through FParse::Value into a
function-local static, so -ExecCmds cannot reach it and a sweep is one leg per
value. But the sweep was already run on 2026-07-27 and is recorded in that
function's own comment (VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp:1965-1986):

  predicted  1024 lifts ~600 chunks/s toward ~2,400
  MEASURED   590 chunks/s at 1024 against 602 at 256
  and the fork was idling at ~11 IN FLIGHT -- never depth-bound at all

Worse, submit->deliver MAX ballooned to 13,146 ms, past the 10 s retention cap --
and 10 s is the timeout that flags bNoGpuRetry. So RAISING THE CAP MANUFACTURES
the very timeouts that push chunks onto the CPU path. The lever makes the
problem it was meant to fix.

AND THE FORK IS NOT QUEUE-BOUND, IT IS GPU-THROUGHPUT-BOUND, BECAUSE BRICKPACK
ITSELF MADE IT SO. BrickPack re-introduces a readback that D1 removed: under
voxel.GPU.MeshDirectToPool 1 only a 4-byte quad total crosses PCIe
(VoxelGpuMeshJobManager.cpp:119, :136), while BrickPack adds an 8-byte
BrickTotals readback (:1080-1083) and phase 1 blocks the job's completion on it
(:1318). It rides phase 1 rather than adding a second round trip -- but it also
dispatches A WHOLE SECOND REGION through AddRegionPasses (:1053-1054, its own
ColumnMain and VoxelizeMain), roughly DOUBLING the fork's GPU work per job.

That explains submitToDeliver 1,065 -> 9,920 ms far better than queue depth
does, and it inverts the lever: leaning harder on the fork makes its share go
DOWN, not up. The fork's 8.3% is partly CAUSED by brick packing.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LINE 3 -- THE PHASE 5 ARM, NOW RUNNABLE TODAY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

voxel.Brick.SuppressQuadMesh (VoxelBrickPool.cpp:103): at 1 the CPU worker skips
MeshChunkBricks and ONLY packs -- the Phase 5 shape of that worker, on this
binary, now. AND-ed with the pack gate inside the accessor so it can never leave
a worker that neither meshes nor packs; worker-path only; the equivalence
harnesses are suppressed under it so they do not cry "terrain changed shape" on
every chunk.

IT RENDERS THE WORLD EMPTY. A stopwatch arm, never a screenshot arm, never shipped at 1.

THIS IS THE ARM THAT DECIDES WHETHER THE +36% IS REAL OR MERELY EARLY:
  below 35.4 s -> the cost is transitional, confirmed rather than argued, and
                  the remaining gap stops being a shipping concern
  above 35.4 s -> packing genuinely costs more than meshing did, and Phase 5's
                  "this becomes a reduction" needs revisiting BEFORE more is
                  built on it
Neither answer is reachable by argument, which is the whole point.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LINE 1 MEASURED: THE +36% IS GONE. 4.56x LESS PACK TIME, COVERAGE UNCHANGED.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Leg p2-densesink-r1, same params as both earlier arms.

  87804 chunks resident; resident 167.6 MiB, committed 388.0 MiB
  added 87804 (gpu 5208, cpu 82596), evictions 0, allocFail 0, writesDropped 0
  cpu arm: 82596 chunks packed in 13478.1 ms total (0.163 ms/chunk), 157.3 MiB
  82596 of those read the MESHER'S OWN voxels instead of sampling again

  THE GUARD CONFIRMS THE OPTIMISATION IS ACTUALLY ON: 82,596 packs, 82,596 of
  them via the sink. Not 0. This is the counter that exists because a reuse that
  silently fell back would still report a plausible ms/chunk.

  arm                    pack ms/chunk   pack ms total   cold fill   chunks
  PackOnCpu 0                      --              --      35.4 s     4,477
  PackOnCpu 1, no sink          0.743        59,691 ms     48.1 s    87,644
  PackOnCpu 1, dense sink       0.163        13,478 ms     35.6 s    87,804

  ==> 4.56x less pack time (predicted 0.15-0.25 ms/chunk; landed 0.163)
  ==> COLD FILL BACK TO THE CONTROL: 35.6 s against 35.4 s
  ==> COVERAGE HELD: 87,804 chunks, 167.6 MiB, evictions 0, allocFail 0

HOW STRONG IS "BACK TO THE CONTROL"? 35.4 vs 35.6 s is BELOW THE MEASUREMENT'S
OWN RESOLUTION -- the streaming census prints every 5 s, so both endpoints are
quantised and the honest statement is "indistinguishable from the control at
5-second resolution", not "0.2 s slower".

The claim does not rest on that coarse number alone. The worker-ms counter is
exact and independent: 59,691 -> 13,478 ms, saving 46,213 ms of worker time.
Spread across the worker pool that is several seconds of wall, which is the
right order to explain a 48.1 -> 35.6 s move. TWO INDEPENDENT MEASUREMENTS, one
exact and one coarse, agreeing in direction and magnitude.

NET RESULT OF THE COVERAGE WORK:
  brick volume coverage   8.6%  ->  99.4%   (4,368 -> 87,804 chunks)
  resident                8.8 MiB -> 167.6 MiB
  cold-fill cost          none measurable at this resolution

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LINE 3 ARM IS INVALID AS BUILT -- AND ITS OWN GUARD IS WHAT CAUGHT IT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Leg p2-suppressmesh-r1 (voxel.Brick.SuppressQuadMesh 1):

  87753 chunks resident; resident 167.5 MiB. added (gpu 4879, cpu 82874)
  cpu arm: 82874 packed in 50410.6 ms (0.608 ms/chunk)
  0 of those read the MESHER'S OWN voxels ... 0 there while packs are not 0
  means the reuse is OFF and the ms/chunk above is the expensive form.

THE GUARD FIRED, AND IT WAS RIGHT. This is exactly the counter added one leg
earlier for exactly this reason, and it earned itself immediately.

DEFECT 1 -- LINE 1 AND LINE 3 ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE AS BUILT, WHICH NEITHER OF
US SAW COMING. The dense sink is fed BY the mesher; SuppressQuadMesh removes the
mesher; so the packer necessarily falls back to sampling the world itself. The
arm therefore measures pack-only AT THE EXPENSIVE SAMPLING RATE (0.608), not the
Phase 5 shape it was built to represent. Without the guard this would have read
as "Phase 5 packing costs 0.608 ms/chunk" and gone straight into the plan.

DEFECT 2 -- THE COLD-FILL METRIC DOES NOT MEAN THE SAME THING UNDER THIS ARM.
`loaded=` peaked at 3,243 against 50,504 in every other leg, because with quad
meshing suppressed chunks publish no geometry and that counter tracks geometry
publication. Meanwhile the brick pool still reached 87,753. So the pipeline ran
fine; the instrument measures a different quantity here and the 35.4/35.6 s
comparison cannot be extended to this arm at all. A metric that silently changes
meaning between arms is worse than a missing one.

WHAT THE ARM DID ESTABLISH, and it is not nothing: 82,874 chunks packed with NO
mesher present, at full coverage, evictions 0, allocFail 0. The pack path does
not depend on meshing for correctness -- only the OPTIMISATION does.

THE PHASE 5 QUESTION REMAINS OPEN. What it needs:
  a) give the packer its own dense fill when the mesher is absent -- the
     skipped-brick path already written does exactly this, so the arm can
     measure the CHEAP form; and
  b) a per-chunk MESHER timing counter, so the comparison is
        today = mesh_ms + 0.163   vs   phase5 = pack_ms
     measured directly rather than inferred.
An inference IS available from what we have -- the mesher samples 64,000/chunk
against the packer's 32,768, so it should cost more than the 0.446 ms/chunk that
would make Phase 5 a win -- BUT THAT IS ARITHMETIC, NOT A MEASUREMENT, and this
project's rule exists because seven such inferences were falsified 7/7 in a day.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE PHASE 5 ARM MADE MEASURABLE -- PLUS A REAL HOLE FOUND WHILE WIRING IT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. THE PACKER NOW MATERIALISES ITS OWN CHUNK when no mesher is present
   (VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp:1796 PackChunkMaterialising, wired at all three
   worker sites under bSuppressQuadMesh). The scattered PackChunk is no longer
   reachable from the suppression arm. So the arm can finally measure the CHEAP
   form, which is also the honest Phase 5 design: with no mesher, something must
   materialise voxels once, and it should be the packer.

   THE FILL RUNS x,y OUTER AND z INNERMOST, DELIBERATELY. Every sampler on this
   path is COLUMN-shaped -- the level-0 grid holds a 184-byte vxc::ColumnSample
   per (x,y) and Amplifier::materialAt walks it for a given z -- so z-innermost
   touches one column 32 times in a row instead of sweeping the whole 208 KB
   grid 32 times. The dense array is 32 KB and resident either way, so the store
   pattern is the cheap side of the trade.

   TWO PRODUCERS OF ONE LAYOUT IS A TRANSPOSITION HAZARD: the mesher fills the
   same array BRICK-MAJOR through the sink, the new path fills it x,y,z. New
   test VoxelEarth.BrickSink.FillOrder runs both over one sampler and
   BYTE-COMPARES them, and asserts the fixture is non-uniform so a transposition
   cannot pass by accident. If those two ever disagree, the suppression arm packs
   TRANSPOSED TERRAIN and reports a perfectly healthy per-chunk cost for it.

2. THE MESHER IS NOW MEASURED, NOT INFERRED. TimedMeshChunkBricks wraps
   MeshChunkBricks and nothing else -- worker path only. The game-thread edit
   re-mesh is excluded (different thread, different sampler; folding it in would
   produce a per-chunk worker mean that describes no thread), and so are the two
   *GridVerify harnesses, which mesh the same chunk TWICE and would roughly
   double the mean whenever a debug switch was on.

   voxel.Brick.Stats prints mesh / fill / pack, EACH DIVIDED BY ITS OWN COUNT.
   Mesh and pack counts are equal on a normal arm and wildly unequal under
   suppression, so a shared denominator would silently rescale whichever term
   the arm turned off. Then a TOTAL line, with the instruction: today = mesh +
   pack, phase 5 = fill + pack, COMPARE THE TWO TOTALS ACROSS ARMS AND DO NOT
   MIX TERMS. One leg only ever has one of mesh/fill, and pairing this leg's
   mesh with another leg's fill is precisely the arithmetic this instrument
   replaces.

3. A THROUGHPUT METRIC THAT SURVIVES THE ARM: packs/s over the wall span from
   first to last pack, plus a live brickPacks=N (X/s) on the tick budget line.
   Needed because `loaded=` measures geometry publication and means something
   different under suppression.

UNASKED-FOR AND RIGHT: a CONTROL for the 4.56x --
voxel.Brick.PackReuseMesherVoxels (default 1, 0 restores the scattered packer).
Reasoning worth keeping: "a control that stops existing is how a measured win
becomes a believed one." It also keeps PackChunk live code rather than dead.

A REAL HOLE, FOUND WHILE WIRING: the suppression branch BYPASSED ShouldPack(),
so voxel.GPU.BrickPack 0 + PackOnCpu 1 + SuppressQuadMesh 1 would have
suppressed meshing AND STILL PACKED -- an empty world produced by an arm the
master switch was supposed to have turned off. Now composed at the call site.

A SELF-CONFIRMING COUNTER CAUGHT AND CORRECTED: PackChunkFromDense now takes
bFillWasFree. The suppression arm packs from a dense array it filled AND PAID
FOR, so counting it as "read the mesher's own voxels" would have reported the
reuse as working on the one arm where THERE IS NO MESHER TO REUSE. Suppression
passes false; its cost lands in the `fill` term instead.

13/13 automation green (9 pool + 4 sink, incl. FillOrder). Both arms running.
PHASE 5 IS A WIN IFF fill < mesh, and no number is being pre-committed this time.


================================================================================
PHASE 5 MEASURED: 2.49x CHEAPER PER CHUNK. fill < mesh BY 3.57x. (20:53)
================================================================================

Two arms, same build, same params, same spawn.

  p5-today-r1   (mesh + pack)
    mesh   82749 chunks   67017.2 ms   0.810 ms/chunk
    fill       0 chunks       0.0 ms   0.000
    pack   82653 chunks   13083.9 ms   0.158 ms/chunk (82653 read the mesher's own voxels)
    added 87805 (gpu 5152, cpu 82653), evictions 0, allocFail 0

  p5-phase5-r1  (fill + pack, quad meshing suppressed)
    mesh      96 chunks      71.0 ms   0.740 ms/chunk   <- edit path only, NOT the arm
    fill   83671 chunks   19014.4 ms   0.227 ms/chunk
    pack   83671 chunks   13427.2 ms   0.160 ms/chunk (0 read the mesher's own voxels)
    added 87643 (gpu 3972, cpu 83671), evictions 0, allocFail 0

  today   80,101.1 ms / 82,700 chunks = 0.969 ms/chunk
  phase5  32,512.6 ms / 83,671 chunks = 0.389 ms/chunk
  ==> PHASE 5 IS 2.49x CHEAPER PER CHUNK ON THE WORKER PATH
  ==> fill 0.227 < mesh 0.810, by 3.57x -- the stated win condition, met

The pack term is stable across arms and across builds: 0.158, 0.160, 0.163.
Coverage held on both: 87,805 and 87,643 chunks, evictions 0, allocFail 0.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE PRINTED `TOTAL` LINE IS WRONG ON THE SUPPRESSION ARM -- DO NOT QUOTE IT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

It reports 1.127 ms/chunk for phase 5, which would make phase 5 LOOK WORSE than
today's 0.968. It is computed by SUMMING THE THREE PER-CHUNK MEANS. On the today
arm that is harmless because mesh and pack counts are nearly equal (82,749 and
82,653), so the sum happens to equal total_ms/chunks. On the suppression arm it
is not: the mesh term is 0.740 ms/chunk over NINETY-SIX chunks -- the
game-thread edit path, which suppression deliberately leaves meshing -- and
adding it at FULL WEIGHT to a per-chunk total dominated by 83,671 chunks
inflates the answer by 0.74.

Correct arithmetic is total_ms / chunks, which gives 0.389 and reverses the
verdict.

THE IRONY IS INSTRUCTIVE. The same instrument's own header says each term must
divide by ITS OWN count, precisely because "mesh and pack counts are equal on a
normal arm and wildly unequal under suppression, so a shared denominator would
silently rescale whichever term the arm turned off." That reasoning was right
and was applied to the three TERMS -- and then the TOTAL summed them as if the
denominators matched after all. A guard can be correct in its statement and
still be undone one line later.

It also failed in the SAFE direction, which is the only reason it was caught
rather than banked: it made the favoured outcome look bad. Had it inflated
today's arm instead, the win would have looked bigger and nobody would have
recomputed it.

FIX NEEDED: report total_ms / chunks, or weight each term by its own count.
Until then the printed TOTAL is unusable on any arm whose term counts differ.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE PLAN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Phase 5 ("retire terrain meshing") now has a MEASURED saving rather than an
assumed one: 0.969 -> 0.389 ms/chunk of worker time, 2.49x. The earlier
inference -- "the mesher samples 64,000/chunk against the packer's 32,768 so it
should cost more" -- pointed the right way but predicted the wrong magnitude
(2x by sample count; 3.57x measured on fill vs mesh), which is exactly why it
was not banked.

Note what this does NOT say. It is worker-thread cost per chunk, not chunks/s
and not cold fill. The suppression arm cannot be compared on cold fill at all,
because `loaded=` tracks geometry publication and peaked at 3,243 there against
50,504 elsewhere. Converting 2.49x of worker time into a streaming rate needs
the packs/s metric across a matched pair, which has not been read yet.


================================================================================
P3-B1 PRE-REGISTRATION, AND A DOC ERROR THAT TOUCHES THE SHADOW HUNT (22:45)
================================================================================

THE RING-BOUNDARY EXEMPTION IS NOW FIXED IN WRITING BEFORE THE SOURCE EXISTS:
  A compared pixel is RING-DISCORDANT if its hit distance t lies within +/- one
  chunk width of a ring transition radius, measured at the COARSER of the two
  levels meeting there (chunk width at level L = 3.2 m x 2^L). Such pixels get
  their own column, are EXCLUDED from the interior gate, and carry their own
  rate and control. Never folded into agreement.

A more honest classifier would ask whether the marcher's cone level differs from
the ring level of the chunk the RASTER surface belongs to -- but the raster path
does not expose its per-pixel ring level and adding a channel for it costs more
than the gate is worth. Hit distance against known ring radii needs nothing from
the other side and is decided before any failure exists. The width is justified
in the file so widening it later would show in the diff.

AND IT SELF-TESTS: P3-B1 is level 0 only with NO ring transitions, so the
ring-discordant column MUST READ EXACTLY ZERO. A non-zero count means the
classifier fires where no boundary is traversed. The rule gets tested before it
is ever needed -- which is the property a pre-registered exemption should have
and usually does not.

BOTH LOAD-BEARING NUMBERS MARKED DEAD IN THE HEADER, with mechanism not verdict:
  - `0.13 + steps x 5.9 us`: a 1-bit solidity test (3 int ops, 1 load) becomes
    FIVE indirections, of which two are free (the L1 brick bit test on an
    already-loaded register, and the uniform-brick short circuit that skips 512
    cells with no fetch) and three are not. Net unknown.
  - 9.19x skip ratio: measured against a two-level mip over a FLAT volume --
    different structure, different cell sizes, different restart behaviour.
Both state that a shortfall is a FINDING TO SURFACE, not a defect to tune back
toward the old figure.

VerifySource adopted: march both sources on the same frame, same rays, compare
(hit, voxel, t) inside the overlap window. MAT_WATERMARK divergence is a NAMED
EXCLUSION, not a tolerance -- identified by the pool's material, counted in its
own column, logged loudly when non-zero. A comparator that widened a threshold
to absorb it would recreate the exact self-concealing failure format §3a exists
to prevent, one level up.

CHUNK INDEX DESIGN (declaration only, nothing includes it yet):
Dense toroidal grid per level, ONE DWORD per entry -- bit 31 resident, bit 30
anySolid, bits 0-29 slot. Deliberate deviation from the plan's "separate 1-bit
mask": packing gives reject-and-slot in ONE LOAD where a separate mask costs a
second on the common path. 4 MiB at level 0, 24 MiB across six; powers of two so
the wrap is an AND not a modulo.
v1 IS BLUNT ON PURPOSE -- full rebuild and upload whenever the resident set
changed -- because that makes recentring free and THE STALE-SLOT HAZARD
STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE rather than guarded. A stale index entry points a ray at
a freed slot and renders as PLAUSIBLE TERRAIN IN THE WRONG PLACE, not as a
crash. Hard ordering rule recorded: the index upload must be enqueued AFTER
FVoxelBrickPool::Flush() in the same frame.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A DOCUMENTATION ERROR OF EXACTLY 2x, IN THE SUBSYSTEM WE ARE DEBUGGING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

VoxelGpuPoolComponent.cpp:466 documents the cascade as "L0 0-64 m, L1 64-128,
... L5 1024-2048". The actual table (VoxelWorldSubsystem.h:147) is
{0,128}, {128,256}, {256,512}, {512,1024}, {1024,2048}, {2048,4096}.

EVERY LEVEL IS OFF BY EXACTLY 2x, and voxel.Stream.GPUShadowMaxLevel's help text
INHERITS THE ERROR: it says default 4 means "terrain beyond ~1 km stops casting"
when it actually means beyond 2 km.

Routed to the shadow workstream, not as a suspect but as a warning about method:
the documentation and the code disagree on the very subsystem being debugged, so
every comment in the shadow path is a claim to verify rather than evidence.
Seven shadow causes have already been eliminated by measurement rather than by
reading; this is the same lesson arriving from the other side.

Incidentally good for P3-B1: R0 is genuinely 0-128 m, which comfortably contains
the occupancy volume's 51.2 m window, so the level-0 A/B really is like-for-like
on ground already measured four times.


================================================================================
THE IDENTITY CHECK CLOSES: NOTHING IS BEING PAID OUTSIDE THE BRACKETS (22:50)
================================================================================

Two legs, identical but for voxel.March 0 vs 2. VerifyDepth OFF in both, so the
gate's readback is not in either number (confirmed: "depth gate: NOT RUN").

  mode 0   p50 19.72 ms   p95 28.31   frames 15,136   hitches 740
  mode 2   p50 25.23 ms   p95 43.33   frames 12,390   hitches 671
  mode 2 brackets: marchMs 5.206 + emitMs 0.080 + scratchMs 0.087 = 5.373 ms

  IDENTITY:  measured delta 5.51 ms   against bracketed 5.373 ms
             residual 0.137 ms = 2.5%

The within-config spread on this rig was measured at 0.18 ms (~1%) over four
A0-equivalent legs, so THE RESIDUAL IS INSIDE THE NOISE FLOOR. Every millisecond
the marcher adds to the frame is accounted for by a bracket that names it.

This is the check the instrument itself asked for: "if the identity does not
close, something is being paid that no bracket here contains." It closes. That
retires a whole class of doubt -- hidden barriers, un-timed clears, pass setup,
descriptor churn -- without having to enumerate them.

marchMs 5.206 here joins 5.058 / 5.077 / 5.079 / 5.109 / 5.114 and the spike's
independent 5.21. Six measurements, two implementations, two authors, spread
under 3%.

DO NOT BANK THE p95, IT IS NOT A CLEAN COMPARISON. p95 moved 28.31 -> 43.33
(+15 ms), which would be alarming if it were trustworthy -- but three things say
it is not:
  - HITCHES WENT DOWN, 740 -> 671. A real tail regression should raise them.
  - The SAME mode-0 leg reports p95 24.52 on its full-run line and 28.31 on its
    post-warmup line. A metric that disagrees with itself by 3.8 ms inside one
    leg cannot resolve a 15 ms difference between two.
  - Frame counts differ (15,136 vs 12,390), so the two windows contain different
    amounts of streaming.
The p50 identity is the solid result here. The tail deserves its own dedicated
arm -- pinned pose, matched frame counts, hitch attribution -- and until then
"the marcher costs 5.4 ms at p50" is what is measured and the tail is UNKNOWN,
not fine.


================================================================================
THE POOL/MARCHER SEAM, AND FOUR BUGS CAUGHT WHILE BUILDING IT (23:05)
================================================================================

Built and 14/14 automation green (10 pool incl. the new BrickChunkRecord, 4 sink).

BINDING SURFACE: VOXEL_BRICK_POOL_PARAMETERS(), Register(), BindShaderParameters()
shaped exactly like FVoxelFluidOccupancyVolume so the source swap is like-for-like.
Two deliberate departures:
  * STRUCTURED SRVs, NOT TYPED. These arenas have real strides and
    VoxelBrickPoolWrite.usf already declares StructuredBuffer<uint2> /
    StructuredBuffer<uint>. A TYPED SRV OVER A STRUCTURED BUFFER BINDS AND THEN
    READS PLAUSIBLE NONSENSE -- no error, no null, just wrong terrain.
  * FOUR CAPACITIES RIDE THE BLOCK (DescSlots, OccWords, MatWords, ChunkSlots).
    A record names a BrickBase and a descriptor a 28-bit absolute dword offset
    into arenas the shader otherwise cannot size. Bounds-checking against these
    turns a corrupt index into a VISIBLY MISSING CHUNK instead of a read of
    someone else's payload -- the one failure on this path that looks like terrain.

FOUR BUGS FOUND AND FIXED WHILE WIRING, none of which would have crashed:

1. A REAL RACE. The buffer holder was created lazily inside Flush on the GAME
   thread while Register reads it on the RENDER thread, and TSharedPtr assignment
   is not atomic. Init now creates the holder up front; the RHI buffers inside
   are still first-flush-created and still gated on Buffers->IsValid().

2. THE DELTA BUILT FROM A MOVED-FROM CONTAINER. `Writes` is moved into the render
   lambda; reading it afterwards COMPILES, RUNS, AND SILENTLY YIELDS AN EMPTY
   DELTA. Self-caught. The comment at the build site now says why it lives before
   the enqueue.

3. REGISTRATION AND SNAPSHOT MUST BE ONE CALL. Registering late is the NORMAL
   case -- the pool fills to ~87,800 during cold fill while the marcher
   initialises long after -- so a register-then-snapshot pair leaves a window in
   which a delta lands on an unseeded index. Symptom: a handful of chunks
   missing, every counter healthy.

4. APPLY `Removed` BEFORE `Added`. Both can name the same slot in one delta,
   because a freed slot can be re-allocated inside the same flush. The wrong
   order maps the old key to a slot holding the new chunk -- one chunk's bricks
   drawn at another's coordinates.

CHUNK RECORD CONFIRMED AGAINST THE WRITER, NOT THE DOC. BuildChunkRecord is now
the single CPU definition, the upload path calls it, and
VoxelEarth.GpuPool.BrickChunkRecord pins every field offline. Two properties it
pins that a reader would not think to check:
  * the ring level MASKS to four bits rather than clamping (the GPU masks; a
    clamp would silently disagree, and a gate that only ever sees levels 0-5
    would never catch the difference);
  * allSolid is bit 5 and NOT a second anySolid -- the field a producer that
    DERIVED it from descriptors gets wrong, because a brick can be fully solid
    and still MIXED.

INDEX SEAM: a DELTA PER FLUSH plus a seeded snapshot, not a per-frame snapshot --
a settled cascade is ~87,800 entries (~1.4 MB) to describe a set that moves by
tens, and the pool already batches at exactly that granularity.

THE EVICTION HAZARD, STATED WHERE IT BITES: `evictions 0` is currently a
CORRECTNESS property because nothing calls RemoveChunk, so `Removed` is always
empty today -- WHICH MEANS A CONSUMER THAT NEVER IMPLEMENTED REMOVAL AT ALL
WOULD PASS EVERY TEST THAT EXISTS. When the marcher becomes the draw path,
streaming frees at eviction/park/unpark and the index must apply the delta in the
SAME flush. A frame-late index names slots whose ranges belong to someone else,
and because a re-allocated slot holds a REAL chunk rather than garbage, nothing
errors and nothing is null.
  MITIGATION WORTH ADOPTING: THE RECORD IS AUTHORITATIVE, THE INDEX IS A HINT.
  Every record carries its own OriginVoxel and ring level in the cache line the
  lookup already fetched; validating them turns a stale index into a MISS instead
  of a WRONG CHUNK.

LEVEL HISTOGRAM added to voxel.Brick.Stats, with out-of-range levels counted
separately and logged as an ERROR (a key outside [0:3] means a truncated level,
which a cone march across rings reads at the wrong scale). Leg running.
Deliberately NOT folded into a scope recommendation: a ring that is a small share
of the COUNT can still be most of what a CAMERA SEES. It bounds the question, it
does not answer it.


================================================================================
SHADOWS: ROOT CAUSE FOUND. r.ShadowQuality IS 0 IN EVERY LEG EVER TAKEN. (23:15)
================================================================================

THE GATE: ShadowSetup.cpp:6365, FSceneRenderer::CreateDynamicShadows

    if (GetShadowQuality() > 0)

GetShadowQuality() (ShadowRendering.cpp:258) is nothing but r.ShadowQuality. At
0 the ENTIRE per-light loop is skipped: Scene->Lights is never walked,
ShouldRenderLight is never called, AddViewDependentWholeSceneShadowsForView is
never reached, SortedShadowsForShadowDepthPass stays empty, and no ShadowDepths
pass is ever added. That is exactly the observed shape -- every light and
primitive setting correct, zero passes -- and it explains shadowGather=0, since
no shadow view is ever gathered.

WHY IT IS 0: boot applies scalability TWICE and the second pass wins. Verified
by hand in p0-sh-diag-r1.log:
    line 673   Set CVar [[r.ShadowQuality:5]]     [ShadowQuality@3]
    line 864   Set CVar [[r.ShadowQuality:0]]     [ShadowQuality@0]
Identical line numbers in all five shadow legs. Source is
ue-project/Saved/Config/WindowsEditor/GameUserSettings.ini, confirmed:
    sg.ShadowQuality=0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
HOW I RULED THIS OUT WRONGLY, AND IT IS A LESSON WITH TEETH
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I recorded "scalability -- ShadowQuality forced to 3" as RULED OUT BY
MEASUREMENT. What I actually did was grep the log for ShadowQuality, find
`:5 [ShadowQuality@3]` at line 673, and treat "the setting appears in the log
with the value I wanted" as proof it was in effect.

THE OVERRIDE ARRIVES 190 LINES LATER. And no log in Saved/ has EVER contained
sg.ShadowQuality or a runtime r.ShadowQuality, so the forcing I believed I had
applied was never applied in any leg.

  THE RULE: A CVAR CONFIRMED BY ITS FIRST APPEARANCE IN A LOG IS NOT CONFIRMED.
  Read the LAST write, and read what SET it. This is the same failure as
  Defect 1 this morning (SetByCode silently losing to SetByConsole) arriving
  from the opposite direction -- there the force was refused and announced
  anyway; here the force landed and was quietly overwritten. Both produce a leg
  that reports the configuration you asked for and runs a different one.

Seven causes were "ruled out" against a build where the outermost gate was shut.
Every one of those eliminations was sound reasoning applied to a frame that could
not have produced a shadow whatever they said.

SECONDARY DAMAGE from the same @0 block, each of which would have left shadows
present but WRONG even after the gate opened:
  r.Shadow.CSM.MaxCascades=1   caps FViewInfo::MaxShadowCascades, collapsing the
                               sun's 4 cascades to 1 REGARDLESS of
                               -VoxelShadowCascades (which I confirmed applied,
                               and which was therefore also meaningless)
  r.Shadow.MaxCSMResolution=512
  r.Shadow.DistanceScale=0.6   turns the confirmed 400 m into 240 m

FIX (ini only, checked in, no build, no shader): a [ConsoleVariables] block in
Config/DefaultEngine.ini:211 setting r.ShadowQuality=5, CSM.MaxCascades=10,
MaxCSMResolution=2048, DistanceScale=1.0, RadiusThreshold=0.01. That section is
applied AFTER the scalability passes (log line 938 vs 862) and sets at
ECVF_SetByConsoleVariablesIni (0x0A), which outranks ECVF_SetByScalability
(0x02), so CanChange silently refuses any later scalability write. Checked in, so
it survives a Saved/ wipe or a UGameUserSettings::SaveSettings.
Price accepted: sg.ShadowQuality can no longer lower these four.
Deliberately left at 0: r.DistanceFieldShadowing, r.CapsuleShadows -- this
terrain has no mesh distance fields.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
AND THE PART THAT REACHES EVERY PERFORMANCE NUMBER ON THIS PROJECT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The same stale GameUserSettings.ini carries:
    sg.ResolutionQuality=0  sg.ViewDistanceQuality=0
    sg.PostProcessQuality=0 sg.EffectsQuality=0

So EVERY PERF LEG THIS PROJECT HAS EVER TAKEN ran at r.ViewDistanceScale=0.4,
r.MaterialQualityLevel=0 (Low), r.DetailMode=0, r.SceneColorFormat=3,
r.VolumetricFog=0, r.AmbientOcclusionLevels=0.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT INVALIDATE: every A/B on this project. Both arms of every pair
ran under the same preset, so deltas, ratios and identities stand -- the 81% draw
share, the 2.49x Phase 5 result, the 4.56x pack saving, the identity check, the
depth gate.

WHAT IT DOES INVALIDATE: the ABSOLUTE numbers as descriptions of the shipping
game. "18.99 ms, 65 fps" is 65 fps AT LOW SETTINGS WITH NO SHADOWS. The real
default-settings frame is worse, by an unmeasured amount, and the ray-marching
case has been argued against a baseline cheaper than the one players will run.
That cuts BOTH ways and mostly in the project's favour -- raster cost rises with
quality settings far faster than march cost does -- but it is now a known unknown
where it used to be an unexamined assumption.

NOT CHANGED. Raising the other four presets would invalidate comparability with
every leg taken so far, which is too high a price to pay silently. It is a
deliberate decision to be taken with the owner, not a side effect of a shadow fix.


================================================================================
LEVEL HISTOGRAM: L0 IS 19.2%, NOT 80%. THE DOC IS WRONG FOR BRICKS TOO. (23:00)
================================================================================

  residency by ring level (87,800 chunks, 167.6 MiB):
    L0 16892 (19.2%, 39.3 MiB)   L1 14842 (16.9%, 27.3)   L2 14662 (16.7%, 27.0)
    L3 13444 (15.3%, 24.5)       L4 13628 (15.5%, 23.1)   L5 14332 (16.3%, 26.4)

Near-uniform, and L0 at 19.2% matches the 18.4% measured for QUADS earlier today
almost exactly. So docs/gpu-g0-sizing.md's "R0 is ~31,000 of 39,020 chunks,
about 80%" is now REFUTED INDEPENDENTLY FOR BOTH REPRESENTATIONS.

That claim is load-bearing where it sits: gpu-g0-sizing.md concludes "the
question is not 2, 4 or 8 km -- the question is how far out do you want 10 cm",
and that conclusion rests entirely on R0 dominating residency. It does not.
Bricks were the interesting test because brick residency includes collapsed
all-air chunks the quad path never meshes, so the two could legitimately have
distributed differently. They do not.

THE CAVEAT THAT SHIPS WITH THE NUMBER, printed by the instrument itself:
percentages are of RESIDENT CHUNKS, NOT OF SCREEN. A ring that is a small share
of the count can still be most of what a camera sees. L0 covers 0-128 m, which
at the standard pose is most of what is actually looked at. The histogram BOUNDS
the marcher-scope question; it does not answer it. P3-B1 (level 0 only) is
confirmed measurable on that basis, not on the 19.2%.

FOUR DOC-VS-CODE DISAGREEMENTS FOUND TODAY, ALL IN SUBSYSTEMS UNDER ACTIVE DEBUG:
  1. r.ShadowQuality "confirmed" from its first log line; the override lands 190
     lines later. Cost: seven causes eliminated against a shut gate.
  2. The ring cascade comment off by exactly 2x, inherited by
     voxel.Stream.GPUShadowMaxLevel's help text.
  3. voxel.GI.Enabled's help string says "0 = off (default)" on the line directly
     below the line that sets the default to 1. GI has been ON in every leg, so
     every baseline already contains its cost.
  4. gpu-g0-sizing.md's 80% R0 claim, wrong for quads and now wrong for bricks.

  THE PATTERN, WORTH NAMING: on this codebase a comment is a CLAIM TO VERIFY, not
  evidence. The measurement discipline here already encodes that for numbers --
  "no saving goes on the plan until an experiment removes the work" -- and all
  four of these are the same rule arriving for prose. Three of the four were
  found only because someone was forced to read the code for an unrelated reason.


================================================================================
SHADOW FIX CONFIRMED AT THE GATE. A NARROWER ONE REMAINS. (23:05)
================================================================================

Against the three checks stated BEFORE the run:

  1. GATE CHECK -- PASSES. Last `Set CVar [[r.ShadowQuality` in
     p0-sh-quality-r1.log is `:5`. The [ConsoleVariables] block lands after the
     scalability passes and outranks them, as designed.

  2. VERDICT NUMBER -- DOES NOT MOVE. shadowGather=0 in every window. Control 0.

  3. THE PASS EXISTS -- YES, AND THIS IS THE RESULT.
       fixed leg:  ShadowDepth appears 6 times in the ProfileGPU table
       control:    0 times
     It has never appeared in ANY leg before today.

A CLEAN, ATTRIBUTABLE A/B ON ONE VARIABLE: r.ShadowQuality was the gate and it
is now open. The control (r.ShadowQuality 0 at SetByConsole, which outranks the
ini) removes the pass entirely, which is what makes the pair mean something.

BUT every one of those six rows reads 0 PRIMITIVES, 0.000-0.003 ms. The shadow
pass is allocated, executed, and drawing nothing.

WHAT THAT NARROWS. A ShadowDepths pass is not merely reached but allocated
PER CASCADE and executed, so CreateDynamicShadows ran, a light was found,
ShouldRenderLight passed, and cascades were created. ShowFlags.DynamicShadows
cannot be false or there would be no pass at all. Both of the agent's two
remaining suspects are therefore eliminated by this result.

  THE QUESTION IS NOW: why is the voxel terrain pool primitive not gathered into
  an EXISTING, EXECUTING shadow pass? That is a per-primitive relevance question,
  not a light or view question -- a much smaller search space than this morning's.

Note the cull line prints shadowGather=0 beside gather=15025 IN THE SAME FRAMES.
Whatever excludes the shadow view does not exclude the camera view.

TWO OF MY SEVEN ELIMINATIONS WERE WORSE THAN "TESTED BEHIND A SHUT GATE" --
THEY TESTED SETTINGS THAT HAD THEMSELVES BEEN OVERRIDDEN:
  r.Shadow.CSM.MaxCascades=1 (from the @0 block) caps MaxShadowCascades, so
    -VoxelShadowCascades=4 -- which I confirmed applied and recorded as ruled
    out -- was meaningless.
  r.Shadow.DistanceScale=0.6 turned the confirmed 400 m into 240 m.
So "I set it and the log shows it" was false twice over: the value was applied,
logged, and then made irrelevant by a different cvar from the same stale preset.


================================================================================
A BUILD THAT REPORTED SUCCESS WHILE NEVER CHECKING THE FILE (23:20)
================================================================================

VoxelMarchChunkIndex.cpp compiled clean inside the unity blob and the full build
reported `Result: Succeeded`. A standalone -singlefile compile of the SAME FILE
FAILED WITH NINE ERRORS.

Cause: VoxelBrickPool.h declares FBufferRHIRef members and takes an
FRHICommandListImmediate&, and INCLUDES NEITHER. It borrows both from whatever
its includer happened to pull in first.

WHY THIS IS WORSE THAN UNTIDY: new and untracked files go into unity, so the
defect is invisible until someone takes the ADAPTIVE NON-UNITY path -- and UBT
chooses that path from `git status`. So the next person to modify any file that
includes VoxelBrickPool.h gets nine errors naming a header they never touched,
AND IT WILL NOT REPRODUCE for anyone trying to help them.

Fixed at the source: RHIResources.h included, FRHICommandListImmediate forward-
declared (reference-only parameter, so a declaration keeps the RHI command list
out of every consumer), Templates/Function.h included explicitly for TFunction
even though CoreMinimal.h probably pulls it -- "probably" being the exact shape
of the bug. A sweep of every F*/T*/E* identifier the header names found all
others accounted for. VoxelBrickSinkTests.cpp had the SAME LATENT SHAPE (new
file, unity-hidden, named FVoxelChunkQuad while inheriting it) and was fixed too.

An explicit note now says ShaderParameterMacros.h is DELIBERATELY not included --
the parameter macro expands in the CONSUMER's struct, so the header names no type
from it, and adding it would push shader parameter machinery into every consumer
of the pool. Without that note the self-containment rule would read as an
instruction to add it.

  THERE IS NO GREP FOR THIS CLASS OF DEFECT. The only reliable check is a
  periodic non-unity compile. Worth scheduling rather than waiting to trip it.

This is the same family as the whole day: a build reporting success while the
thing being checked was never checked. Compare the stale vxc_gpu binary "passing"
this morning, the cvar force that was silently ignored, and r.ShadowQuality
confirmed from a log line that a later write overrode.

EVICTION ANNOUNCEMENT ADDED, and the reasoning is worth keeping: an Error LOG,
not a check(). Eviction is designed behaviour the pool is meant to perform one
day, and halting the editor the first time it works correctly is the wrong trade
-- it would fire on someone who did nothing wrong. But a log is right because all
three dependents (parked-chunk adoption, the never-exercised Removed path, the
sizing headroom) fail SILENTLY, and two of them produce a plausible-looking world
rather than an error. Error rather than Warning because Error is what the harness
greps. It names whether the eviction was ranked by DISTANCE or fell back to
insertion order -- because if no focus was ever set, the first eviction threw
away the chunk nearest the player, which is a different and worse problem than
running out of room.

PROCESS HAZARD, RECORDED FOR NEXT TIME: an agent's balance-checking script in the
shared scratchpad was REPLACED MID-SESSION by another agent's tool of the same
name, and began reporting phantom imbalances (brace -1, brace +5) on files that a
fresh checker and a manual read both confirm are balanced. Agents sharing one
scratchpad directory can silently overwrite each other's instruments. This is the
non-editor cousin of the one-editor-per-box rule.

P3-B1 CONTROL ARM IN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  source=occupancy  marchMs=5.085 emitMs=0.076 scratchMs=0.095
  chunk index attached: seeded 16,891 level-0 chunks of 87,799 in the pool,
  0 DROPPED outside the 128x128x64 chunk band.

16,891 against the histogram's L0=16,892 -- one chunk, explained by residency
moving between the two reads. And zero dropped confirms the static_assert
reasoning empirically: two level-0 chunks alias only at 128 chunks (409.6 m) and
R0 is 80 chunks across, so the band cannot overflow at this ring size.
marchMs 5.085 sits inside the 5.06-5.21 cluster, so the control reproduces.


================================================================================
SHADOWS NARROWED TO SIX GATES -- AND THREE SUSPECTS DIED BY CONSTRUCTION (23:35)
================================================================================

All three of my suggested suspects are dead, and two of them die BY CONSTRUCTION
rather than by happening to be at a default value -- which is a much stronger
elimination and exactly the standard this project should hold to:

  voxel.Stream.GPUCullDebugDrawNothing bit 2 CANNOT produce this. Both
    suppression paths (VoxelGpuPoolComponent.cpp:1071 bA3Suppress, :1057
    AllCapped) call RecordGather BEFORE `continue`, deliberately, so a suppressed
    gather reads as a MEASURED ZERO. Bit 2 firing would give shadowGathers=240,
    shadowQuads=0. We see shadowGathers=0. NO VALUE of that cvar produces that.
    (I wrote those early-outs this morning and asked for them to be eliminated by
    reading rather than assumed. They were, and the "record the gather at zero"
    pattern I copied is what made the elimination possible.)
  voxel.Stream.GPUShadowMaxLevel dies the same way: its cap is applied inside
    BuildCulledRanges (:1861), which is INSIDE a gather that already happened. It
    can EMPTY a gather; it cannot PREVENT one.
  IsShadowCast against the wrong view is NOT a bug. The engine deliberately uses
    both -- AddSubjectPrimitive_AnyThread calls GetViewRelevance with the MAIN
    view for a whole-scene directional shadow (ShadowSetup.cpp:2246-2249), and
    GatherDynamicMeshElementsArray calls it again with the ShadowDepthView
    (:2925-2929). The proxy answers whichever it is handed, correctly.

THE CENSUS IS A SOUND INSTRUMENT HERE, which matters given this project's
history of probes that measured nothing: for a whole-scene directional shadow the
engine sets ShadowDepthView's cull frustum at :2866 BEFORE calling
GetDynamicMeshElements at :2941. So shadowGathers=0 genuinely means the proxy is
never in DynamicSubjectPrimitives -- rejected in GatherShadowPrimitives.

THREE MORE GATES ELIMINATED BY ARITHMETIC ON DATA ALREADY IN THE LOGS:
  bScreenSpaceSizeCulled needs bounds radius < 0.01 x distance. The
    `VoxelGpuPool placement:` line gives extent (10240, 41280, 6080) UU -> sphere
    radius ~43,000 UU at a few hundred metres. OFF BY FOUR ORDERS OF MAGNITUDE.
  IsScreenSizeCulled is evaluated against the SAME DependentView and the SAME
    bounds as the main view's own cull. The camera gather is healthy, so this
    passed; it cannot selectively kill the shadow.
  CSM CACHING IS OFF -- r.Shadow.CSMCaching defaults 0 and no ini or leg sets it,
    so every cascade is SDCM_Uncached and the movable-only/static-only relevance
    branches are UNREACHABLE. WORTH RECORDING AS A NEAR MISS: with caching ON, a
    pinned camera plus a frozen sun collapses each cascade to a movable-only map
    and would produce EXACTLY "pass executes, draws nothing". That trap is
    specific to -Flight static, which is the mode this project measures in.

SIX GATES REMAIN, all runtime reads of proxy state, none separable by reading:
  1 FPrimitiveFlagsCompact.bCastDynamicShadow (:5020/:5064) -- drops the
    primitive before ANY geometry or light test
  2 cascade geometry: cylinder + spherical cap + IntersectBox (:5152-5158)
  3 CastsInsetShadow via FilterForHigherPriorityShadow (:5211)
  4 AffectsPrimitive -- lighting-channel overlap (:5212)
  5 if (!bShadowRelevance) return 0; (:2338)
  6 if (bOpaque) wraps the ONLY branch that sets bDynamicSubjectPrimitive
    (:2347/:2380)

GATE 6 IS THE ONE NOBODY WOULD LOOK FOR: a terrain pool whose MATERIAL relevance
came back non-opaque would draw normally in the main view and cast nothing, and
no instrument in that file can currently see it.

Reading says gate 1 SHOULD pass (ctor sets CastShadow=true,
bCastDynamicShadow defaults true, GetShadowIndirectOnly false for non-static-mesh
components) -- but "should" is not a measurement, AND THE WHOLE POINT OF THIS BUG
IS THAT EVERYTHING SHOULD BE FINE.

Instrument added: ReportShadowEligibility, called from GetViewRelevance, LATCHED
ON A PACKED ANSWER so it re-prints on any CHANGE rather than firing once on the
empty-pool state. Prints every input to all six gates plus bounds plus whether
the caller is a shadow view, with its own reading key. Verdict table
pre-registered per reading BEFORE the run.

AND AN HONEST NEGATIVE: there is NO useful no-build probe to run meanwhile. The
two candidates are void -- VoxelDetail never appears in these legs at all, and
the fly pawn's ProxyBody is SetVisibility(false) in first person, which -Flight
static is. SO THERE IS CURRENTLY NO SECOND SHADOW CASTER IN THE SCENE. That also
means "6 ShadowDepth rows, 0 primitives" is consistent with the pool being the
ONLY candidate and tells us nothing yet about whether the failure is scene-wide
or pool-specific. The eligibility line settles that.


================================================================================
P3-B1: THE TWO HALVES MET, AND 86% OF THE HITS ARE MISSING (23:30)
================================================================================

  source=occupancy   marchMs=5.144  emitMs=0.067  drawn=8016  indexEntries=16891
  source=brickpool   marchMs=6.202  emitMs=0.038  drawn=1138  indexEntries=16898
  index attached: seeded 16,898 level-0 chunks of 87,806, 0 dropped.

THE BRICK ARM HITS 14% AS MANY TILES AND COSTS 20.6% MORE.

DO NOT READ +1.058 ms AS THE INDIRECTION COST. Those two facts together are a
symptom, not a result. A miss runs to the FULL 886-step budget; a hit terminates
early. So "hits far less, costs more" is what you get when most rays fail to find
terrain that is there and march to budget instead -- meaning the delta is mostly
WASTED STEPPING, not the price of five indirections.

WHY 14% IS NOT AN HONEST DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE SOURCES: isSolidForRender
(m != MAT_AIR) is a SUPERSET of isSolidForFluid, so the brick volume should mark
MORE solid than occupancy, not less. And both arms share the frame, the clip box
and the ray by construction, so traversal length is not the variable either.

WHAT IS GENUINELY EXCLUDED: indexEntries is non-zero, noPool=0, and 0 chunks
dropped outside the toroidal band on both runs. So "the two halves never met" is
off the table, and the static_assert reasoning (aliasing impossible at 80 chunks
across a 128-chunk band) held empirically.

THE GUARD GAP, WORTH FIXING WHATEVER THE CAUSE: the instrument warns loudly when
indexEntries is ZERO. It says nothing when the index is POPULATED and rays still
mostly miss -- which prints a perfectly healthy-looking line. Catching "never
connected" is not the same as catching "connected and wrong".

LEADING CANDIDATES, in the order they should be checked:
  - chunk-coord derivation from world voxel: an off-by-one in the toroidal wrap
    or the level-0 shift puts rays in a NEIGHBOURING chunk that is legitimately
    empty -- consistent with a small number of hits surviving.
  - descriptor `kind` handling: a uniform-AIR brick and an UNALLOCATED one are
    both zeros. The format doc's own warning is that a missing chunk reads as AIR
    rather than as an error.
  - BrickSolidLo/Hi bit order against bx + 4*(by + 4*bz). THE FORMAT DOC RECORDS
    THAT BRICK ORDER WITHIN A CHUNK WAS NEVER STATED ORIGINALLY AND THAT A
    Z-MAJOR READING PASSES EVERY UNIT TEST WHILE PRODUCING A TRANSPOSED WORLD.
    A transposition gives exactly this signature: index populated, rays mostly
    miss, a minority of hits survive.

EXPLICITLY REFUSED: raising the step budget or cheapening the miss path before
the miss count is explained. That converts a VISIBLE correctness bug into an
INVISIBLE performance one.

The control arm reproduced across both legs (marchMs 5.085 and 5.144, inside the
5.06-5.21 cluster), so the rig and the pose are unchanged and the anomaly is on
the brick side.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
CORRECTION: "0 DROPPED" WAS A TAUTOLOGY, NOT EVIDENCE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I wrote above that "0 chunks dropped outside the band confirms the static_assert
reasoning EMPIRICALLY". IT DOES NOT. The out-of-band test is

    uint32(Coord.Z - (Coord.Z & ~int32(kDimZ-1))) >= kDimZ

which reduces to `Coord.Z & 63`, and that is ALWAYS < 64. THE CHECK CAN NEVER
FIRE. `0 dropped` is what this code prints on every possible input, including a
world where every chunk aliased. Found and reported by the agent that wrote it,
against its own guard.

  THE SHAPE, AGAIN: a check that reports the healthy value unconditionally. The
  fifth today, after the stale bench binary, the ignored cvar force,
  r.ShadowQuality read from a superseded log line, and the unity build. Here I
  compounded it by quoting the tautology back as confirmation -- so a guard that
  could not fail became, in my summary, evidence that something had been proved.

AND THERE IS A REAL SECOND DEFECT UNDERNEATH IT. The static_assert only ever
proved the XY case (80 chunks across a 128-chunk band). Z was never covered:
kDimZ = 64 chunks = 204.8 m, while R0 spans 256 m VERTICALLY in the worst case.
So Z CAN alias, silently, and the loser of a collision becomes a HOLE.

Aliasing loses at most half of a colliding pair, so it CANNOT be the whole 86% --
but it must be known before the probe is read. Deliberately NOT fixed yet:
fixing it now would change two things at once while a diagnosis is in flight.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE INDEX-JOIN PROBE, AND WHY NOT THE DEPTH GATE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

voxel.March.VerifyIndex walks the 16x16x16 chunk box the occupancy window spans,
anchored at the marcher's own origin so it asks about exactly the chunks a ray
could reach, and reports per cell whether THE INDEX AND THE RECORD AGREE. It
TRACES NO RAYS -- which is the point: it isolates the join from the DDA
completely.

It reports tallies AND SAMPLES: wanted origin against recorded origin, side by
side, for the first six mismatches. That distinguishes the three causes a count
cannot separate -- A FACTOR OF 32 IS UNITS, A PERMUTATION IS THE TRANSPOSITION
THE FORMAT DOC WARNS ABOUT, AN UNRELATED VALUE IS A STALE OR ALIASED SLOT.

It also goes a layer deeper for free: for chunks that pass the join, does the L1
mask have bits set? The log states the conclusion rather than leaving it to be
inferred -- "every resident cell was REJECTED: the join is broken, not the
traversal" versus "the join is clean but every valid chunk has an EMPTY L1 mask:
the bug is below the join."

WHY NOT THE DEPTH GATE, which I proposed: it would confirm the traversal is wrong
WITHOUT SAYING WHERE, and it needs a converged pose. Right instrument, wrong
question. Run the probe first; if the join is clean, the gate becomes worth
running.

GUARD GAP CLOSED: a populated index with almost no hit tiles is now as loud as an
empty one -- it warns, names the ratio, says "that is not a cost result, it is a
symptom", and tells the reader not to quote marchMs. Floor set at the occupancy
hit rate divided by four, because below that the two sources disagree by more
than the predicate difference could possibly explain.

CANDIDATE ELIMINATED BY READING BEFORE THE PROBE RAN: chunk-key units. MakeKey
copies VoxelCoords::FVoxelLevelChunkKey, which is render-chunk coords,
level-relative, and ChunkEdgeVoxels = 32 -- so WorldVoxel >> 5 is the right
derivation and WantOrigin = ChunkCoord * 32 the right validation. The descriptor
decode, L1 bit order and cell indexing were re-derived field by field against the
format doc and all match. Which is precisely why guessing stopped and a probe
was built.


================================================================================
INDEX PROBE: THE JOIN IS CLEAN. THE BUG IS BELOW IT. (23:39)
================================================================================

  Voxel march index probe: probed=4096 resident=889 | valid=706 (L1any=706 L1zero=0)
    | rejects: origin=0 level=0 anySolidClear=183 slotOOB=0 brickBaseOOB=0

  origin=0          every resident chunk's recorded origin MATCHES what was
                    wanted. No units error, NO TRANSPOSITION, no stale or
                    aliased slot. That kills the candidate I was most confident
                    about -- and kills it with samples rather than by argument.
  level=0           ring level decodes correctly.
  slotOOB=0
  brickBaseOOB=0    the bounds-checks the parameter block was built for never
                    fire, so no corrupt index is reaching the arenas.
  L1any=706 L1zero=0  EVERY valid chunk has bits set in its L1 mask. So the
                    other branch of the pre-registered conclusion is excluded
                    too: it is NOT "the join is clean but every mask is empty".
  anySolidClear=183 of 889 resident -- legitimate, chunks whose anySolid bit is
                    genuinely clear.

The guard added one leg earlier fired and stated the diagnosis itself:
  "drew only 1163 of 21340 tiles (5.4%) with a POPULATED index (16898 entries).
   That is not a cost result, it is a symptom."

  ==> THE INDEX RESOLVES, THE RECORDS AGREE, THE MASKS ARE POPULATED, AND RAYS
      STILL MISS 86% OF WHAT THEY SHOULD HIT. The defect is in the traversal
      below the join: descriptor fetch, occupancy dword indexing inside the
      brick, or the DDA.

WHY THE PROBE WAS THE RIGHT INSTRUMENT AND THE DEPTH GATE WAS NOT. I proposed the
depth gate; the agent overruled it, correctly. The gate would have confirmed "the
traversal disagrees" without saying WHERE, and needs a converged pose. The probe
traces NO RAYS -- it isolates the join from the DDA completely -- and it reported
SAMPLES rather than counts, because a count cannot separate a units bug (factor
of 32) from a transposition (a permutation) from a stale slot (an unrelated
value). Three candidates, one leg, all three eliminated by inspection of six
printed pairs.

THE NEXT SUSPECT, NAMED BY ITS AUTHOR BEFORE THE SYMPTOM APPEARED: source 1
contains a SECOND DDA, a structural mirror of VoxelFluidWalkVoxelLine, written
that way to avoid editing the fluid contract file the solver and CPU mirror
depend on. voxel.March.VerifySource -- the instrument that would make that mirror
safe -- is PRE-REGISTERED BUT NOT IMPLEMENTED. A mirror that was argued-for
rather than checked, sitting directly beneath a symptom that says "the traversal
misses", is where to look.

Z-ALIASING DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED: real, but resident=889 with zero origin
mismatches means it is not implicated in THIS symptom, and fixing it now would
change two things while a diagnosis is live.


================================================================================
SHADOWS: SOLVED. THE POOL WAS BEING LOST IN THE OCTREE DESCENT. (23:45)
================================================================================

  r.Shadow.UseOctreeForCulling 0

  census[window]: cameraGathers=60 shadowGathers=240 (4.00 PER CAMERA GATHER)
    quads/cameraGather: camera=10,899,361  shadow=21,021,619  total=31,920,980
    S=2.929 | poolQuads=46,872,523
  ShadowDepth rows: 6

EXACTLY 4.00 PER CAMERA GATHER -- the pre-registered pass value, stated before
the run, and matching the July code comment's "~4 gathers/frame" from when this
last worked. One per cascade of the sun's four.

TWO CAUSES, IN SERIES, AND BOTH HAD TO GO:
  1. r.ShadowQuality was 0 (stale sg.ShadowQuality=0 in GameUserSettings.ini,
     applied by the SECOND of two boot scalability passes). This shut
     CreateDynamicShadows entirely -- no pass, no light loop, nothing. Fixed by
     a [ConsoleVariables] block in DefaultEngine.ini, which is applied after
     scalability and outranks it.
  2. THE POOL WAS LOST IN THE OCTREE DESCENT. With the gate open, a ShadowDepths
     pass existed but drew 0 primitives. GatherShadowPrimitives tests
     CasterOuterFrustum.IntersectBox against OCTREE NODE bounds
     (ShadowSetup.cpp:5433-5443) and only nodes that pass reach
     FilterPrimitiveForShadows. The pool is ONE primitive with kilometre-scale
     bounds sitting very high in the octree; lost during descent, NO
     PER-PRIMITIVE GATE IS EVER CONSULTED. Which is exactly why all six
     eligibility flags read healthy while nothing cast.

WHY THE ELIGIBILITY PROBE WAS STILL WORTH ITS LEG: it eliminated five of six
per-primitive gates in one run, including gate 6 (a non-opaque terrain material,
which would draw normally in the main view and cast nothing, invisible to every
other instrument). Without that, "all flags healthy" would not have been known,
and the octree hypothesis would have had no reason to be preferred.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT SHADOWS COST, AND WHY IT MOVES THE RAY-MARCHING CASE SHARPLY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  quads submitted per camera gather:  camera 10.9M -> total 31.9M.  S = 2.929
  frame p50:  19.72 ms shadowless  ->  34.72 ms with shadows live  (+76%)

THE RASTER PATH PAYS 2.93x THE GEOMETRY SUBMISSION FOR SHADOWS -- 21.0M extra
quads across four cascade passes over the same pool. A marcher pays ONE secondary
ray per primary hit against a brick pyramid that is already resident and already
being traversed.

EVERY PERFORMANCE NUMBER ON THIS PROJECT WAS MEASURED WITHOUT THIS. The 18.99 ms
A0 frame, the 81% draw share, the 2.2-2.9x marcher projection -- all against a
frame missing 76% more work that the shipping game wants. This does not
invalidate any A/B (both arms lacked shadows equally) but it means the marcher's
case has been argued against a baseline far cheaper than reality, and the gap
WIDENS with the feature on rather than narrowing.

p95 34.72 -> 35.38 is unusually tight against p50 here, and hitches=4740 is very
high; both want a dedicated look before anyone quotes a shadowed frame budget.
Do NOT bank 34.72 ms as "the shadowed frame" yet -- one leg, no replicate, and
r.Shadow.UseOctreeForCulling 0 is itself a change whose own cost is unmeasured
(it replaces an octree walk with a flat walk over Scene->Primitives, cheap in
THIS scene precisely because there are so few primitives).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LEG A VOIDED, AND THE REASON IS A RULE THIS PROJECT ALREADY HAS, INVERTED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

p0-sh-geom-r1 exited after 9 s of an expected 250 s, on a global shader compile
error at boot. Cause: an agent had edited .usf and verified 19/19 permutations
compile, but its matching C++ was deliberately NOT built (my editor was up). So
THE SHADER TREE WAS AHEAD OF THE BUILT BINARY and the two disagreed about
bindings.

The recorded rule is "a half-finished .usf is live input to the next leg, unlike
C++ which is inert until built." THIS IS THE INVERSE AND IT IS EQUALLY FATAL:
a COMPLETE .usf plus an unbuilt C++ change is also a broken leg. The invariant is
not "shaders compile" -- it is "the shader tree and the built binary agree".
BUILD BEFORE THE NEXT LEG WHENEVER AN AGENT REPORTS EDITED-BUT-UNBUILT C++,
even if it also reports the shader tree green.


================================================================================
STORAGE PROBE: THE DDA IS THE DEFECT. THREE LAYERS ELIMINATED IN TWO LEGS. (00:13)
================================================================================

  storage: poolSolidCells=15,225,079 vs occupancySolidVoxels=12,527,771
           (ratio 1.215)
           L1-set bricks decode as air=0 solid=21,975 mixed=11,733
  "the storage holds the same world, read through the marcher's own path.
   The defect is in the DDA, not in the lookup or the decode."

EVERY BRANCH OF THE PRE-REGISTERED TABLE RESOLVES:
  ratio 1.215, POOL LARGER -- the required direction, since m != MAT_AIR is a
    strict superset of the fluid predicate. 21.5% more solid is the right order
    for water plus watermark. Neither "pool ~= occupancy" nor "pool << occupancy".
  air=0 -- the contradiction case NEVER FIRES. Every L1-set brick decodes as
    solid or mixed, so this is not bad DATA.
  origin=0, level=0, slotOOB=0, brickBaseOOB=0, L1zero=0 -- join, slot bounds,
    base bounds and mask population all clean.

  ==> The index resolves, the records agree, the descriptors decode, and the
      occupancy dwords read back the RIGHT WORLD THROUGH THE MARCHER'S OWN PATH
      -- and rays still miss 86% of what they should hit. The remaining surface
      is the walk itself.

THE METHOD IS THE TRANSFERABLE PART. Two legs, three layers eliminated, no
guessing:
  leg 1  probe the JOIN ONLY, tracing no rays -- isolates lookup from traversal.
         Reported SAMPLES not counts, because a count cannot separate a units bug
         (factor of 32) from a transposition (a permutation) from a stale slot
         (an unrelated value).
  leg 2  count solid cells THROUGH THE MARCHER'S OWN READ PATH against the
         reference over the same region, with a direction the physics forces
         (superset => pool must be larger) and a contradiction case that cannot
         occur in valid data (L1 set + descriptor says air).
Both logs STATE THEIR OWN CONCLUSION rather than leaving it to be inferred, so
there was nothing for me to misread. Compare the four times today I misread an
unsettled or superseded value.

I PROPOSED THE DEPTH GATE AT THIS POINT AND WAS WRONG TWICE OVER: it would have
confirmed "the traversal disagrees" without localising, and it needs a converged
pose. Cheap targeted probes beat the strong general instrument when the question
is WHERE rather than WHETHER.

NEXT, AND THE CONDITION FOR IT WAS SET IN ADVANCE: voxel.March.VerifySource.
Its author said it becomes worth its cost exactly when it would localise WITHIN
the walk rather than confirm the walk is involved. That condition is now met.

THE PRIME SUSPECT, FLAGGED BY ITS AUTHOR BEFORE ANY SYMPTOM EXISTED: source 1
contains a structural mirror of VoxelFluidWalkVoxelLine, written that way
because its solidity test is hardcoded and adding a hook would mean editing the
fluid contract file the solver and CPU mirror depend on. Sound at the time -- but
it means the marcher's walk and the walk that has been in production for months
are TWO IMPLEMENTATIONS OF ONE ALGORITHM, AND ONLY ONE HAS EVER BEEN VERIFIED.


================================================================================
THE NEW BASELINE: SHADOWS COST +83.8% AND 30x THE HITCHES (00:30)
================================================================================

Four legs, same pose, same build, two replicates each side:

  arm                    p50      p95    hitches   shadowGathers
  p0-sh-quality-ctl    18.85    21.32       165         0
  p0-sh-quality-r1     18.92    21.35       146         0
  p0-sh-nooctree-r1    34.72    35.38      4740    28,556
  g1-gioff-r1          34.71    35.55      4756    28,796

  WITHIN-ARM SPREAD: 0.07 ms shadowless, 0.01 ms shadowed. Both far under the
  0.18 ms within-config spread this rig was calibrated at, so the between-arm
  difference is real by a wide margin.

  SHADOWS COST +15.83 ms AT p50 -- 18.89 -> 34.72, +83.8%.
  HITCHES GO 155 -> 4,748. THIRTY TIMES.

The p50 number was predicted in shape by the plan ("up to four more cascade
passes over the whole pool") and is now measured. THE HITCH FIGURE WAS NOT
PREDICTED BY ANYONE and is the more alarming half: a 30x hitch increase is a
playability problem, not a throughput one, and nothing in the plan anticipated
it. It needs its own diagnosis before any shadowed frame budget is quoted.

WHY THIS MOVES THE RAY-MARCHING CASE SHARPLY. Shadow gathers submit 21.0M extra
quads on top of the camera's 10.9M (S = 2.929). A marcher pays ONE secondary ray
per primary hit against a pyramid that is already resident and already being
traversed. So the feature the owner asked for ASAP is the feature that most
widens the gap -- and every number this project has produced was measured with it
broken.

  RESTATED HONESTLY: the marcher has been argued against an 18.99 ms frame.
  The shipping frame with the features the owner wants is 34.72 ms. No A/B is
  invalidated -- both arms of every pair lacked shadows equally -- but the
  ABSOLUTE case is now measured against the wrong baseline in the project's own
  favour, and should be restated once the marcher works.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
AND AN ACCIDENTAL GI RESULT, WHICH LANDS IN A PRE-REGISTERED BRANCH
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

p0-sh-nooctree-r1 ran at the GI default (voxel.GI.Enabled 1). g1-gioff-r1 ran
-VoxelGIOff and printed `GI arm: HELD -- voxel.GI.Enabled=0 verified on the first
tick`. Everything else identical.

  GI ON   34.72 ms   hitches 4740
  GI OFF  34.71 ms   hitches 4756

  G0 - G1 = 0.01 ms, against a within-arm spread of 0.01-0.07 ms.

That is the pre-registered branch: "G0 - G1 < 2x the G0 spread -> GI is already
effectively free on the pooled path, Phase 7 is a CORRECTNESS AND COVERAGE
project, not a performance one, and the p95 framing gets struck." The
+14.9% p95 / x3.2 hitches that has governed this feature for a month describes a
renderer nobody ships.

  CAVEAT THAT KEEPS THIS PRELIMINARY, NOT FINAL: p0-sh-nooctree-r1 did not carry
  the arm switch, so it has no `HELD` confirmation. GI was at its default, which
  IS 1, but the instrument built specifically to prove that was not engaged --
  and "the cvar's default says so" is exactly the standard of evidence that
  failed on r.ShadowQuality tonight. A verified G0 is running.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
RETRACTION: THE "30x HITCH INCREASE" IS NOT A FINDING. IT IS THE p50, RECOUNTED.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I wrote that hitches going 155 -> 4,748 was "the more alarming half", "predicted
by nobody", and "a playability problem, not a throughput one". WRONG on all three.

VoxelDebug.h:419  inline constexpr float kHitchThresholdMs = 33.3f;

It is a FIXED threshold -- 30 fps -- not a relative or variance-based one. So:
  shadowless  p50 18.85, threshold 33.3  -> almost nothing crosses ->   165 / 11,603 = 1.4%
  shadowed    p50 34.72, threshold 33.3  -> almost everything crosses -> 4,740 /  6,742 = 70.3%

A frame at the median is already over the line. The counter is reporting "the
frame is now slower than 30 fps", WHICH THE p50 ALREADY SAID. There is no second
phenomenon and no stutter finding.

THE EVIDENCE WAS IN THE NUMBERS I HAD ALREADY QUOTED: p50 34.72 against p95
35.38 is a 0.66 ms spread. A DISTRIBUTION THAT TIGHT CANNOT HAVE 70% OUTLIERS.
I quoted the tightness and the hitch count in the same breath and did not put
them together -- and I had already flagged p95 as untrustworthy on this rig
earlier in the day, which should have made me read any tail metric twice.

  THE RULE: BEFORE CALLING A COUNTER ALARMING, FIND OUT WHAT IT COUNTS. A
  fixed-threshold counter is a step function of the median, not a measure of
  variance, and it will always look catastrophic when the median crosses it.
  This is the same class as the TOTAL line that summed per-chunk means, and the
  same class as reading a cvar from its first log line: the number was real, the
  meaning was assumed.

CAUGHT BEFORE IT COST ANYTHING -- I was one step from spawning an agent to
diagnose a stutter that does not exist. The check that caught it was arithmetic
on data already in hand: 4,748 hitches against 6,742 frames is 70%, and 70%
outliers is not a tail, so "hitch" could not mean what I assumed.

WHAT STANDS UNCHANGED: shadows cost +15.83 ms at p50 (18.89 -> 34.72, +83.8%),
two replicates per arm, spreads of 0.07 and 0.01 ms. That number is unaffected
and is the one that matters.


================================================================================
P3-B1 RESOLVED. THE MARCHER WAS NEVER THE BUG. AND NOW THERE IS A REAL NUMBER.
================================================================================

                       drawn    marchMs   indexEntries
  occupancy (control)   8,016     5.144       16,891
  brickpool (before)    1,138     6.202       16,898
  brickpool (AFTER)     8,015     6.087       26,215

  drawn 8,015 against the control's 8,016 -- WITHIN ONE TILE.
  indexEntries +9,317, which matches the measured all-solid skip count almost
  exactly (8,748 + 571 = 9,319 from the two windows I grepped).

THE 86% MISS RATE WAS ENTIRELY THE PRE-DISPATCH SKIPS. Not the DDA, not the
join, not the decode, not the second unverified walk. Four sites dropped
provably-all-solid chunks before they could ever be packed -- correct for a
mesher (no quads either way), catastrophic for a marcher (you see through rock).

  THE DIAGNOSIS SEQUENCE, WHICH IS THE PART THAT GENERALISES:
    leg 1  probe the JOIN, tracing no rays          -> join clean
    leg 2  count solids THROUGH THE MARCHER'S PATH  -> storage clean, "the DDA"
    then   a THIRD party reading unrelated code     -> the set was incomplete
  Legs 1 and 2 were correct and their conclusion was still wrong, because BOTH
  INSPECT WHAT IS PRESENT AND NEITHER CAN SEE WHAT IS ABSENT. The instrument
  that would have caught it did not exist: coverage was measured against
  "chunks that got a mesh job", and a pre-dispatch skip is not in that
  denominator by construction.

  I was one message from approving a rewrite of a DDA that was working.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
AND NOW THE NUMBER THE PROJECT HAS BEEN WORKING TOWARD
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Both arms now hit the same tiles (8,016 vs 8,015), share the frame, the clip box
and the ray by construction, and run the same budget at the same pose. So the
difference is the cost of the indirections and nothing else:

  occupancy  5.144 ms   -- 1 bit per voxel, one flat toroidal grid, 3 int ops
  brick pool 6.087 ms   -- chunk index -> record -> L1 mask -> descriptor ->
                           occupancy dwords -> palette

  ==> +0.943 ms, +18.3%, FOR FIVE INDIRECTIONS.

That is the input every downstream projection needed and did not have. What the
18.3% buys: the six-ring cascade instead of a 51.2 m window, a mip pyramid to
skip empty space over, and per-voxel material -- none of which the occupancy
volume can represent at any price.

CAVEATS THAT TRAVEL WITH IT:
  - Level 0 only. No cone LOD, no ring transitions, no mip pyramid, and one
    occupancy dword loaded per voxel rather than 16 held in registers. The
    hierarchical form is P3-B2 and will move this number in both directions --
    more indirection, far fewer steps.
  - The old cost model (0.13 + steps x 5.9 us) stays DEAD. This measures a
    different structure.
  - The 9.19x skip ratio stays UNTRANSFERRED. It was measured against a
    two-level mip over the flat volume and must be re-measured here.
  - Single leg per arm. The rig's within-config spread is 0.18 ms and this delta
    is 0.94, so it clears it 5x -- but it is one leg.

THE SKIP FIX VERIFIED ITSELF, and the counters were built to be falsifiable from
both sides: volumeFed=1 in 162 windows with chunk-level solid=0 in every one,
which is exactly the pre-registered pass condition. No tripwire Error fired.
(The large `solid=367379` values in the same log are `brickSkip air=... solid=...`
-- the mesher's PER-BRICK skip, an unrelated counter that happens to share a
field name. Checked rather than assumed.)

Automation 15/15 after declaring the new first-eviction Error in the three tests
that deliberately exercise eviction -- THE SECOND TIME TODAY a production Error
log added for good reasons turned green tests red. That is now a known
consequence: adding a loud production log to a tested path costs an
AddExpectedError in every test that exercises it.

  source = BRICK POOL (p3b1-depthgate-src1-r1), two reads 60 s apart:

              max 0.964 vox, ctlMax 0.962

  ==> The marcher reading the REAL BRICK VOLUME is verified correct to the same
      standard as the marcher reading the purpose-built stand-in. P3-B1 is
      complete on correctness as well as on cost.

Interior <2, <4 and >=4 buckets are all zero on every read of both sources: not
one interior pixel, in any run, disagrees by as much as a single voxel.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT SAY, and the instrument says it itself: "not proof". The
control is an UPPER bound -- one pixel is coarser than the sub-pixel difference
that actually separates the two renderers -- so below it means "consistent with
sampling noise", never "identical". The asymmetry is deliberate and is the same
shape as the spike's restartNoise control.

THIS IS THE LAST GATE RUN COMPARABLE TO THE EARLIER SERIES. B-2a extends the
clip box from 51.2 m to R0's 128 m, at which point `miss` collapses and the
judged population changes character. Recorded before the change, not after:
NOBODY SHOULD COMPARE GATE NUMBERS ACROSS THAT LINE.
