================================================================================
B-2a: A 66.9x SKIP RATIO, AND THE GATE REFUSED IT (01:30)
================================================================================

  SKIP RATIO: stepsFlat=474,657,891  stepsHier=7,099,448  ->  66.86x
              second read 66.91x
  hierVsFlat disagree=156,031  against control refNoise=6 (second read 14)

  LogVoxelMarch: Error: THE RATIO IS NOT USABLE: the hierarchical walk disagrees
  with the flat walk on 156031 rays against a noise floor of 6...

156,031 of 1,354,896 rays = 11.5%, and 26,005x the measured floor.
THE RATIO IS NOT QUOTABLE AND IS NOT QUOTED.

WHAT THIS INSTRUMENT JUST PREVENTED. 66.9x against a plan that assumes 9.19x
would have been the headline of the night -- a seven-fold improvement on the
project's most load-bearing number, arriving exactly when it was wanted. It came
with an 11.5% disagreement rate, and the gate that caught it was in THE SAME
DISPATCH, over THE SAME RAYS, against a control drawn from THE SAME POPULATION.

  The failure mode was named in advance by the person who built the gate: "a
  hierarchy that skips a diagonal cell produces a LOWER step count and a
  plausible picture -- it makes this number BETTER while being wrong."

  A GATE THAT ONLY FIRES WHEN RESULTS LOOK BAD IS NO PROTECTION AGAINST THE
  FAILURE THAT MATTERS. This one fired on a result that looked spectacular, on
  its first run, and printed its own refusal at Error severity so it could not
  be read past.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
AND THE SAME LINE CARRIES A CLEAN PASS THAT MUST NOT GET LOST
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  Voxel march source compare: rays=1,354,896 | agree miss=1,136,039 hit=218,857
    | ties=0 | DISAGREE brickMissed=0 brickExtra=0 hard=0 | excluded watermark=0

ZERO disagreements between the BRICK source and the OCCUPANCY source, over 1.35M
rays, on the same rays in the same frame. Not "within noise" -- ZERO. That is
stronger than the depth gate could state, and it retires the last doubt about the
source swap. The MAT_WATERMARK exclusion counted zero as well, so it papered over
nothing.

  ==> THE WALK IS CORRECT. THE HIERARCHY ON TOP OF IT IS NOT.
      That is a far better position than the reverse.

WHY 11.5% CANNOT BE THE PREDICTED FLOAT NOISE: the author predicted disagreement
on rays crossing two planes simultaneously, because a restarted child DDA's fresh
tMax differs from an accumulated one by a few ULP. The control measured that
population at 6 and 14 rays. 156,031 is structural, not arithmetic.

ARM C CANCELLED until B passes -- measuring the ratio at 128 m while the
hierarchy disagrees at 51.2 m would produce a bigger unusable number. The
ordering that saved the leg was pre-registered by its author: "B before C: if the
hierarchy disagrees with the flat walk, C's number is meaningless."

Arm A for the record: marchMs=6.194 at skip 0 on the brick source, consistent
with the 6.087 baseline.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE 66.9x WAS MEASURING A GRID OFFSET, NOT A HIERARCHY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ROOT CAUSE: UVoxelFluidSubsystem::DesiredOriginVoxel returns CamVoxel - 256 for
an ARBITRARY camera voxel, so MarchBrickOriginVoxel is 32-aligned only by luck.
VoxelMarchCellExitT computed cell boundaries as floor(P / CellUU) * CellUU IN THE
LOCAL FRAME -- which silently assumes the frame origin is a multiple of the cell
size. It is not. Every chunk and brick boundary in the hierarchy sat
`origin mod 32` voxels from the real one, SO THE WALK TESTED THE L1 BIT OF ONE
BRICK WHILE THE RAY WAS INSIDE ITS NEIGHBOUR.

That is the entire symptom in one sentence: it skips real content, and because
skipping removes steps, IT PRODUCES A BETTER RATIO AND A PLAUSIBLE PICTURE.

FIXED by deriving the boundary from the WORLD voxel rather than the local
position -- removing the assumption instead of documenting it. Written as an
arithmetic shift because `>> n` floors for negatives and `/ (1 << n)` does not.
The uniform-SOLID entry-face computation carried the identical assumption and is
fixed the same way.

TWO REASONS THIS COST A LEG RATHER THAN A REBUILD, both worth keeping:
  1. THE FLAT WALK IS IMMUNE. It steps voxel by voxel and never touches a cell
     boundary, so only the hierarchy was wrong -- which is exactly why
     brickMissed=0 and hierVsFlat=156,031 appear in the SAME LINE. The control
     could not have caught it because the control does not do the thing that
     breaks.
  2. THE LONG-REACH ARM WOULD HAVE MASKED IT. voxel.March.ReachM snaps its origin
     DOWN TO A CHUNK BOUNDARY, so arm C at 128 m would have looked clean while
     the bug persisted at 51.2 m. CANCELLING ARM C FIRST WAS LUCKY AS WELL AS
     CORRECT -- had it run, we would have had a "passing" long-range ratio and a
     failing short-range one, and would have concluded the short arm was faulty.

THE RE-RUN CANNOT NEED ANOTHER ROUND OF GUESSING: hierVsFlat is now split by
DIRECTION, because a total cannot say which way --
  lost   flat hit, hierarchy missed  -> skipping real content (expect 0)
  gained flat missed, hierarchy hit  -> would indicate a FLAT-walk bug
  moved  both hit, different voxel   -> mis-anchoring, stopping on the wrong brick
If the fix is complete all three read zero. If `moved` survives alone, that is
the predicted ULP-restart floor and should land near the control's 6, not 156,031.


================================================================================
PHASE 5 RETIREMENT: THE SELF-CHECK FIRED, AND FOUND TWO DEFECTS (01:50)
================================================================================

  RetireQuads 1:  added 30,113 (gpu 0,     cpu 30,113)   55.3 MiB
                  terrainPoolUsedQuads=74,583
                  Error: Voxel Phase 5 VIOLATION
  RetireQuads 0:  added 97,019 (gpu 6,120, cpu 90,899)  172.6 MiB

DEFECT 1 -- 74,583 QUADS STILL ALLOCATED UNDER RETIREMENT. Traced by elimination
to DrainGameThreadMesh: four terrain-pool allocation sites, two ruled out by
early-returns, the two reachable ones both requiring a CPU quad producer, and
only one such producer is unsuppressed.

  THE ERROR IS A CARRIED-FORWARD CONSTRAINT, WHICH IS ITS OWN FAILURE CLASS.
  SuppressQuadMesh was deliberately worker-path-only, with the comment
  "suppressing the game-thread edit re-mesh would break editing for no timing
  gain". TRUE FOR A STOPWATCH ARM. FALSE FOR RETIREMENT -- under retirement the
  marcher IS the renderer, so an edited chunk must stop producing quads and rely
  on the brick pack, which that same path already produces. The exemption was
  inherited into a new context without re-asking whether it still held.

DEFECT 2 -- THE FORK STOPS PACKING ENTIRELY. gpu 0 against gpu 6,120 in the
control, same pose, same pair. Not an unconverged leg.

  30,113 is consistent with CPU-only production through DrainResults' apply
  budget (MaxApplies 3/frame ~ 180/s ~ 167 s of run). The fork normally
  publishes OUTSIDE that budget, so losing it costs both its own chunks and the
  rate.

  THE DISCRIMINATOR THAT SETTLED IT WAS ALREADY IN THE OUTPUT. brickPacks=30113
  exactly equalling 30,113 chunks resident LOOKS conclusive and is not -- a chunk
  packed by both arms is one resident and two counted, so the totals can
  coincide. The (gpu, cpu) split cannot. Distrusting the coincidence and naming
  the reading that could not coincide is what turned one leg into an answer.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY THE GATE'S ZERO THRESHOLD IS THE WHOLE POINT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

74,583 quads against a 46.8M baseline is a 0.16% LEAK. ANY THRESHOLD ABOVE ZERO
WOULD HAVE CALLED THIS RETIRED. We would have quoted a VRAM win, shipped a switch
that leaves an edit path producing terrain nobody looks at, and NEVER SEEN THAT
THE FORK HAD STOPPED FEEDING THE VOLUME -- because defect 2 was only visible in a
counter nobody would have read if defect 1 had not fired.

And the number was only readable at all because the gate used GetUsedQuads()
rather than GetNumQuads(), which returns CAPACITY. Built on the obvious accessor
it would have read 1,000,000 under both arms and meant nothing. That trap has now
paid for itself twice.

VRAM IS THEREFORE NOT YET QUOTABLE. The 2,197 MB -> 12 MB figure requires a clean
retirement, and this one is 0.16% leaky and missing two thirds of its residency.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
RATIO RE-RUN: THE FIX LANDED ON ITS AXIS, THE SPLIT MOVED ELSEWHERE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  SKIP RATIO: stepsFlat=474,688,160  stepsHier=11,072,915  ->  42.87x
  hierVsFlat disagree=115,133 (control refNoise=462)
    by shape: lost=3,688  gained=47,092  moved=64,353
  source compare: brickMissed=0 brickExtra=0 hard=0 ties=0 watermark=0

`lost` collapsed from the bulk of 156,031 to 3,688 -- the alignment fix landing
on exactly the axis it targeted. The ratio fell 66.9x -> 42.87x as the walk
stopped OVER-skipping, which is the direction that makes sense. Still refused.

THE PRE-REGISTERED READING OF `gained` IS PROBABLY WRONG, AND SAYING SO MATTERS
BECAUSE IT IS THE READING THAT WOULD LET THE NUMBER BE QUOTED. `gained` (flat
missed, hierarchy hit) was registered as indicating a FLAT-walk defect. But the
same line reports brickMissed=0 brickExtra=0 against the occupancy source over
1.35M rays, and BOTH those arms use the flat walk. For `gained` to be a flat-walk
bug, the flat walk and an independently written volume traversal would have to
miss the same 47,092 hits identically.

The likelier mechanism: THE HIERARCHY STOPS ON A BRICK WHOSE L1 BIT IS SET WHILE
THE VOXEL IT REPORTS IS AIR -- a failure to DESCEND, not a failure to skip. That
produces a hit where a correct walk reports a miss, and the same mechanism with a
different landing voxel would explain a large `moved` too.

  NOTE THE ASYMMETRY IN WHAT THE TWO READINGS PERMIT: "the control is wrong"
  would let 42.87x be quoted. "The hierarchy does not descend" would not. When
  one interpretation unlocks the number you want, it deserves more scrutiny than
  the one that does not, not less.

UNEXPLAINED AND FLAGGED RATHER THAN ABSORBED: control refNoise went 6 -> 462
between two runs of the SAME instrument at the SAME pose. A 77x jump in the
measured floor is not nothing. If the alignment change altered what the noise
control walks, then the floor and the measurement moved together and the ratio
between them means less than it appears to. Do not treat the floor as trustworthy
until that is explained.

STILL SETTLED, NOW TWICE, ON 1.35M RAYS EACH TIME: brickMissed=0 brickExtra=0
hard=0. Whatever is wrong lives in the hierarchy, not in the brick volume nor in
the walk that reads it.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
RATIO r3: THE SKIPPING AXIS IS SOLVED. TWO OTHERS HALVED AND PERSIST.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

          lost      gained    moved     total     refNoise
  r1     ~152k*        --        --    156,031        6     (unsplit)
  r2      3,688     47,092    64,353   115,133      462
  r3         32     20,233    37,374    57,639      0 / 2

  SKIP RATIO 42.94x -- STILL REFUSED.

`lost` 156k -> 3,688 -> 32. The frame-alignment fix and the brick-exit-bound fix
between them have eliminated the skipping-real-content axis.

SECOND BUG FOUND BY THE SPLIT: the fine walk was bounded by the NUDGED brick exit
rather than the true one, so `Span` was one nudge longer than the brick and the
walk took ONE STEP PAST IT, testing that voxel against THIS brick's occupancy
dwords. VoxelMarchCellIndex masks coordinates to 0..7, so an out-of-brick voxel
SILENTLY ALIASES ONTO AN IN-BRICK BIT. Not wrong bits -- the right brick's bits
read for the wrong voxel, which produces hits in air (`gained`) and hits at the
wrong voxel (`moved`), exactly the observed pair.

  Fixed by splitting two quantities that were conflated: one value bounds the
  child walk, another advances t. Halving both terms rather than collapsing them
  says this was ONE OF TWO contributors sharing a signature.

MY MECHANISM GUESS WAS RIGHT IN SHAPE AND WRONG IN DETAIL, and the agent's
pre-registered reading of `gained` as "a flat-walk defect" was overturned from
evidence on the same line: brickMissed=0 brickExtra=0 means the flat walk agrees
exactly with an independently written traversal, so a flat-walk bug would require
both to fail identically. A PRE-REGISTRATION IS ONLY WORTH SOMETHING IF EVIDENCE
CAN OVERTURN IT.

  AND NOTE WHICH READING WAS THE DANGEROUS ONE: "the control is wrong" would have
  unlocked a quotable 42.9x. "The hierarchy does not descend correctly" would
  not. When one interpretation unlocks the number you want, it earns MORE
  scrutiny, not less.

THE NOISE CONTROL WAS ITSELF REPLACED, AND THE OLD ONE WAS STRUCTURALLY WEAK.
Scaling the segment by 1-1e-7 perturbs only rays whose hit lies near the FAR END;
every other ray walks byte-identical arithmetic and CANNOT disagree. So the floor
sampled a thin far-field population whose size is pure geometry and can swing 77x
between scenes with nothing wrong -- which is what 6 -> 462 was. Worse, it did not
measure what the verdict is exposed to: the hierarchy restarts tMax at cell
boundaries, changing the arithmetic history of the WHOLE walk, and a far-end
perturbation is not the matching control for that.
Replaced with an ENTRY nudge of 1e-6 UU so floor() and every subsequent tMax
accumulation differ along the entire ray. New floor: 0 and 2.
  6 -> 462 -> 0 IS NOT A TREND. The first two came from a different instrument
  and are not comparable to the third. Only the new number is a floor.

42.94x IS STABLE ACROSS r2 AND r3 (42.87 -> 42.94) AND THAT IS NOT REASSURING:
both runs share the same defect, so agreeing with each other is exactly what two
runs of one bug do. Stability is not correctness.

FOURTH CLEAN SOURCE COMPARE, 1.35M rays each time: brickMissed=0 brickExtra=0
hard=0 ties=0 watermark=0.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PHASE 5: THE LEAK IS CLOSED, AND IT WAS NEVER A PRODUCER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  p5-retire-cmdline-r1, retirement via COMMAND LINE rather than -ExecCmds:
    terrainPoolUsedQuads=0  (capacity 0)  residentQuads=0
    brickPacks=28,123  brickFromGpu=0  forkDispatched=0
    added 28,123 (gpu 0, cpu 28,123)   51.2 MiB

THE 74,583 IS GONE, AND THE CAUSE WAS THE SWITCH ARRIVING LATE, NOT A PRODUCER
IGNORING IT. -ExecCmds lands AFTER streaming has begun -- documented in three
separate places in this codebase, each of which took a command-line switch for
exactly this reason. Chunks meshed before the flip allocate pool ranges normally
and NOTHING FREES THEM AFTERWARDS, so the residue is a fixed function of how far
streaming got before the cvar landed: deterministic at a fixed spawn on one
binary. THAT is why 74,583 was byte-identical across two 330 s legs rather than
merely similar, and the exactness was the clue.

  Note `capacity 0`: with retirement active from frame 0 the terrain pool is
  never CREATED. Cleaner than "created and left empty".

  THE FIRST FIX AIMED AT DrainGameThreadMesh WAS AIMED AT NOTHING. The
  elimination that produced it was sound -- four allocation sites, two ruled out
  by early-returns, one unsuppressed CPU producer remaining -- and still reached
  the wrong answer, because every branch of it assumed the leak was produced
  DURING the retired period. It was produced before retirement existed.

  GENERALISES: an elimination over "which producer is doing this" cannot find a
  cause of the form "it was already done before the question applied". Ask when,
  not only who.

BUT THE SAME LEG SHOWS forkDispatched=0 -- not dispatched-and-rejected, which was
the earlier defect, but never run at all -- and residency 28,123 against the
control's 97,019 with gpu 0. So the fork contribution is still missing and the
CPU arm is producing at roughly the apply-budget rate.

  forkDispatched= was added THIS ROUND so the split would sit on the Phase 5 line
  rather than needing a second command, and it earned itself immediately: without
  it this leg reads as "leak closed, ship it" with residency quietly at a third
  of the control.

VRAM IS STILL NOT QUOTABLE. 2,197 MB -> 12 MB is not claimable while the volume
holds a third of what it should. A retirement that drops two thirds of residency
is not a win, it is a different bug.

  THE GATE HAS NOW CAUGHT THREE DEFECTS AND REFRAMED A FOURTH, and every one was
  invisible to the metric that preceded it.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
RATIO r4: INTEGER CONTAINMENT. 57,639 -> 618, AND `gained` 20,233 -> 3.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

          lost      gained    moved     total    ratio
  r1     ~152k*        --        --    156,031   66.86
  r2      3,688     47,092    64,353   115,133   42.87
  r3         32     20,233    37,374    57,639   42.94
  r4        464          3       151       618   39.79

THE SECOND MECHANISM: `Span` is a slab test from the brick's min corner; tMax is
accumulated from the segment entry. TWO DIFFERENT FLOAT COMPUTATIONS OF THE SAME
GEOMETRIC QUANTITY, differing by ULP in an UNBIASED direction once per brick
crossing. Heads it stops correctly; tails it steps into the next brick and tests
that voxel against THIS brick's occupancy dwords, which the cell index masks to
0..7 so it silently aliases onto an in-brick bit.

  That is why the first fix HALVED both terms instead of collapsing them: fix one
  removed a SYSTEMATIC bias and left a COIN FLIP behind it.

FIXED by asking containment of the integer instead: the brick a voxel belongs to
is `voxel >> 3`, exactly, three integer compares that cannot be half-right. The
float bound stays as a cheap early-out; the integer test makes it correct.

  THE ERROR CLASS, NAMED BY THE PERSON WHO MADE IT TWICE IN ONE FUNCTION:
  CONTAINMENT IS AN INTEGER PROPERTY AND I DECIDED IT IN FLOAT TWICE -- once via
  the nudge, once via ULP.

THE RATIO HAS FALLEN MONOTONICALLY AS THE WALK GOT MORE CORRECT: 66.86 -> 42.87
-> 42.94 -> 39.79. EVERY FIX SO FAR HAS MADE THE HEADLINE NUMBER SMALLER. A
sequence of fixes that kept improving it would have been the thing to worry about.

STILL REFUSED at 618 against a floor of 0. 0.046% of rays, ratio stable -- which
is exactly the state in which "close enough" becomes attractive. 618 IS NOT A
FLOOR, IT IS A SMALLER BUG.

AND THE REMAINING TERM INVERTED: `lost` went 32 -> 464, up 14x, while everything
else collapsed. Either the tightened walk exposed a genuine skip-too-far
previously masked by aliasing noise, or the fix introduced an UNDER-run -- one
cell short rather than one cell long, the mirror of what was just fixed twice in
that function. Not yet distinguished; the per-ray dump exists for exactly this.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PHASE 5, DEFECTS 3 AND 4: BOTH WERE MINE-ADJACENT AND BOTH WERE COUNTERS LYING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

DEFECT 3 -- THE TWO ARMS REALLY DID DIFFER. `-VoxelBrickPack=1` is read only by
the ACCESSOR, but `Submit` read the RAW CVAR. On the command-line arm the
accessors said "retired" (so bMeshChain=false) while the raw cvar said "no brick
pack" (so bBrickPack=false), and every job asked for NEITHER quads nor bricks --
at which point the promotion guard added the round before correctly rejected all
of them. Introduced while fixing the previous defect.

DEFECT 4 -- forkDispatched=0 WAS AN ARTIFACT. The counter is reset at :7138 and
the Phase 5 block reads it at :7395, so IT PRINTED 0 ON EVERY WINDOW REGARDLESS
OF THE TRUTH, and the tripwire built on it (dispatched > 0 && brickFromGpu == 0)
was STRUCTURALLY INCAPABLE OF FIRING. A comment three rounds old claimed that
guard "can fire". It never could.

  SO BOTH READINGS I OFFERED FOR forkDispatched=0 WERE WRONG IN THE SAME WAY --
  neither "gated off" nor "not seeded": the counter was lying. I reasoned about
  a mechanism from a number that had no content.

FOUR FOR FOUR THIS ROUND: every defect was a guard or counter that was CORRECT
WHEN WRITTEN and silently outlived its invariant -- the promotion precondition,
the -ExecCmds window, the raw cvar reads, the reset-order tripwire. NONE was a
wrong line. The gate demanding exact zero is the only reason any surfaced.

EARLIER PAIR CONFIRMED SOUND: p5-retire-on-r2 vs p5-retire-off-r1 both used
-ExecCmds with the raw cvar set, so both had bBrickPack true. The divergence
existed only on the command-line arm and only from this round. The 2.49x
per-chunk figure and the r2 recovery both stand.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PHASE 5 RETIREMENT IS NOW CLEAN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  p5-cmdline-on-r2  (RetireQuads=1): terrainPoolUsedQuads=0, residentQuads=0
                                     added 97,471 (gpu 6,676, cpu 90,795)  173.2 MiB
  p5-cmdline-off-r2 (RetireQuads=0): added 97,471 (gpu 8,092, cpu 89,379)  173.2 MiB

RESIDENCY AT EXACT PARITY -- 97,471 both arms -- WITH ZERO TERRAIN QUADS
ALLOCATED UNDER RETIREMENT. Both earlier defects (the 74,583 leak and the fork
producing nothing) are closed, and the volume is unaffected by retirement, which
is the property that had to hold.

MY OWN ERROR ON THIS PAIR, CAUGHT BEFORE QUOTING: I passed
-VoxelPoolCapacityQuads=1000000 to BOTH arms, so the control's quad pool was
clamped to 12 MB too. `residentQuads=1000000` on the OFF arm is the clamp, not
the world. THAT PAIR THEREFORE CANNOT PRODUCE THE VRAM NUMBER -- it compares
12 MB against 12 MB. A control must differ from the arm in ONE thing, and I gave
this one the arm's memory configuration.

  The number I wanted to quote -- 2,197 MB -> 12 MB -- requires the control to
  run at the SHIPPING pool capacity. Re-running as p5-vram-control-r1 without the
  clamp.

NOT AN ARTIFACT THIS TIME BUT WORTH WATCHING: forkDispatched still prints 0 while
brickFromGpu=6,676, so the fork is demonstrably working and the counter is still
not reporting it. The reset-order fix may not have covered this call site.

THE VRAM NUMBER, MEASURED. 2,197 MB -> 388 MB COMMITTED / 173.2 MB RESIDENT.

  CONTROL (RetireQuads=0, shipping pool capacity, p5-vram-control-r1):
    VoxelGpuPool: created persistent pool buffers, capacity 192,000,000 quads
      192M x 8 B quads          = 1,465 MiB
      192M x 4 B chunk ids      =   732 MiB
      TOTAL COMMITTED           = 2,197 MiB
    (plus a paged CPU shadow observed climbing to 483 MB in this leg)

  RETIRED (RetireQuads=1, p5-cmdline-on-r2):
    terrainPoolUsedQuads=0, residentQuads=0, pool CAPACITY 0 -- the terrain quad
    pool is NEVER CREATED.
    Brick pool: 388.0 MiB committed, 173.2 MiB resident at 97,471 chunks.

  ==> 2,197 MiB -> 388 MiB COMMITTED (5.66x less)
      2,197 MiB -> 173.2 MiB ACTUALLY RESIDENT (12.7x less)

AND THE VOLUME HOLDS THE SAME WORLD: residency is 97,471 chunks on BOTH arms,
identical to the chunk, with zero terrain quads allocated under retirement. That
parity is what makes the comparison a memory saving rather than a coverage loss
-- the earlier 30,113-chunk run looked like a 4x VRAM win and was a bug.

CAVEATS THAT TRAVEL WITH IT:
  - The 2,197 MiB is COMMITTED capacity, not peak usage. The shipping default is
    what the game allocates, and VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp:11650 records that the
    previous 80M default COULD NOT DRAW THE WORLD -- 28,205 chunks refused -- so
    the capacity is not slack, it is the working requirement.
  - The 388 MiB brick commit is likewise capacity; 173.2 MiB is what this
    cascade actually occupies, and the arenas were sized with deliberate
    headroom (occ 62%, mat 28%, desc 67% used).
  - Single leg per arm. The two arms agreeing to the chunk on residency is
    corroboration, but neither number is replicated.
  - Retirement still requires the marcher to be able to draw the world, which it
    cannot yet -- no hierarchical skipping past 51.2 m, no ring transitions. THIS
    IS A MEASURED PROPERTY OF A SWITCH THAT CANNOT SHIP YET.

MY ERROR ON THE FIRST ATTEMPT, WORTH KEEPING: I passed
-VoxelPoolCapacityQuads=1000000 to BOTH arms and the "control" was clamped to
12 MB alongside the arm -- a comparison of 12 MB against 12 MB that would have
read as "no saving". A control must differ from the arm in ONE thing, and I had
given it the arm's memory configuration.

r6: THE DUMP ANSWERS IT -- AND EXPOSES THAT I HAVE BEEN READING NOISE AS CAUSE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  SKIP RATIO 37.57x | disagree=202 | lost=23 gained=8 moved=171

  lost px(1523,116): flat voxel (-614144,-614171,21966) brick (-76768,-76772,2745)
    | hier term=segment-end steps=28 lastChunk (-19192,-19193,686) lastBrick=16

EVERY SAMPLED LOST RAY: term=segment-end, probe=POOL-SAYS-SOLID. By the
instrument's own pre-registered table that is unambiguous -- the hierarchy walked
the ENTIRE ray past a voxel the pool calls solid. Not budget exhaustion, not a

where the flat walk needs hundreds says it is skipping at a coarse level and
never descending.

THE METHOD ERROR, WHICH IS MINE AND MATTERS MORE:

          lost      gained    moved     total    ratio
  r4        464          3       151       618   39.79
  r5        457          6         24       487   38.81
  r6         23          8       171       202   37.57

NO TRAVERSAL CHANGE WAS MADE BETWEEN r5 AND r6 -- only the dump was added. Yet
`lost` went 457 -> 23 and `moved` went 24 -> 171.

  ==> THESE PER-TERM COUNTS CARRY SUBSTANTIAL RUN-TO-RUN VARIANCE, and I have
      been reading round-to-round deltas as CAUSAL all night. The fluid settles
      differently each leg, `agree hit` moves by tens of thousands between runs,
      and the population being judged is not the same population.

WHAT SURVIVES: 156,031 -> 618 across four fixes is far outside anything this
variance explains, and `gained` 20,233 -> 3 tracked a mechanism that was

WHAT I WITHDRAW: "moved 151 -> 24 shows the single-authority fix landed on that
term". It went back to 171 with no change. I told the agent that was evidence and
it was not.

  THE GENERAL FORM: a controlled A/B needs the control to be run, not assumed
  from the previous round. I ran seven consecutive single legs and treated each
  as the control for the next, on an instrument whose population is
  scene-dependent. Two identical runs (b2a-variance-a/b) are now queued to
  establish the band that should have existed before round two.

STILL OPEN AND NOW SHARPER: steps=15..28 with lastChunk ADJACENT to the flat
walk's brick suggests the chunk-level or brick-level test rejects a cell that
contains the hit -- the L1 mask or chunk-record anySolid saying empty where the
pool says solid. THAT WOULD CONTRADICT SEVEN CLEAN SOURCE COMPARES, which is
exactly the case the instrument said would need EXPLAINING rather than fixing.
Probe extension requested before any fix.


================================================================================
THE NULL PAIR: 102x SWING ON TWO IDENTICAL RUNS. SEVEN ROUNDS OF READING VOIDED.
================================================================================

  b2a-variance-a   ratio 39.46x  disagree=41     lost=27    gained=14  moved=0    hit=45,638
  b2a-variance-b   ratio 37.72x  disagree=4,191  lost=3,695 gained=370 moved=126  hit=457,433

SAME BINARY, SAME CVARS, SAME POSE, SAME SEED. disagree swings 102x. hit swings
10x.

That is not noise around a value -- THE POPULATION BEING JUDGED DIFFERS BY AN
ORDER OF MAGNITUDE between runs, because the marcher's hit count depends on the
FLUID occupancy volume's content and the fluid settles differently every leg.
Normalising does not rescue it: 41/45,638 = 0.090% against 4,191/457,433 =
0.916%, still 10x apart.

WHAT IS RETIRED:
  - Every per-term count across all seven rounds, as a fix-tracking signal.
  - The r4/r5/r6 comparisons specifically. I directed three rounds of work off
    differences that this band swallows whole.
  - "The ratio fell monotonically as correctness improved" -- I said this TWICE
    as the strongest quality signal in the sequence. The ratio's own band is
    37.57 / 37.72 / 38.81 / 39.46 on an identical traversal, ~5%. The monotonic
    fall was partly a walk through noise. WITHDRAWN.

WHAT SURVIVES, CHECKED AGAINST THE BAND RATHER THAN ASSERTED:
  - r1's 156,031 is 37x the worst variance run's total.
  - `gained` 20,233 -> 3 is 6000x.
  Both are outside anything this explains. The frame-alignment fix and the
  integer-containment fix are real, and the mechanisms were named and derived
  rather than inferred from the counts.

THE LESSON, AND IT IS THE SAME ONE THREE TIMES TONIGHT IN A NEW COSTUME:
A CONTROL MUST BE RUN, NOT ASSUMED FROM THE PREVIOUS ROUND. I ran seven
consecutive single legs and treated each as the control for the next, on an
instrument whose population is scene-dependent. The null pair cost two legs and
should have been the second thing measured, not the ninth.

  Compare: the depth gate had a same-frame control from the first run and its
  verdict has been trustworthy throughout. The source compare had one too --
  seven clean reads, all meaningful. THE INSTRUMENTS WITH SAME-FRAME CONTROLS
  PRODUCED USABLE ANSWERS ALL NIGHT; THE ONE COMPARED ACROSS LEGS DID NOT.

ROOT CAUSE IS INCIDENTAL AND FIXABLE. The marcher reads the brick pool. It needs
voxel.Fluid.Enable 1 ONLY because the view extension registers off the fluid tick
-- a Phase 0 convenience that has outlived its purpose and now injects a 10x
population swing into every measurement. Severing it makes the scene the terrain,
which is deterministic at a fixed spawn.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE VARIANCE CAUSE WAS THE FRAME, NOT THE FLUID CONTENT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

My diagnosis ("the marcher's hit count depends on the fluid volume's CONTENT")
was wrong, and the correct one is narrower and explains more.

Under source 1 the marcher reads the BRICK POOL, which the fluid does not touch.
What it borrowed was the fluid volume's FRAME: FrameOriginVoxel =
Volume->GetOriginVoxel(), which is DesiredOriginVoxel(camera) run through
RecentreTo's HYSTERESIS -- so it depends on the HISTORY of camera motion and on
whether a recentre happened to land that run.

  Different frame -> different clip box -> DIFFERENT RAYS.

That is why the judged population moved 10x, and crucially it also explains the
BRICK-ONLY terms swinging, which "the fluid content varies" could not. My
explanation was consistent with the headline symptom and could not account for
the rest of the evidence -- and I did not notice, because the headline symptom
was the one I went looking to explain.

FIXED: the frame is now camera-anchored and chunk-snapped -- a pure function of
the pose. Default reach 25.6 m preserves exactly the geometry every prior number
was taken at, so the swap changes DETERMINISM without changing WHAT IS MEASURED.
The fluid still runs; it is simply no longer in the measurement.

A COST ACCEPTED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND: VoxelFluidWalkVoxelLine resolves world
voxels as localStart + FluidVolumeOriginVoxel, so the occupancy walk's local
frame IS the volume frame by construction. That file is the fluid contract --
shared with the solver, the collision tests and the CPU mirror -- and not the
marcher's to change. So under the camera frame the occupancy comparison would
address the wrong voxels, and it is GATED OFF saying "NOT RUN... This is not a
pass and not a failure." It remains available at Source 0 and is settled over
seven clean runs.

The control therefore moved onto the walk it actually judges: the flat BRICK walk
against a 1e-6 UU entry nudge -- same construction, same volume, same arithmetic
history the restarts perturb, no fluid in it. The old occupancy floor keeps its
own slot, because TWO COMPARISONS NEED TWO FLOORS and conflating them is how a
verdict gets read against the wrong one.

THE DURABLE FORM, agreed by both of us: WE SPENT SEVEN ROUNDS READING DELTAS
WITHOUT EVER MEASURING THE NOISE BAND OF THE INSTRUMENT PRODUCING THEM. The null
pair should have been the FIRST leg of B-2a, not the eighth.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NULL PAIR ON THE CAMERA-ANCHORED FRAME: THE GEOMETRY REPRODUCES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  b2a-null-c  stepsFlat=546,169,061  stepsHier=14,669,379  37.23x
              disagree=71   lost=29   gained=2  moved=40
  b2a-null-d  stepsFlat=546,076,889  stepsHier=14,586,764  37.44x
              disagree=562  lost=520  gained=8  moved=34

  stepsFlat AGREES TO 0.017%. Before the frame fix the judged population differed
  by 10x. The rays are now the same rays.
  THE RATIO AGREES TO 0.56% (37.23 vs 37.44), against a ~5% band before. FIRST
  TIME IN THIS WORKSTREAM THAT TWO IDENTICAL RUNS PRODUCED THE SAME ANSWER.

  But `lost` is 29 vs 520 -- still 18x -- while gained (2 vs 8) and moved
  (40 vs 34) are now tight. So `lost` has a variance source that is NOT the frame
  and is NOT shared with the other two terms.

CANDIDATE, NOT YET ESTABLISHED: the rays are identical and both walks read the
same pool, so a difference in volume CONTENT should affect both equally and
produce no `lost` at all. Yet `lost` alone moves. Brick residency is not
identical between legs (97,019 / 97,082 / 97,471 tonight) and the comparator
samples at a FIXED DEFERRED TIME while the pool is still accepting chunks.
If that is the mechanism, the fix is not in the traversal: THE COMPARATOR MUST
RUN ON A SETTLED VOLUME AND NOTHING CURRENTLY MAKES IT WAIT FOR ONE.

  Every other gate here learned that separately -- the byte-equality gate reads
  only a converged pool, the depth gate needed a settled scene, and I broke
  "read nothing before it settles" four times today by hand. This would be the
  fifth instance and the first inside a shader-side instrument.

WHAT THIS CHANGES: the ratio now has a MEASURED BAND on a walk whose remaining
defect touches at most 520 of ~1.35M rays. It is still refused and should stay
refused -- but "nothing here is measurable" is no longer true, which matters when
deciding whether B-2b proceeds.


================================================================================
THE INDEX HASH SETTLES IT: THE LEGS MARCHED DIFFERENT WORLDS (04:40)
================================================================================

  b2a-hash-k  indexHash=0x357145c4d30dd56f  37.09x  disagree=100  lost=46 gained=14 moved=40
  b2a-hash-l  indexHash=0xdcc59afc0c13752c  37.82x  disagree=15   lost=9  gained=6  moved=0

HASHES DIFFER, SO `lost` WAS NEVER COMPARABLE ACROSS THOSE RUNS. The pre-registered
branch fired: convergence, not traversal, and not the RDG synchronisation defect.

  EQUAL COUNTS ARE NOT EQUAL CONTENTS -- and a count structurally cannot say so.
  The hash is order-dependent ON PURPOSE: two grids holding the same chunks in
  different CELLS are different worlds to a ray, and a commutative checksum would
  have called them equal and prolonged this by another round.

THE RDG DEFECT IS UNTESTED, NOT EXONERATED. The branch that would have implicated
it -- matching hashes with swinging counts -- did not occur. The index upload
still writes through a raw RHILock/memcpy OUTSIDE RDG while the marcher reads it
INSIDE RDG passes, which is an unsynchronised write to a resource RDG believes it
owns. It should be fixed on its own terms rather than because a measurement
demanded it. It was flagged by its own author BEFORE the leg that could have
implicated it, which is the right way round.

WHY THE COMPLETENESS GATE WAS NOT ENOUGH: it compares index entries against the
pool's level-0 count, but THE POOL'S OWN COUNT VARIES BETWEEN LEGS -- 97,019 /
97,082 / 97,471 tonight. So "complete" is measured against a moving target and
the index can faithfully match a pool that itself holds a slightly different
world each run. The signal that does not move is the streaming system's own
convergence (jobsInFlight=0 AND pendingJobs=0 AND loaded at peak, held), which
every other gate here already uses.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE RATIO, CHARACTERISED THOUGH NOT YET CERTIFIED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  37.09  37.09  37.13  37.23  37.44  37.61  37.82

NINE RUNS, ~2% TOTAL SPREAD, across three settle regimes and two different frame
constructions. It has not left that band since the frame fix regardless of what
the correctness terms did.

  Against the plan's assumed 9.19x -- which was measured on a two-level mip over
  a FLAT 1-bit volume and marked DEAD -- this is FOUR TIMES BETTER than the
  projection the entire performance case rests on.

STILL BEHIND A GATE THAT HAS NOT PASSED, and stays unquoted as a result. But it
is characterised rather than unknown, and the risk surfaced early ("if the skip
ratio comes back materially lower, that is a finding about the project") has
landed decisively on the good side.


================================================================================
THE 64-RAY DUMP FOUND THE MECHANISM AND ITS OWN VERDICT LINE BURIED IT (05:24)
================================================================================

  read 1  disagree=4,316  lost=3,781  gained=374  moved=161
    face axis  x=5  y=57  z=2        <-- 89% ON ONE AXIS
    sign       -=3  +=61             <-- 95% ON ONE SIGN
    on a brick face = 59 of 64       <-- 92% ON A BOUNDARY
    verdict printed: "no single shared property across the dumped rays."

  read 2  disagree=239   lost=12   gained=3   moved=224
    x=8 y=30 z=26 | -=29 +=35 | on a brick face=36 of 64
    verdict printed: "no single shared property across the dumped rays."

THE VERDICT LOGIC TESTS FOR *EVERY* AND THE SIGNAL IS 89%. So a mechanism with a
handful of exceptions reads IDENTICALLY TO NOISE, and the instrument printed
"consistent with a residual float floor" over the top of the most concentrated
population it has ever produced.

  EVERY is the right bar for declaring CERTAINTY and the wrong bar for declaring
  PRESENCE. Same shape as every other failure tonight: A CHECK WHOSE STATED
  CONCLUSION IS NARROWER THAN ITS OWN DATA.

THE TWO READS TOGETHER ARE THE ACTUAL FINDING:
  high count (4,316) -> STRONGLY CONCENTRATED  (+Y face, brick boundaries)
  low count  (239)   -> DIFFUSE, and `lost` collapses to 12 while `moved`
                        becomes the bulk at 224

  ==> PLAUSIBLY TWO POPULATIONS summed into one number all night: a structural
      +Y brick-face defect that dominates when it fires, and a small diffuse
      residue consistent with the predicted restart floor.

      THAT EXPLAINS THE WILD SWING. We were watching a BIMODAL quantity and
      reading it as one, which is why "lost" moved 20x-150x between runs while
      the ratio never left a 2% band. A bimodal count has no meaningful variance
      band, and nine rounds of delta-reading were applied to one.

THE ASYMMETRY IS THE CLUE: 61 of 64 on the POSITIVE sign says a face-entry or
face-exit handoff is not symmetric between +Y and -Y. That is the same class of
error already made twice in this function -- the nudge, then the ULP -- and both
times it was ONE AUTHORITY DISAGREEING WITH ANOTHER AT A BOUNDARY.

Ratio this leg: 36.38x and 36.25x. Ten runs, 36.25-37.82, all with stepsOcc=0
confirming the occupancy arm is correctly gated off under the camera frame.

  s1-shadow-c0b  cascades on, march off      p50 34.86 ms  p95 35.55
  s1-shadow-m1b  cascades on, march ON       p50 40.82 ms  p95 41.42  (+5.96)

RESTATED AGAINST THE SHADOWLESS BASELINE, which is the number that matters for

    shadows via CSM cascades   18.89 -> 34.72 ms   +15.83 ms
    shadows via marching       18.89 -> 24.97 ms   + 6.08 ms
  ==> THE FEATURE GOT 2.6x CHEAPER.

THE MARCH PASS ITSELF IS STABLE ACROSS EVERY CONFIGURATION TRIED:
  gpuMs 5.397 / 5.491 / 5.630, max 5.57-7.18, n=600 per window, three windows
  per leg. exhausted=0.00 in all -- the step budget never bound, so the step
  count is MEASURED rather than clamped.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT THIS DOES NOT SAY, AND THE LIST IS NOT SHORT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  1. MODE 1 INJECTS NOTHING. The mask is written to scratch and never reaches a
     receiver, so this measures THE COST OF COMPUTING marched shadows, not the
     cost of a frame that DISPLAYS them. S2 (light-function injection) is
     designed and unbuilt.
  2. THE MASK IS PROBABLY WRONG. 53% of marched rays start inside geometry
     (near-dominant, 209,577 of 328,288), and that is unexplained -- the
     footprint hypothesis was falsified by the band split. A correct mask may
     cost more or less; nobody knows.
  3. R1 UNDERSTATES THE RETIREMENT, flagged by its author before the run: with
     GPUShadowMaxLevel -1 the four cascade GATHER WALKS still run, they simply
     submit nothing. A true retirement removes those too.
  4. Single leg for R1. The 34.86 side has two replicates; the 24.97 side has one.
  5. 64 m reach, level 0 only. Casters beyond that cast nothing -- v0's stated
     limitation, and it must appear on every capture.

SO THE HONEST HEADLINE IS: computing sun shadows by marching costs 6.08 ms where
computing them by cascades costs 15.83 ms, at this pose, with the mask unverified
and undisplayed. That is a real 2.6x on a feature the owner asked for, and it is
NOT yet "shadows work and are 2.6x cheaper".


================================================================================
THE SHADOW MASK'S NORMAL IS A CONSTANT: THE GBUFFER READ IS PROBABLY ZERO (07:20)
================================================================================

  normal survey over all 128 dumped records: 128 x N=(-0.58,-0.58,-0.58)

NOT ONE SURFACE -- 128 RECORDS ACROSS FRAMES, ALL BYTE-IDENTICAL. A surface
property varies; a constant does not. And the specific constant is the tell:
(-0.58,-0.58,-0.58) is normalize(-1,-1,-1), and the standard GBuffer normal
decode is 2*n - 1, so 2*(0,0,0) - 1 = (-1,-1,-1).

  ==> THE GBUFFER NORMAL IS PROBABLY SAMPLING AS ZERO, and the decode is
      faithfully turning "no data" into a plausible-looking unit vector that
      passes every sanity check a reader would apply.

SAME DEFECT CLASS AS THE MARCHER'S EMIT EARLIER TONIGHT, which was handed 1x1
black system textures for SceneColor and every GBuffer because
GetOrCreateSceneTextureUniformBuffer IGNORES the SetupMode argument when the
frame already has scene textures. That one reported a plausible 0.074 ms for
writing dummies and was caught only because a downstream consumer had stricter
requirements. Second instance in one night of a scene-texture binding silently
returning nothing.

EVERYTHING RECONCILES UNDER THIS READING:
  NdotL=0.06 on every sample     one constant vector against a fixed sun
  backface=0 everywhere          a single normal cannot separate anything
  ~50% inside, burial exactly 1  a diagonal offset clears 0.58 voxels per axis
                                 at offset 1.0 and never leaves the voxel
  1.0 -> 1.5 worse, 0.5 mixed    no scalar along a FIXED WRONG DIRECTION works,
                                 which is why all three sweeps disagreed with
                                 both competing models

FOUR HYPOTHESES DIED GETTING HERE, THREE OF THEM MINE:
  footprint-at-range (author's)  falsified by the band split: near dominates
  grazing-angle pose (mine)      falsified by equal per-band RATES
  deep burial (author's)         falsified by burial=1 at 99.9%
  offset-magnitude coin (mine)   falsified by 1.5 making it worse

  EACH DIED TO A NUMBER, NOT AN ARGUMENT, AND EACH DEATH NEEDED AN INSTRUMENT
  THAT DID NOT EXIST THE ROUND BEFORE: per-band denominators, a burial
  histogram, and finally a normal survey. The denominators alone converted a
  confident wrong conclusion into a falsified one in a single leg.

WHAT DOES NOT MOVE: gpuMs 5.303 / 5.491 / 5.630 / 5.760 across every sweep, and
R1's 34.86 -> 24.97 ms. Those rays marched and cost regardless of where they
started, so THE PERFORMANCE CASE FOR MARCHED SHADOWS STANDS ON ITS OWN. What is
in question is whether the current mask means anything, and it probably does not.


================================================================================
THE GBUFFER FIX LANDS. EVERY PRE-REGISTERED CRITERION MET ON THE FIRST RUN.
================================================================================

  GBufferA bound from MRT slot 1, name='GBufferA', extent=1552x880,
  format=A2B10G10R10.                          <-- a real texture at scene extent

  F1:  nonAxisN/f = 0.0        canary: normals are axis-aligned (was 100%)
       backface   = 194,028    non-zero AT LAST (was 0 -- one constant normal
                               cannot separate front from back)
       inside RATE near 0.8%  mid 1.7%  far 1.6%     (was 53.9 / 52.5 / 46.5)
       inside/f   = 4,946      from 328,288 -- A 66x COLLAPSE
       hit%       = 3.4        predicted 5-10 from D1's own arithmetic
                               (59.2 - 53.0 = 6.2). Landed 3.4.
       gpuMs mean = 6.502      UP from 5.397, exactly as pre-flagged

  ROOT CAUSE, CONFIRMED IN ENGINE SOURCE: the uniform buffer at
  PostRenderBasePassDeferred is built with SceneDepth ONLY
  (DeferredShadingRenderer.cpp:2499), and unset GBuffer slots fall back to
  SystemTextures.Black (SceneTextures.cpp:1108) -- NON-NULL, so the null check
  was structurally unable to fail. Black through the standard 2n-1 decode gives
  normalize(-1,-1,-1) = (-0.577)^3 on every pixel of every frame.
  THE DECODE WAS NEVER WRONG. THE INPUT WAS.

  SECOND INSTANCE TONIGHT of a scene-texture binding silently returning nothing
  at this exact hook. The marcher's emit hit the same thing and reported a
  plausible 0.074 ms for writing 1x1 dummies.

THESE SUPERSEDE THE EARLIER 24.97 / 9.89 ms. That configuration had 53% of rays

================================================================================
THE TWO MODES ARE TWO DIFFERENT DEFECTS, NOT ONE AT TWO INTENSITIES (08:40)
================================================================================

Six legs on the fixed build, mode by `disagree` magnitude:
  r4 1153 HIGH | r5 229 LOW | r6 1060 HIGH | r7 1060 HIGH | r8 27 LOW | r9 234 LOW

  HIGH  lost by descent: neverDescended=941  descendedAndMissed=42   (95.7% never)
  LOW   lost by descent: neverDescended=6    descendedAndMissed=9-12 (INVERTED)

THAT INVERSION IS THE STRUCTURAL FACT. They are not one population at different
intensities. The HIGH mode is AN ADVANCE THAT SKIPS A BRICK WITH CONTENT; the
LOW mode is A STEPPING MISS INSIDE A BRICK THAT WAS ENTERED. Fixing either will
not move the other, and NINE ROUNDS OF SUMMING THEM PRODUCED NOISE THAT BOTH OF
US READ AS SIGNAL.

THE DESCENT MASK NOW DISCRIMINATES -- inconclusive went 100% -> 0%. Its earlier
failure was structural: the mask followed the LAST CHUNK WALKED and reset on
every crossing, and a `lost` ray BY DEFINITION continues past the hit, so by the
time it finished the mask described a chunk far beyond the one being asked
about. 100% inconclusive is exactly what that design produces. Fixed by passing
the chunk the FLAT walk already identified and holding it for the whole walk.

THE DUPLICATED-EXPRESSION FIX IS FORMALLY FALSIFIED. It targeted lost onFace=550
and high mode now reads 977. Removing that authority did not stop the skip. The
mechanism (VoxelMarchCellExitT recomputing the cell from the same t) was real and
the fix is correct on its own terms, but IT WAS NOT THIS POPULATION'S CAUSE.

  AND THE COLLAPSE I REPORTED AS SUCCESS -- "lost onFace 550 -> 22-36" -- WAS
  MEASURED ENTIRELY IN LOW MODE. I ran three legs, all landed low, and I
  reported bimodality gone. That is the exact error I had warned the agent
  about two rounds earlier: THREE LEGS OF ONE MODE IS A COIN LANDING HEADS
  THREE TIMES, NOT EVIDENCE OF UNIMODALITY.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A CONSERVATION CHECK THAT HAS NEVER FAILED IS NOT YET KNOWN TO BE A CHECK
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

r7: disagree=1060, lost=983 -> 77 left for gained+moved, but the delta bins sum
to 173. Arithmetically impossible, and the new conservation check DID NOT FIRE.
r9: disagree=234, lost=18, moved=212 -> self-consistent, check also silent.

So the check has never been exercised by a genuine failure and WE DO NOT KNOW
WHETHER IT WORKS. Worse, a conservation check that passes on an impossible line
converts a VISIBLE contradiction into a CERTIFIED one.

  The remedy is the shadow workstream's discipline applied here: a mutation arm
  that deliberately corrupts a slot and confirms the check prints its failure.
  Every instrument that mattered tonight was one that FIRED.

ALSO UNRESOLVED: r7 printed `moved onFace=0 offFace=0` beside `moved by delta
(all 173 rays)`; r9's two accountings AGREE exactly (143+69 = 212 = 212). So the
face split is not systematically wrong -- it reads zero on SOME legs only. That
is more dangerous than a constant error: a reader who checks it once and finds
it consistent will trust it thereafter.

THE METHOD ERROR, AND IT IS THE SAME ONE THREE TIMES TONIGHT IN A THIRD COSTUME:
  first  -- reading round-to-round deltas before measuring the instrument's band
  second -- treating three legs of one mode as evidence of unimodality
  third  -- treating three legs near each other as A BASELINE VALUE

  All three are the same failure: A CONTROL MUST BE RUN, AND A POPULATION MUST BE
  CHARACTERISED BEFORE ANY DIFFERENCE DRAWN FROM IT MEANS ANYTHING. I have now
  made it at three different scopes in one session, and caught it each time only
  because something else forced a repeat.

  CONSEQUENCE FOR WHAT COUNTS AS A FIX: with a 13x spread, "collapses to near
  zero" is provable and "550 -> 22" is not. I reported the latter as success.

=== 2026-08-21  B-2b-1 RING LEGS + AN ARCHIVE-WIDE RETRACTION ===

Legs (spawn -61440,-61440, static, 470 s, reach 256 m, SkipLevels 2):

                ratio    disagree    hitL0     hitL1   ringDiscordant
  b2b-ctl-r1   36.86x        169    349,750       0          0    <- voxel.March.Rings 0

  b2b-rings-r2 40.07x    126,425    207,110       0          0

RESULT: rings-on is broken. hitL1 = 0 on both arms, ringDiscordant = 0, so the
L1-vs-L0 comparison that WAS the deliverable of B-2b-1 did not happen. The
40.5x/40.1x figures are NOT "the ratio at 256 m" and must not be quoted: they
are a damaged level-0 walk over a longer segment.

The control DID reproduce the level-0 baseline bit-exactly (36.86x, disagree
169), which is the one clean result -- the per-ring ray rescale is sound.

--- CORRECTION MADE IN FLIGHT ---
I first reported "the L1 index is empty" from the line
  [00.02.22] index attached: seeded 0 chunks of 0 in the pool (L0 0, L1 0; ...)
That is the ATTACH line at t+2.4 s against an EMPTY pool, not a steady-state
census. Retracted. Per-level index residency is not instrumented at stats time
at all, which is why the wrong line was the only one available to misread.

--- THE ACTUAL FINDING, AND IT INVALIDATES THE ARCHIVE ---
  [00.02.27] LogVoxelMarchIndex: Warning: observed level-0 chunk span
  (9603,9601,346) is at or past HALF the grid (128,128,128) ... the symptom is
  chunks silently shadowing each other as HOLES rather than as wrong terrain.

Span 9,603 against a 128 grid: ~75 chunks per cell, all but one shadowed.

Swept all 780 logs in Saved/. THE WARNING FIRES IN EVERY LEG THAT ATTACHES THE
INDEX -- 100%, no exceptions. Legs that come back clean (p5-retire-*, g1-gioff-*,
b2a-settle900-*, b2a-bins-t) have attach=0; they never attached it. So the index
has been aliased since the day it was introduced and THERE IS NO CLEAN BASELINE
LEG IN THE ARCHIVE. The 36.86x control is not a reference that rings-on failed
against; both sit on the same broken index.

WHY THIS PLAUSIBLY INFLATES THE RATIO RATHER THAN DEPRESSING IT: a shadowed
chunk reads as a HOLE (the guard says so), a hole is empty space, and empty
space is precisely what the hierarchy skips well. The marcher may be looking
brilliant at flying through a world of holes it invented. Supporting but not
proof: control hitL0 349,750 / 1,354,896 rays = 26% hit rate, low for a camera
inside 51.2 m of terrain.

Same error shape as the 94% boundary concentration: a measurement that reports
impressively because of a selection effect, not the mechanism claimed.

--- GATE v4 IS ONE-SIDED AND PASSED A CATASTROPHE ---
  GATE v4: PASS -- flat-only 6,826 vs hier-only 225,022, asymmetry 0.03x
  GATE v4 detection: 0.03x is 284.5 sigma from 1.0 | excess -218,196 rays (-16.1%)
The bar tests flat-only <= 1.5 * hier-only, so a 33x collapse in the OTHER
direction passes. A PASS at 0.03x with a -16% excess is worse than a FAIL,
because it would have been reported as success. Needs a two-sided bar.

--- FIX DIRECTION (recorded so the obvious wrong one is not taken) ---
Do NOT raise kDimXY/kDimZ. A 9,603 span needs a 16,384 grid; at three axes that
is an address space, not a table. The defect is upstream: the index is fed
chunks it should not hold. At 256 m reach the marched band is ~160 chunks
across. Bound what ENTERS the index to the marched reach; the existing
static_assert then keeps proving the grid is big enough, and the span tracker
becomes a real check instead of a permanent warning. The same wrong-units
suspicion (coarse keys measured as level-0) is the leading candidate for
hitL1 = 0.

OPEN, blocking any quoted ratio:
  1. cause of the 9,603 span
  2. a leg with the guard SILENT -- the first honest baseline this project has had
  3. re-measure the level-0 ratio on it; if it is not ~36.9x, the number was the
     selection effect and we needed to know
  4. per-level index residency at stats time
  5. two-sided gate v4

The guard worked perfectly -- named the mode, named the symptom, printed at 2.4 s
in every leg. The failure is that nobody, me included across two messages, read
the log above the line they came for.

--- RETRACTION OF THE RETRACTION ABOVE (same day, before any leg was re-run) ---

The "index has been aliased in 100% of legs since it was introduced" claim IS
WITHDRAWN. It was wrong, and the way it was wrong is worth more than the claim.

ObservedMin/ObservedMax are NEVER RESET and NEVER SHRINK ON EVICTION. They are
the union of every chunk coordinate the index has ever been OFFERED, across the
whole session. 9,603 chunks x 3.2 m = 30.7 km is not a resident band -- it is
HOW FAR THE CAMERA TRAVELLED.

Aliasing requires SIMULTANEITY. Two chunks never resident at the same moment
cannot shadow each other, and a union over time cannot tell the difference.

That also explains the 100% firing rate, and the explanation matters more than
the fact: A MONOTONICALLY GROWING STATISTIC AGAINST A FIXED THRESHOLD MUST
EVENTUALLY FIRE IN EVERY LEG THAT RUNS LONG ENOUGH. It could not have come out
any other way, so the 100% carried no information whatsoever.

This is the boundary-concentration error one level up -- in the INSTRUMENT
rather than in the data. I mined a structurally-guaranteed reading for
mechanism, exactly as before, while writing a message warning about doing it.

New general rule, earned twice now:
  NEVER GATE ON A STATISTIC THAT CANNOT COME OUT THE OTHER WAY.

Note what is NOT retracted: the ring legs are still broken (hitL1 = 0,
ringDiscordant = 0, disagree 231,852) and the 40.5x/40.1x figures are still not
quotable. Only the ARCHIVE-WIDE invalidation is withdrawn. Whether the baseline
is clean is now OPEN and measurable rather than assumed in either direction.

A third defect in the same guard, from this round's own work: it called itself
"level-0 span" while the index had just been made to accept level-1, so it was
folding two coordinate spaces into one number.

WHAT REPLACED IT (built, compiled, linked -- VoxelEarthShaders.dll + VoxelEarth.dll):
  * COLLISIONS ARE OBSERVED, NOT INFERRED. TMap of cell -> owning chunk coord,
    sized to the resident set (~41k) not the grid. An add landing on a cell a
    DIFFERENT chunk already holds IS the shadowing, counted per level. One map
    probe on the add path, and it cannot report a catastrophe that is not
    happening.
  * The span survives, renamed GetCumulativeCoordSpan, printed with "THE SPAN IS
    A TRAVEL LOG, NOT AN ALIASING CLAIM" on the line so it cannot be misread a
    fourth time.
  * The inflation direction is wired in as a VERDICT: non-zero collisions log at
    Error with "Each is a hole the marcher invented, and holes are exactly what
    the hierarchy skips fastest -- so the skip ratio from this leg is inflated by
    an unknown amount and must not be quoted." Zero collisions says so plainly.
  * DroppedOutOfBand was declared, reset, PRINTED in the attach log -- and never
    incremented anywhere. It read 0 forever, which looks like "nothing dropped"
    and meant "nothing counted". Now real, and the offered-per-level split
    answers the L1 question directly: "the pool never offered L1" (residency)
    vs "we were offered them and dropped them" (marcher defect).
  * GATE v4 IS TWO-SIDED: 0.67x <= asymmetry <= 1.5x. The inverted case gets its
    own verdict naming it as the HARDER failure -- a hierarchy finding content
    the flat walk misses is a broken reference or a hallucinating walk, not a
    skip defect.
  * BUDGET-EXHAUSTION COUNTERS. At 256 m a ray crosses 10x the distance on the
    same allowance, so "no hit" and "ran out of allowance" had become the same
    observation -- a candidate cause of the hitL0 collapse. Verdict fires at 5%
    and says the ratio is then a property of voxel.March.StepBudget rather than
    of the walk, and explicitly says NOT to raise it to make a number look
    better: decide the budget for the reach, pre-register, re-run.

HARNESS: tools/voxel-leg-summary.ps1 now runs an INTEGRITY SCAN before printing
any metric and REFUSES the row for an invalidated leg (-AllowInvalid overrides
and makes you say so). It keys on the observed-collision verdict, NOT on the
span -- the first version keyed on the span and would have refused every leg
ever run, which is the same error a third time. Negative control checked: a
clean leg (p5-retire-on-r1) still summarises normally.

Re-running control + rings on the new counters. Three questions, one grep each:
was any chunk ever actually shadowed; does the pool offer L1 at all; were the
ratios budget-limited.

  BELOW the 0.67x bar.
Same configuration the ONE-SIDED bar called PASS three hours earlier. And the
