p50. One leg per arm. Static flight at -61440,-61440.

TWO SUSPECTS KILLED BY ANALYSIS RATHER THAN BY LEGS, both of which I had wrong:
 1. RETIREMENT IS NOT A COST, IT IS A SAVING, AND IT WAS ALREADY MEASURED IN THIS
    FILE. armA:2560-2582 --
        today  (mesh+pack) 0.969 ms/chunk   (mesh 0.810 + pack 0.158)
        phase5 (fill+pack) 0.389 ms/chunk   (fill 0.227 + pack 0.160)
        => PHASE 5 IS 2.49x CHEAPER PER CHUNK ON THE WORKER PATH
    PackChunkMaterialising makes a flat 32,768 sampler calls per chunk against
    the mesher path's up to 64,000, and its dense sink removes the scattered
    form's 2-3x re-sampling. I named it as bisection suspect #1 without reading a
    measurement that was 4,000 lines above my own entry in the same file.
 2. THE GPU MESH TIMEOUTS ARE A READOUT, NOT A CAUSE. The completion path
    handles brick-only jobs correctly and is SHORTER than the quad path:
    bNeedQuadTotal and bNeedTables are both false under bQuadMesh=false
    (VoxelGpuMeshJobManager.cpp:1395-1404), so TotalReadback -- the one thing
    only the mesh chain writes -- is never waited on. 252 retired jobs delivered,
    0 failed, before the stall.
    THE ACTUAL FRAME WAS 76 SECONDS LONG: renderWaitMs 73,000-80,000 ms against
    renderMs of only 6-151 ms. The 10 s timeout (VoxelGpuMeshJobManager.h:353,
    hardcoded, no cvar) is measured from Submit, so at 76 s/tick EVERY in-flight
    job dies on the first poll. frameMs=400.00 is the CLAMP, not the frame.
    Raising the timeout would convert 32 timeouts into 32 jobs that still take
    76 s to poll. DO NOT TOUCH THE TIMEOUT.

SO THE ONE REMAINING BLOCKER IS NARROWED TO ASSETS, and its signature is not
voxel work at all: the game thread blocked on the render thread for ~76 s while
the render thread's own voxel timers read single-digit ms, plus worker jobs
running at an effective 0.25 GHz (jobUs=37131 against jobKcyc=9172), which is
DESCHEDULING, not slowness. That is an oversubscription/contention shape --
detail-asset resolve jobs and brick packing on the same pool -- and it is a
producer-scheduling problem, not a renderer one.

WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE PROJECT, PLAINLY: the ray marcher is DONE and it
delivers. 4.3x the frame rate at the same pose, with per-voxel materials, the
quad path deleted, and GI running over the same bricks. Assets in the volume
(Phase 6's remaining half) is now the only thing between this and shipping, and
it is a scheduling defect with a measured signature rather than an open question.

================================================================================
2026-08-21  WHY THE MARCHED WORLD LOOKS LIKE "PIECES": A ZERO NORMAL.
================================================================================
THE OWNER'S REPORT, three times across three configurations and finally decisive:
"high terrain above the character is there and rendered spotty; the same flat
plane at the player and below has no voxels." A capture at pitch -40 deg with the
clipmap off -- camera 40 m above ground, so the ground is ~48 m ahead and well
inside ring 0 -- came back COMPLETELY EMPTY, a uniform blue-grey field, while the
same run reported:
  marchMs=3.545 emitMs=0.249 | frames=17941 | tiles drawn=21123 of 21340 (99%)
  | indexEntries=98343 | declined all 0
DRAWING 99% OF TILES AND SHOWING NOTHING. That contradiction is the whole clue:
the pixels ARE being written; they have no SHAPE.

THE DEFECT, located in code:
  VoxelBrickTraverse.ush:1706  H.FaceAxis = -1;      (initialised)
  VoxelBrickTraverse.ush:1732  H.bStartedInside = true; ... return H;
      -- returns bHit=true WITHOUT EVER SETTING FaceAxis, because the segment's
         first voxel was already solid and no face was ever crossed.
  VoxelMarch.usf:774           WorldNormal = float3(FaceAxis==0?S:0,
                                                    FaceAxis==1?S:0,
                                                    FaceAxis==2?S:0);
      -- with FaceAxis == -1 all three selects miss and this is float3(0,0,0).
  VoxelMarch.usf                grep for bStartedInside / FaceAxis < 0 / == -1
      -- ZERO OCCURRENCES. Nothing gates it. The zero goes straight into
         OutGBufferA = float4(EncodeNormal(WorldNormal), 0).

A ZERO NORMAL IS NOT A WRONG DIRECTION, IT IS NO DIRECTION. Deferred lighting
gives it no diffuse response, so the pixel shades as a flat featureless field
while depth, material, stencil and every counter report it as correctly drawn.

AND IT EXPLAINS WHY THE DEPTH GATE PASSED. That gate compares DEPTH, and the
depth on these pixels is RIGHT -- 0.1039% interior disagreement against a 0.6687%
control. The gate was never wrong; it was never asked about normals. The plan's
own risk line asked for a pass that "writes a flat depth AND NORMAL and confirms
SSAO/TSR/Lumen react to it". The depth half was built and run. The normal half
was not, and this is what lived in the gap.

WHY IT CONCENTRATES NEAR THE CAMERA (hypothesis, not yet measured): the ring loop
calls the walk ONCE PER RING with that ring's own SegIn. Ring 0 starts at the
camera, in air, and produces proper faces. But any ring L>0 whose segment begins
below the surface starts INSIDE SOLID -- so whenever ring 0 fails to hit, the
next ring returns a started-inside hit with no face. Far silhouettes against sky
keep their faces; ground the ray reaches after a ring transition does not. That
matches "spotty above, flat below" exactly. TO CONFIRM: count started-inside hits
per ring. NOT DONE YET -- the fix below does not depend on it.

THE FIX (VoxelMarch.usf, after the three selects):
    if (V.FaceAxis < 0) { WorldNormal = -Ray.DirWorld; }
There is NO correct geometric face here -- the ray never crossed one -- so this
is a CONVENTION and is written as one: face the viewer. -DirWorld is unit length
(VoxelMarchBuildRay normalises it), gives a defined diffuse response, and
degrades to plain shading rather than to black. IT IS NOT THE SURFACE'S TRUE
ORIENTATION and must never be read as one.
Re-capture at the identical pose is running. If the frame fills with shaded
ground, this was it.

================================================================================
2026-08-21  THE ASSET STALL: ROOT CAUSE FOUND, AND IT IS NOT THE RENDERER
================================================================================
CORRECTION FIRST, because I told it the wrong way round: renderWaitMs is the
RENDER THREAD'S OWN IDLE TIME WAITING ON THE GAME THREAD, not the game thread
waiting on the render thread (GRenderThreadWaitTime = RenderThreadIdle,
UE_5.8 SlateRHIRenderer.cpp:1357; read at VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp:5992). So
renderWaitMs=76246 renderMs=138 means THE RENDER THREAD SAT IDLE FOR 76 s. And
the ProfileGPU in that same run reports Frame Time: 14.55 ms. THE GPU AND THE
RENDER THREAD ARE BOTH HEALTHY. The stall is entirely on the game thread.

ROOT CAUSE: vxc::GeneratedWorld::materialAt, voxel-core/include/voxelcore/
generator.h:173-184 --
    const MaterialId m = amp_->materialAt(vx, vy, vz);
    if (m != MAT_AIR || assets_ == nullptr || assets_->empty()) return m;
    const AssetVoxelRect rect{vx, vy, vx, vy};
    const std::vector<AssetInstance> insts = assets_->instancesForRect(rect, ...);
FOR EVERY AIR VOXEL it runs a full site enumeration AND a heap vector, and it
does NOT pass terrainOnly (defaults false, assetfield.h:235-237), so it walks the
dense DETAIL lattice and calls columnFacts() per candidate site.

MEASURED, two independent instruments agreeing to 1%:
    with assets     288 us per call
    without assets  0.16 us per call      => ~1,800x
  one-shot asset PROBE  272,250 calls  78.3 s   (VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp:6368)
  one occupancy region  262,144 calls  ~76 s
  same region, no assets                42.8 ms

THE CONSUMER THAT TURNS IT INTO A 76-SECOND FRAME: the fluid occupancy fill.
  VoxelFluidSubsystem.cpp:1207 -- while (Built < MaxRegions && ...
                                        && (Built == 0 || elapsed < BudgetSeconds))
  `Built == 0` GUARANTEES ONE FULL REGION PER TICK NO MATTER HOW FAR OVER THE
  4 ms BUDGET IT GOES (voxel.Fluid.Occupancy.PackMsPerTick, default 4.0).
  A region is 64^3 = 262,144 voxels and PackRegionBricks samples EVERY one with
  no early-out (VoxelFluidOccupancy.h:384-412). LatchOrigin queues 512 of them.
  512 regions at one per 76 s is ~10.8 HOURS.

CROSS-RUN SEPARATION, which is what makes this attribution rather than a story:
  full-minus-assets   no assets, fluid spawn   worst gap 1.1 s   HEALTHY
  p6-verifystore      assets, NO fluid spawn   ONE 78.9 s gap (the one-shot
                                               probe), then <= 0.7 s
  p7-gi-march         assets, NO fluid spawn   ONE 76.6 s gap, then <= 0.7 s
  full-shipping-v2    assets AND fluid spawn   72-79 s EVERY FRAME
=> ASSETS ALONE cost one 78 s probe frame. ASSETS + FLUID SPAWN is what makes it
   permanent. March, GI and RetireQuads are NOT implicated: p6-verifystore has
   none of them and stalls once identically; p5-retire-cmdline has march +
   RetireQuads + fluid WITHOUT assets and is healthy.

AND THIS EXACT DEFECT WAS ALREADY FIXED ONCE, IN A DIFFERENT CALLER. The level-0
mesher's own comment records it (VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp:11772-11778):
"AField->materialAt per air voxel ... one global mutex per ask ... ~17 chunks/s".
The mesher was rewritten away from it. World::materialAt was not.

TWO RED HERRINGS KILLED, both of which I had raised:
  * WORKER CONTENTION IS NOT IT. Detail jobs are capped adaptively at 4-24
    (VoxelDetailAssetSubsystem.cpp:1418-1420) and sat pinned at the floor of 4
    against 252 terrain jobs. And jobGHz is WORSE in the healthy no-asset control
    (0.03-0.07) than in the stalled leg (0.25), so descheduling is a symptom of
    long frames, not a cause.
  * NOTHING IN THE ASSET PATH BLOCKS THE RENDER THREAD. No FlushRenderingCommands,
    no fence, no synchronous readback in VoxelDetailAssetSubsystem.cpp. The
    StaticMesh ReleaseResourcesFence.Wait is guarded by
    AreRenderingResourcesInitialized() and every detail mesh is a fresh NewObject,
    so it never runs.
  * "streaming bound widened by 451 voxels" MULTIPLIES NOTHING IN STREAMING. It
    feeds SkipBrick only (VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp:3639-3643, :11945-11948). The
    real admission widening raised the z-range for 189 of 5088 footprints, and
    `tracked` is LOWER with assets (9097) than without (13856).

READING NOTE FOR THESE LOGS: frameMs=400.00 is the engine's DeltaTime CLAMP, and
every "(5s window)" counter is 5 s of CLAMPED GAME TIME -- so on a 76 s frame
those windows span many minutes of wall clock. tickMs=80637 (1612% of wall) is
that same artifact, not a second stall.

2026-08-21  THE OWNER'S A/B STANDARD, RUN AT LAST. THE MARCHER FAILS IT.
================================================================================
The owner set the acceptance test in one sentence: the marched image at a pose
should look approximately like the OLD renderer's image at the same pose. That is
the right test and it had never been run at a pose where the marcher misbehaves.

IDENTICAL CONDITIONS, both arms: spawn -61440,-61440, camera 40 m above ground
(z=2227.6, ground top 2187.6), pitch -40, yaw 45, sun frozen 12:00 03-20, 235 s
settle, -VoxelNoClipmap in BOTH, -NoRiverRibbons in BOTH.
Streaming fully settled in the control: loaded=50591 quads=46,872,523
tracked=100863 jobsInFlight=0 pendingJobs=0.

  QUAD PATH  (VoxelVerify00067.png): a full frame of dense voxel mountainside.
  MARCHER    (VoxelVerify00065.png): BLANK SKY. No geometry at all.

AND THE MARCHER'S OWN COUNTERS FOR THAT BLANK FRAME:
  marchMs=3.664 emitMs=0.186 | frames=18501 | tiles drawn=21120 of 21340 (99%)
  | indexEntries=98395 | declined noView=0 noVolume=0 noTextures=0
    unsupported=0 noPool=0
IT REPORTS DRAWING ALMOST EVERYTHING AND SHOWS NOTHING. Every counter is healthy
and the frame is empty -- the exact failure shape this project has been burned by
repeatedly, and the reason the owner's screenshot rule exists.

THE DEFECT IS DIRECTIONAL, and that is the strongest clue available:
  pitch -12 (toward the horizon): the marcher DOES render terrain.
  pitch -40 (looking down at ground 48 m away, WELL INSIDE RING 0's 0-128 m):
    nothing.
Same binary, same spawn, same settle, same everything but the pitch.

WHAT THIS DOES TO TONIGHT'S OTHER RESULTS -- stated plainly rather than buried:
  * THE DEPTH GATE'S PASS STANDS BUT IS NARROWER THAN I PRESENTED IT. It compares
    marched depth against RASTER depth over pixels where BOTH produced a surface.
    It cannot see a pixel the marcher never wrote, so a whole-frame miss is
    invisible to it BY CONSTRUCTION. 0.1039% vs a 0.6687% control is still true
    and still means the surfaces AGREE WHERE BOTH EXIST. It never meant coverage.
  * THE 8.14 ms p50 IS NOT A LIKE-FOR-LIKE FRAME. If the marcher is dropping
    downward-facing geometry it is also not paying for it. The 4.3x MUST NOT BE
    QUOTED until coverage is fixed and the number is re-taken. I recorded that
    ratio as the project's headline earlier tonight; IT IS NOW PROVISIONAL AND
    THE COVERAGE DEFECT IS THE REASON.
  * The zero-normal fix (started-inside hits, FaceAxis == -1) is a REAL defect and
    is still fixed, but it is NOT this. The re-capture with it applied was
    byte-for-byte as empty as the one before.

NEXT, AND IT IS A MEASUREMENT NOT A GUESS: voxel.March.VerifySource at pitch -40
prints MODEFP entered/hit PER RING. A downward ray that enters ring 0 and hits
nothing, while the quad path has geometry 48 m away, localises this to ring 0's
walk. If instead ring 0 is never ENTERED, it is the segment setup (TMin/TMax or
the ray build), not the traversal. Those two need opposite fixes and the counter
already exists to tell them apart.

--- THE RING LOCALISATION COULD NOT BE TAKEN. INSTRUMENT, NOT RENDERER. ---
downray-diag: mode 2, VerifySource 1, pitch -40, clipmap off, 400 s run.
VerifySource DISPATCHED -- ProfileGPU shows VoxelMarch.VerifySource at 217.149 ms
-- but its readback never landed:
  "IDENTICAL TO THE PREVIOUS REPORT (arbTotal=0 flatOnly=0 hierOnly=0).
   indexHash now 0x0000000000000000, previously 0x0000000000000000"
All counters zero and the hash zero means nothing was ever read back, so MODEFP
printed no per-ring entered/hit at all. Zero MODEFP lines in the leg.
The settle gate was NOT the blocker this time (the non-blocking "index holds
98295 of the pool's 27538 level-0 chunks" line fired, which only prints once
bVolumeSettled is true).

SO THE RING QUESTION -- "is ring 0 entered and missing, or never entered?" --
IS STILL OPEN, and it is the question whose two answers need opposite fixes.

WHAT IS NOT OPEN, AND DOES NOT DEPEND ON ANY INSTRUMENT: the A/B above. Two
images, one pose, identical conditions, streaming settled, clipmap off in both.
The quad path draws a full mountainside; the marcher draws sky. That is the
owner's own acceptance standard and it is a FAIL, and it needed no counter to
establish -- which is exactly why that standard exists.

FOR WHOEVER PICKS THIS UP, IN ORDER:
 1. The cheapest path to the ring answer avoids the broken readback entirely:
    capture mode 1 at pitch -12, -20, -30, -40 and find where coverage breaks.
    A sharp cliff between two pitches localises it far better than a counter that
    will not report, and each capture is ~5 minutes.
 2. voxel.March.VerifySource's readback not landing is now its own defect, and it
    is the SECOND readback in this feature that does not land (see the
    voxel.Cover.VerifyStore standalone-FRDGBuilder crash above). Two readbacks,
    two failures, same feature -- look for a shared cause before fixing either
    separately.
 3. Do not quote the 8.14 ms / 4.3x headline until coverage is fixed. A renderer
    that omits geometry does not pay for it.

================================================================================
2026-08-21  THE SKIP DEFECT IS FALSIFIED, AND THE "-12 WORKS" CLAIM WAS WRONG
================================================================================
Two things I carried into this session as established are now dead. Both were
mine.

--- 1. THE HIERARCHY IS NOT THE CAUSE. FALSIFIED, TWO-SIDED. ---
marcher-flat-pitch40: the failing capture re-run with ONE flag changed,
voxel.March.SkipLevels 2 -> 0, i.e. the flat walk instead of the hierarchical
one. Everything else byte-identical (spawn -61440,-61440, alt +40 m, pitch -40,
yaw 45, sun frozen, 235 s settle, -VoxelNoClipmap, -VoxelRetireQuads=1).

  hierarchical (VoxelVerify00065): blank.   marchMs= 3.545  tiles drawn=21120
  flat         (VoxelVerify00069): blank.   marchMs=35.547  tiles drawn=21120

  IMAGE DIFF, hier vs flat: max channel delta 4, mean 0.2476.
  Both are the same smooth ~500-colour gradient. The 10x more expensive walk
  produced NO visible difference.

So the skip is not what is losing the geometry, and the shader comment at
VoxelBrickTraverse.ush:578-585 -- "the hierarchy misses real content the flat
walk finds", citing the PASS -> 3.80x -> 32.25x progression -- IS STALE. That
progression was already retracted in this same file (see the 2026-08-21
retraction above: same leg, same config, 50 s apart, FAIL 32.25x and PASS 1.34x).
The comment preserved a finding the record had withdrawn. It needs correcting.

--- 2. "THE MARCHER RENDERS TERRAIN AT PITCH -12" WAS A CLIPMAP. ---
The directional framing rested on that one observation. Checking the leg's own
command line:

  capture-marcher-shipping (pitch -12): NO -VoxelNoClipmap.
  capture-marcher-nearfield (pitch -40):   -VoxelNoClipmap.

The smooth white ridge filling the middle of VoxelVerify00061 is AVoxelClipmapActor,
not the marcher. What the marcher actually contributed at -12 is the SPECKLED
brown rock above the ridge line -- plus a MAGENTA patch, which is
VOXEL_MARCH_PLACEHOLDER_MATERIAL, i.e. a hit whose material decode failed.

The two legs differed in the clipmap AND the pitch, so PITCH IS NOT ISOLATED and
"the defect is directional" IS NOT ESTABLISHED. It may still be true. The
evidence offered for it does not reach it, and the A/B that looked like a
controlled pair was not one.

--- 3. THE TILE COUNTER WAS NEVER A COVERAGE MEASURE, AND THAT DISSOLVES THE
       "REPORTS DRAWING EVERYTHING AND SHOWS NOTHING" PARADOX. ---
VoxelMarch.usf:543-550: a tile is appended if ANY ONE of its 64 pixels hits
(InterlockedOr into a groupshared flag, one write per 8x8 group). So
"drawn=21120 of 21340 (99%)" is consistent with a PER-PIXEL hit rate of ~1.6%,
if the surviving hits are spread evenly. There was never a contradiction between
that counter and an empty frame, and I presented it as one for hours.

MEASURED, in the region where the marcher DOES draw (VoxelVerify00061, rock
crop 80x100 px): 87% of pixels are rock, dropout ~13%, no row or column
periodicity. So where it draws it mostly fills; the speckle is a second, smaller
defect than the missing bulk.

--- WHAT IS CLEARED, AND BY WHOM ---
Two independent reads of the downstream half found no defect:
  * emit pipeline state: depth test CF_DepthNearOrEqual with depth WRITE enabled,
    SceneDepth bound FExclusiveDepthStencil::DepthWrite_StencilWrite
    (VoxelMarchRenderer.cpp:4742-4745, :4785-4787), viewport = view rect
    (:4797), DrawPrimitiveIndirect off a GPU-written arg buffer (:4804).
  * the depth path: OutDepth = HitClip.z/HitClip.w projected through
    ResolvedView.TranslatedWorldToClip (VoxelMarch.usf:755-756), no clamp, no
    saturate, no near-plane early-out; MarchRayOriginLocalUU cancels exactly, so
    HitTWS = TranslatedWorldCameraOrigin + DirWorld*THitUU. The hit bit is ORed
    unconditionally into P.y bit 0 (:313), so a real hit CANNOT alias the
    uint2(0,0) miss sentinel. Pack/unpack round-trip and both sides read the
    same MarchPackOriginVoxel (:3962 march, :4688 emit).

--- THREE DEFECTS FOUND EN ROUTE, NONE OF THEM YET SHOWN TO BE THIS ONE ---
  A. A DATA RACE, and the engine caught it, in the FAILING leg itself
     (capture-marcher-nearfield.log:4103):
       "Ensure condition failed: this->Array.Num() == InitialNum
        Container has changed during ranged-for iteration!"
       FVoxelBrickPool::GetLevelCensus()      VoxelBrickPool.cpp:646
       FVoxelMarchRenderExtension::PreRenderBasePass_RenderThread()
                                              VoxelMarchRenderer.cpp:4068
     The render thread walks the resident-chunk TMap every frame for a settle
     gate while the game thread mutates it. Real, reproducible, and it wants an
     O(1) per-level counter rather than a walk.
  B. THE SKIP CONTROL ARM IS INERT. VoxelBrickTraverse.ush, the
     `#if VOXEL_MARCH_SKIP_LEVELS >= 2` block and its `#else` are BYTE-IDENTICAL
     -- both look the chunk up and both do `t = tChunkNext; continue;` on a
     miss. The comment on the #else says "the chunk level is NOT skipped ... it
     simply does not use a miss to jump", and the code jumps. SkipLevels 1 and 2
     cannot differ at the chunk level, so any sweep across them measured one arm
     twice.
  C. THE SETTLE GATE COMPARES DIFFERENT POPULATIONS. VoxelMarchRenderer.cpp:4061
     says "The index tracks LEVEL 0 ONLY (kIndexedLevel)" and gates on
     IndexEntriesNow == PoolLevel0Chunks. The index is multi-level now
     (indexEntries=98343 against a level-0 pool census of 27538), so that
     equality cannot hold and bIndexComplete is permanently false.

--- NEXT, AND IT IS THE BISECTION THE WHOLE THING TURNS ON ---
marcher-depthgate-pitch40: mode 2 + voxel.March.VerifyDepth 1 at the failing
pose with the QUAD PATH LEFT ON as the reference. The gate already prints
`compared=` beside `miss=` (VoxelMarchRenderer.cpp:2256-2261), and unlike
VerifySource ITS READBACK LANDS -- it delivered 0.1039% vs 0.6687% earlier
tonight.
  miss >> compared  => the traversal is not hitting per-pixel. Traversal defect.
  compared >> miss  => it hits and the pixels do not survive. Emit/depth defect.
Those need opposite fixes and this is the instrument that separates them.

--- THE BISECTION LANDED, AND IT POINTS AWAY FROM THE TRAVERSAL ---
marcher-depthgate-pitch40: mode 2 + VerifyDepth 1, SAME failing pose (alt +40 m,
pitch -40, -VoxelNoClipmap), quad path LEFT ON as the reference, same brick-pack
producer flags. The gate's readback landed on the first try.

  compared = 990,645     miss = 364,192     hitOnSky = 59
  (of ~1,354,896 pixels at the 1552x873 internal target)

THE MARCHER PRODUCES A HIT ON 73% OF PIXELS AT THE POSE WHERE MODE 1 DRAWS AN
EMPTY FRAME. compared >> miss, which is the branch that says the traversal is
NOT the defect: the hits are computed and do not survive to the image.

That retires, on measurement rather than argument:
  * "the marcher misses geometry on downward rays" -- it does not; it hits 73%
    of them at exactly that pitch.
  * the ring-0 localisation question ("entered and missing, or never entered?").
    It is entered and it hits. The question was built on the tile counter, and
    the tile counter never meant what I read it to mean.

WHAT THE GATE ALSO SAYS, and it is a real result in its own right:
  INTERIOR px=6325 disagree=1770 (27.98%) vs control 625 (9.88%), max 1.276 vox
  => FAIL. But every disagreement is under 2 voxels (<0.25=3254, <0.5=1301,
  <1=1731, <2=39, >=2=0). That is a precision/LOD-placement defect, NOT a
  coverage one, and it is a SEPARATE bug from the empty frame. Note the interior
  population is tiny (6,325 px of 990,645 compared; edgeMaskCovers=99.36%),
  because at this pose almost everything is silhouette.

  AND THE EARLIER 0.1039% INTERIOR PASS DOES NOT TRANSFER TO THIS POSE. Same
  gate, same build, different pose: 0.1039% there, 27.98% here. A gate verdict
  is a statement about the pose it was taken at.

--- A CONFOUND IN THE ORIGINAL A/B, RECORDED BECAUSE IT WAS MINE ---
The pair that established "quad draws a mountainside, marcher draws sky" was not
single-variable. Command lines:
  quad control 00067: voxel.Fluid.Enable 1, voxel.March 0.  NO fluid spawn.
  marcher     00065: ... voxel.Fluid.Spawn 40000, -VoxelRetireQuads=1,
                     -VoxelBrickPack=1, -VoxelBrickPackOnCpu=1.
So they differed in the marcher AND in 40,000 PBF particles AND in the producer.
The fluid spawn is not optional -- the marcher's game-thread hookup rides the
fluid tick -- but it is still a difference, and I presented the pair as clean.
It does NOT change the outcome (the marcher arm had quads retired and showed
nothing), and the water is NOT what fills the frame: every Fluid perf line in
that leg reads renderMs=off, so the particles are simulated and never drawn.
The uniform blue-grey is the sky atmosphere with nothing in front of it.

--- NEXT: THE VARIABLE THE ORIGINAL PAIR NEVER ISOLATED ---
marcher-clipmapon-pitch40: mode 1, pitch -40, quads retired -- and the CLIPMAP
LEFT ON. One variable against the failing leg.
The reason to suspect it: the only frame in which anything marcher-shaped has
ever appeared (VoxelVerify00061, pitch -12) is also the only one that had the
clipmap on. A clipmap-on frame has real prepass depth everywhere, and the march
seeds TMax from prepass depth (VoxelMarch.usf:488-497) under a guard whose own
comment warns that a t_max read from an unpopulated depth buffer "would clip
every ray to the near plane and the terrain would simply not be there".
AND A CONTROL IS REQUIRED AFTER IT, because the speckled brown in 00061 may be
the CLIPMAP's own dithered LOD and not the marcher at all: run march 0 with the
clipmap on at the same pose and diff. If those two images match, the marcher has
never put a pixel on screen in mode 1.

================================================================================
2026-08-21  THE TRAVERSAL IS FINE. IT HITS 98.8% OF THE FRAME. IT IS THE EMIT.
================================================================================
marcher-gate-retired: the depth gate run in THE EXACT FAILING CONFIGURATION --
mode 2 + VerifyDepth 1, alt +40 m, pitch -40, -VoxelRetireQuads=1,
-VoxelNoClipmap. So the prepass carries NO terrain depth, which is the state
mode 1 draws an empty frame in.

  miss = 16,003     hitOnSky = 1,338,893     compared = 0
  (of ~1,354,896 pixels)

hitOnSky is "the marcher produced a HIT and the raster had far-plane there". So:

  THE MARCHER HITS 98.8% OF THE PIXELS IN THE FRAME IT DRAWS NOTHING IN.

The VisBuffer is essentially fully populated with valid hits, at the pose, in the
configuration, where mode 1 renders sky. The verify kernel reads the SAME
MarchVis texture through the SAME VoxelMarchUnpackVis the emit reads it with, so
this is not an instrument that could disagree with the emit about bHit.

THE DEFECT IS IN THE EMIT / RESOLVE PATH. The traversal, the index, the brick
pool, the ring cascade and the record validation are all doing their job.

THIS ALSO KILLS THE t_max HYPOTHESIS, which was the last live traversal-side
idea: the march seeds TMax from prepass depth, and the worry was that an
UNPOPULATED prepass would collapse TMax and decline every ray. It does not --
with the prepass empty the marcher still hits 98.8%. Analytically consistent:
ConvertFromDeviceZ at DeviceZ=0 under reverse-Z infinite-far is 1/(0*C - 0),
i.e. +inf, so TMax = min(TExit, inf) = TExit.

--- THE ARITHMETIC THAT SAYS IT IS THE DEPTH TEST OR THE DRAW, NOT THE SHADER
    LOGIC ---
The emit writes OutSceneColor = float4(0,0,0,0) for every pixel it does not
discard, with TStaticBlendState<> (opaque, no blend). So a pixel the emit
actually shaded would end up BLACK in SceneColor unless lighting later adds to
it. THE FRAME IS SKY-COLOURED, NOT BLACK. Therefore SceneColor was never
written at those pixels -- the emit's writes are not landing at all, rather than
landing and being mis-shaded. A failed DEPTH TEST discards colour AND depth
together and would leave exactly this.

Ruled out by reading, with file:line:
  * emitFrames == frames (18630/18630) and Stats.EmitFrames++ is at :4910,
    AFTER GraphBuilder.AddPass at ~:4760 -- so the pass IS being added.
  * the PS's discard cannot be firing: bHit is set unconditionally into P.y
    bit 0 (VoxelMarch.usf:313) and the gate proves it is set for 98.8% of px.
  * the tile quads cover the frame: 21,120 tiles x 64 px = 1,351,680.
  * VS/PS/march agree on pixel space: march writes at DispatchThreadId,
    PS reads In.Position.xy - MarchViewRectMin, VS builds NDC from view-LOCAL
    pixels against a viewport set to the view rect (:4797).

--- ALSO SETTLED, AND IT REMOVES TWO MORE OF MY OWN CLAIMS ---
  * THE CLIPMAP IS NOT THE VARIABLE. marcher-clipmapon-pitch40 (mode 1, pitch
    -40, quads retired, clipmap ON) against the clipmap-OFF leg:
    max channel delta 4, mean 0.2685. Identical. So the one frame that ever
    showed marcher-shaped pixels (00061, pitch -12) did NOT show them because
    the clipmap was on.
  * WHICH MEANS THE SPECKLED BROWN IN 00061 IS STILL UNATTRIBUTED. It is either
    the clipmap's own dithered LOD or the marcher. It needs a march-0 control at
    that pose to say which, and until that control exists NOBODY SHOULD SAY THE
    MARCHER HAS EVER PUT A PIXEL ON THE REAL FRAME. My memory note
    "marcher mode 1 unproven" was right and I talked myself out of it.

================================================================================
2026-08-21  ROOT CAUSE: ResolvedView WAS NEVER RESOLVED. OutDepth WAS NaN.
================================================================================
VoxelMarch.usf used ResolvedView at five sites inside VoxelMarchEmitPS and
NEVER CALLED ResolveView() anywhere in the file.

ResolvedView is NOT an alias for View. UE 5.8, InstancedStereo.ush:52:

    static ViewState ResolvedView = (ViewState)0.0f;

A zero-initialised static that stays zero until something assigns it. Engine
shaders get it from StereoSetupVS/StereoSetupPS. A custom pass that never calls
ResolveView() reads an ALL-ZERO VIEW, and nothing warns: it compiles, it binds,
it runs.

THE CHAIN, end to end:
  ResolvedView.TranslatedWorldToClip == the zero matrix
  -> HitClip = mul(float4(HitTWS,1), 0) = (0,0,0,0)          (VoxelMarch.usf:761)
  -> OutDepth = HitClip.z / HitClip.w = 0/0 = NaN            (:762)
  -> NaN loses EVERY comparison, so the depth test fails for every pixel
  -> AND A PIXEL THAT FAILS THE DEPTH TEST WRITES NEITHER DEPTH NOR COLOUR.

So the emit rasterised the entire screen and threw all of it away in the ROP.

WHY EVERY INSTRUMENT SAID THE MARCHER WAS HEALTHY, which is the part worth
keeping: tiles, emitFrames, marchMs, emitMs and the decline counters ALL
describe work that happens BEFORE the raster output stage. Not one of them can
see a pixel killed by the depth test. And the depth gate and every mode-2 gate
read the VisBuffer DIRECTLY -- they never execute this pixel shader at all. The
feature had a gate for the traversal, a gate for the depth values, a gate for
the source, and NO GATE AT ALL for "did the emit's writes survive the ROP",
which is the one thing that was broken.

IT ALSO EXPLAINS, WITHOUT ANY EXTRA HYPOTHESIS:
  * sky rather than black. The emit writes OutSceneColor = 0 for every shaded
    pixel, so a landing write would have made the frame BLACK. It stayed sky
    because nothing landed.
  * flat vs hierarchical identical to within TSR noise (max delta 4) at 10x the
    march cost -- both feed the same doomed emit.
  * clipmap on vs off identical (max delta 4) -- same reason.
  * "marcher mode 1 unproven: scratch mode runs; drawing into the real frame
    never has." That note was correct. Mode 1 has never drawn a pixel.
  * the depth gate's earlier 0.1039% interior pass was TRUE AND IRRELEVANT to
    coverage -- it compares VisBuffer depth against raster depth and never
    touches the emit.

THE FIX: `ResolvedView = ResolveView();` as the first statement of
VoxelMarchEmitPS. One line. Shader-only -- no C++ change, so it compiles at
editor boot.

AUDIT, because the same shape could be elsewhere: two files in this project
reference ResolvedView.
  VoxelMarch.usf              uses 5, resolves 1  (fixed)
  VoxelQuadVertexFactory.ush  uses 2, resolves 0  -- CORRECT AS IS. A vertex
    factory runs inside the engine's own base-pass shader, which calls
    StereoSetupVS before invoking it. The rule is: whoever owns the ENTRY POINT
    owns the resolve, and VoxelMarch.usf owns its own entry points.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX, AND MUST NOT BE ASSUMED TO:
  * the interior depth disagreement measured at this pose (27.98% vs 9.88%
    control, max 1.276 vox). Separate defect, sub-2-voxel, still open.
  * the FVoxelBrickPool::GetLevelCensus() render-thread data race.
  * the inert SkipLevels chunk-level control arm.
  * the settle gate comparing multi-level index entries to a level-0 census.
  * voxel.Cover.VerifyStore's crash and voxel.March.VerifySource's dead readback.
  * the 4.3x frame-time headline, which STILL must not be quoted: it was
    measured on a renderer that was discarding every pixel it drew.

--- THE EMIT RASTERISES CORRECTLY AND ITS FRAGMENTS NEVER REACH THE FRAME ---
Escalating mutations, each one ruling out the previous suspect. All at the
failing pose (alt +40 m, pitch -40, quads retired, clipmap off), all in mode 1:

  1. emissive magenta written AFTER the !bHit discard      -> 0 magenta px
  2. emissive magenta written BEFORE any discard,
     unconditional, plus OutDepth forced to 0.5            -> 0 magenta px
  3. VS collapsed to a single FULL-SCREEN quad (instance 0),
     tile math bypassed entirely, magenta + depth 0.5      -> 0 magenta px
  4. DrawPrimitiveIndirect replaced by a DIRECT
     DrawPrimitive(0, 2, 21340) over the whole tile grid   -> 0 magenta px

And ProfileGPU on arm 4 proves the geometry IS being rasterised:

  VoxelMarch.Emit(scene)   1 draw | 42,680 primitives | 128,040 vertices | 0.096 ms

42,680 = 21,340 tiles x 2 triangles; 128,040 = 21,340 x 6 vertices. The pass
also sits in the RIGHT PLACE in the frame: VoxelMarch(compute) -> BasePass ->
VoxelMarchEmit -> Lights -> PostProcessing. So the GBuffer it writes is written
before lighting, which is what the design requires.

SO: FRAGMENTS ARE PRODUCED, WITH AN UNCONDITIONAL EMISSIVE WRITE AND A DEPTH
THAT CANNOT FAIL GreaterEqual AGAINST THE SKY'S 0, AND NOTHING REACHES THE
PRESENTED FRAME. The loss is at or after the ROP -- the depth/stencil state or
the render-target bindings -- and NOT in the traversal, the tile compaction, the
vertex shader, the pixel shader's logic, or the draw call form.

A COUNTER I ALMOST MISREAD, RECORDED SO NOBODY ELSE DOES: the same ProfileGPU
row on the INDIRECT arm reads 0 primitives / 0 vertices. That is NOT evidence
the indirect draw is broken -- ProfileGPU takes its primitive count from the
CPU-side draw parameters, and for DrawPrimitiveIndirect the CPU does not know
the count. Treat that zero as "unknown", not as "nothing drawn". The indirect
draw has been RESTORED; it is not implicated by anything measured here.

WHAT IS FIXED AND STAYS FIXED: ResolvedView is now resolved (one line, top of
VoxelMarchEmitPS). It was a genuine defect -- OutDepth really was 0/0 = NaN --
but it is LATENT rather than causal, because the fragments do not survive to be
depth-tested at all. Do not let the fix's presence imply the frame was fixed.

--- WHERE THE NEXT PERSON SHOULD START ---
The question is now narrow enough to name: WHY DOES AN RDG RASTER PASS AT
PostRenderBasePassDeferred, WITH REAL BINDINGS, WRITE NOTHING, WHEN A COMPUTE
PASS ADDED IN THE SAME HOOK ON THE SAME FRAME WORKS?
  1. A PIX or RenderDoc capture of one frame answers this in minutes and every
     black-box leg from here will be slower and less certain. This is the point
     to stop bisecting and go look.
  2. The cheapest code-side suspect is the DEPTH-STENCIL state, because it is
     the one piece of pipeline state that was copied from Nanite rather than
     from this project's own working raster passes:
       TStaticDepthStencilState<true, CF_DepthNearOrEqual, true, CF_Always,
                                SO_Keep, SO_Keep, SO_Replace>
     with stencil ref 0x80 and the DEFAULT StencilWriteMask of 0xFF. Every
     working raster pass in this project (VoxelFluidRender.cpp:860, :929, :1423)
     passes stencil ref 0 and does not enable front-face stencil at all. Try it
     with stencil disabled before trying anything else.
  3. Second suspect: the RenderTargets argument at that hook may not be the
     textures post-processing consumes. VoxelFluidRender is the working
     reference and it composites POST-opaque into its own SV_Targets; nothing in
     this project has ever successfully written the GBuffer from a custom pass,
     which the plan's own risk table called out as "new ground, no prior art".

================================================================================
2026-08-21  THE MARCHER DRAWS TERRAIN. THE SKY WAS PAINTING OVER IT.
================================================================================
VoxelVerify00101 is the first frame in this project's history in which the ray
marcher has put terrain on the screen. Same pose as the quad control
(VoxelVerify00067): spawn -61440,-61440, camera 40 m above ground, pitch -40,
yaw 45, sun frozen 12:00 03-20, 235 s settle, -VoxelNoClipmap, quads retired.

  marcher, sky+fog OFF (00101): 22,117 unique colours, 8.57 MB -- TERRAIN
  marcher, sky+fog ON  (00103):    506 unique colours, 2.77 MB -- BLANK
  quad control         (00067):                        8.86 MB -- TERRAIN

SAME BUILD, ONE VARIABLE (r.SkyAtmosphere 0, r.Fog 0, ShowFlag.Atmosphere 0,
ShowFlag.Fog 0). So the emit's COLOUR writes were landing all along and the
atmosphere pass was painting over them.

--- WHAT THE "BLANK FRAME" ACTUALLY WAS ---
The emit's DEPTH writes do not land. SceneDepth therefore still reads far-plane
where the terrain is, and the sky/aerial-perspective pass, which uses depth to
decide what is sky, covers the whole frame. The colour was always there,
underneath.

CONFIRMED BY FORCING THE DEPTH, not inferred: with sky ON and
`OutDepth = 0.9f` -- a reverse-Z value one step off the near plane, which
NOTHING can legitimately paint over -- the frame is still blank (00105: 481
colours, 2.77 MB). A depth that near cannot be occluded, so the write itself is
being dropped.

--- THE MUTATION LADDER THAT GOT HERE, AND WHY EACH RUNG WAS NEEDED ---
Every one of these rendered NOTHING, and each ruled out the suspect above it:
  1. emissive magenta after the !bHit discard
  2. emissive magenta before any discard, unconditional, + OutDepth 0.5
  3. VS collapsed to one FULL-SCREEN quad, tile math bypassed
  4. DrawPrimitiveIndirect -> direct DrawPrimitive over the whole tile grid
  5. depth-stencil DELIBERATELY UNBOUND, depth test and stencil both off
  6. Params->RenderTargets replaced with the engine's own base-pass
     FRenderTargetBindingSlots, VERBATIM
Rung 6 with sky OFF is what finally showed magenta at 99.99% of pixels -- which
means rungs 1-6 were all being defeated by the SAME later pass, not by six
different causes. THE WHOLE LADDER WAS ONE BUG WEARING SIX MASKS, and the thing
that unmasked it was turning off a pass I had never suspected.

RULED OUT ALONG THE WAY, each on evidence:
  * RDG render-pass MERGING with the base pass. FRenderTargetBinding::
    CanMergeBefore requires DepthStencilAccess == Other.DepthStencilAccess
    (ShaderParameterMacros.h:643-650); the base pass is DepthRead_StencilWrite
    (DeferredShadingRenderer.cpp:2110-2113, full prepass) and the emit asks for
    DepthWrite_StencilWrite, so the two CANNOT merge.
  * r.BasePassWriteDepthEvenWithFullPrepass=1, which flips the base pass to
    DepthWrite_StencilWrite. Still blank (00107).
  * ClearGBufferAtMaxZ, which runs immediately after this hook: it needs
    r.ClearSceneMethod 2 and the default is 1.
  * The bindings, the draw form, the geometry, the tile compaction, the pixel
    shader's logic, the depth test and the stencil test -- rungs 1-6.

--- WHAT IS STILL OPEN, AND IT IS NOW ONE SENTENCE ---
WHY DOES AN RDG RASTER PASS AT PostRenderBasePassDeferred LAND ITS COLOUR AND
NOT ITS DEPTH, ON A DEPTH-STENCIL IT BOUND DepthWrite_StencilWrite?

The architecturally correct answer is probably NOT to keep fighting this hook.
Nanite writes its depth in its OWN pass BEFORE the base pass
(Nanite::EmitDepthTargets), which is exactly the arrangement this hook is
fighting. The marcher already runs its compute march at PreRenderBasePass and
has the VisBuffer in hand there, so a depth-only raster pass at that hook --
where SceneDepth is unambiguously writable -- followed by the existing GBuffer
emit at PostRenderBasePassDeferred with an equal-depth test, is a small change
and matches the engine's own pattern.

DO NOT SHIP r.SkyAtmosphere 0 AS A FIX. It is a diagnostic that proves where the
picture went; the terrain it reveals is lit without atmospheric contribution,
which is why 00101 reads cold and teal rather than sunlit.

================================================================================
2026-08-21  FIXED. THE MARCHER RENDERS WITH THE SKY ON.
================================================================================
  marcher, depth pre-emit, sky+fog ON (00109): 58,154 colours, 9.23 MB
  marcher, before the fix,  sky+fog ON (00103):    506 colours, 2.77 MB
  quad control, same pose             (00067): 31,728 colours, 8.89 MB
Same pose, same settle, quads retired on the marcher arm. marchMs=3.537,
emitMs=0.201, frames==emitFrames==18651, zero declines, zero ensures, no crash.

--- THE FIX, AND IT IS TWO INDEPENDENT DEFECTS, BOTH REAL ---

1. ResolvedView WAS NEVER RESOLVED.
   VoxelMarch.usf used ResolvedView at five sites and never called
   ResolveView(). InstancedStereo.ush:52 declares it
   `static ViewState ResolvedView = (ViewState)0.0f` -- a zero-initialised
   static that ENGINE shaders get filled in by StereoSetupVS/PS. A custom pass
   owning its own entry points must resolve it itself. TranslatedWorldToClip was
   therefore the zero matrix and OutDepth was 0/0 = NaN.
   FIX: `ResolvedView = ResolveView();` at the top of VoxelMarchEmitPS (and in
   the new depth pass).
   NOTE: VoxelQuadVertexFactory.ush also uses ResolvedView without resolving and
   is CORRECT -- a vertex factory runs inside the engine's own base-pass shader,
   which has already resolved it. The rule: whoever owns the ENTRY POINT owns
   the resolve.

2. SceneDepth IS NOT WRITABLE FROM PostRenderBasePassDeferred.
   With a full prepass the base pass runs DepthRead_StencilWrite
   (DeferredShadingRenderer.cpp:2110-2113). A raster pass added at that hook
   lands its COLOUR and SILENTLY DROPS ITS DEPTH. Proven, not inferred: with the
   emit forced to OutDepth = 0.9 -- one step off the near plane, which nothing
   can legitimately paint over -- the frame was STILL sky-covered (00105).
   FIX: a new depth-only raster pass, VoxelMarch.DepthPreEmit, at
   PreRenderBasePass, where SceneDepth is unambiguously writable. It shares
   VoxelMarchEmitVS (same tile quads, same hit list) and writes only SV_Depth.
   This is the engine's own pattern -- Nanite::EmitDepthTargets writes its depth
   before the base pass for exactly this reason. Gated on Arm.Mode == 1 so mode
   2 stays a clean control.

--- WHY THIS WAS SO HARD TO SEE, WORTH KEEPING ---
The two defects MASKED EACH OTHER and both were invisible to every instrument:
  * With depth NaN, colour was rejected too, so the frame looked like the emit
    never ran.
  * With depth fixed but undeliverable, colour landed and the SKY PAINTED OVER
    IT -- so the frame looked identical to the NaN case.
Every counter this module owns -- tiles, emitFrames, marchMs, emitMs, all five
decline counters -- describes work BEFORE the raster output stage. The depth
gate and every mode-2 gate read the VisBuffer DIRECTLY and never execute the
emit pixel shader at all. The feature had a gate for the traversal, for the
depth VALUES and for the source, and NO GATE for "did the picture survive to the
screen", which was the only thing broken. That gap is the lesson.

The unmasking step was turning off a pass nobody had suspected: with
r.SkyAtmosphere 0 + r.Fog 0, an unconditional emissive magenta filled 99.99% of
the frame. Six earlier mutations had all rendered nothing and each looked like a
different bug; they were ONE bug wearing six masks.

--- STILL OPEN, AND NOT TOUCHED BY THIS ---
  * interior depth disagreement at this pose: 27.98% vs a 9.88% control, max
    1.276 voxels. Sub-2-voxel, so placement/LOD, not coverage. Re-run the gate
    now that the renderer actually draws.
  * FVoxelBrickPool::GetLevelCensus() render-thread data race (engine ensure,
    VoxelBrickPool.cpp:646 from VoxelMarchRenderer.cpp PreRenderBasePass).
  * the inert SkipLevels chunk-level control arm (the #if >= 2 and #else blocks
    are byte-identical).
  * the settle gate comparing multi-level index entries to a level-0 census.
  * voxel.March.VerifySource's readback never landing; voxel.Cover.VerifyStore's
    standalone-FRDGBuilder crash.
  * THE FRAME-TIME HEADLINE MUST BE RE-TAKEN. Every previous marcher timing was
    measured on a renderer that was discarding its own output.

================================================================================
2026-08-21  THE FRAME-TIME HEADLINE, RE-TAKEN ON A RENDERER THAT ACTUALLY DRAWS
================================================================================
Every previous marcher timing was measured while the emit was discarding its own
output, so all of them are void. These are the first like-for-like numbers.

TWO LEGS PER ARM, because this project's standing rule is that a single leg is
not a finding. Sanctioned harness (tools/voxel-run-gpu-arm.ps1), 2560x1440,
spawn -61440,-61440, STATIC flight (pins position AND rotation), 90 s preflight
/ 150 s flight, sun frozen 12:00 03-20, -VoxelNoClipmap and -VoxelRiverRibbons=0
in BOTH arms, and -- deliberately -- `voxel.DeferExec 40 voxel.Fluid.Spawn
40000` in BOTH arms, because the marcher's game-thread hookup requires it and an
arm that spawns 40,000 particles against one that does not is the confound that
spoiled the first A/B of the night.

  post-warmup (t >= 10 s)          p50            p95           max
  quad path        leg 1       35.69 ms       52.23 ms      328.99 ms
  quad path        leg 2       35.73 ms       47.82 ms      309.92 ms
  MARCHER          leg 1       15.10 ms       43.30 ms      363.17 ms
  MARCHER          leg 2       14.33 ms       43.53 ms      354.20 ms

  p50: 35.71 -> 14.72 ms   = 2.43x   (28 fps -> 68 fps)
  p95: 50.03 -> 43.42 ms   = 1.15x

REPEATABILITY: the quad arm's p50 varies 0.1% across legs and the marcher's 5%.
Both arms converged identically before the window -- jobsInFlight=0,
pendingJobs=0, tracked=100863 in all four legs.

THE HEADLINE IS 2.4x ON THE MEDIAN, NOT 4.3x. The retracted 4.3x came from
8.14 ms measured on a frame the marcher was not paying for.

--- THE MORE INTERESTING HALF: p95 BARELY MOVES ---
2.43x on p50 against 1.15x on p95, and max frame times ~350 ms in BOTH arms.
So the marcher fixes the MEDIAN frame and does almost nothing for the TAIL,
which means the tail is not the draw path. It cannot be the clipmap (off in both
arms) and it cannot be terrain raster (retired in the marcher arm). The
remaining candidates are streaming/apply hitches and the fluid sim, and that is
now the largest single frame-time item this project has NOT attributed.
DO NOT quote a fps improvement from p50 alone; on this evidence the worst frames
are unchanged.

READING NOTE: the quad arm's own log warns that its median frame (35.7 ms) sits
ABOVE the 33.3 ms hitch threshold, so its hitches=5168 counts frame rate, not
spikes, and is not comparable to the marcher's hitches=994. Judge both on p95.

A COUNTER THAT LOOKS LIKE A CONFOUND AND IS NOT: chunksLoaded=50500 on the quad
arm against 0 on the marcher. `loaded` counts QUAD-POOL publications, which
retirement makes zero by construction. Residency is identical -- tracked=100863
in both -- so this is not the marcher measuring an emptier world.

--- IS THE QUAD PATH ACTUALLY OFF? MUTATION-PROVEN, BOTH DIRECTIONS ---
Asked directly by the owner, and it is the right question -- this project's
recurring failure is an arm that measured nothing while looking healthy.

NEGATIVE CONTROL. Identical to the acceptance capture, ONE flag changed,
voxel.March 1 -> 0, quads still retired:
  marcher ON,  quads retired (00109): 58,154 colours, 9.23 MB -- TERRAIN
  marcher OFF, quads retired (00111):     502 colours, 2.74 MB -- EMPTY
  quad path,   marcher off   (00067): 31,728 colours, 8.89 MB -- TERRAIN
With the marcher off and quads retired NOTHING RENDERS. So every terrain pixel
in 00109 is the marcher's; nothing else in the frame is drawing terrain.

AND THE QUAD PATH IS DEMONSTRABLY IDLE, not merely quiet:
  quad arm:    quads=46,820,051 in the pool
               VoxelTerrainPool cull: drawnQuads=10,896,459 (23.3%)
  marcher arm: retired=1  terrainPoolUsedQuads=0  residentQuads=0  quads=0
               NO `VoxelTerrainPool cull:` LINE AT ALL -- the cull never runs.

AND THE RASTER STAGES CARRY NO TERRAIN. ProfileGPU under the marcher config:
  PrePass          0 draws       0 primitives       0 vertices   0.004 ms
  BasePass         1 draw      960 primitives     559 vertices   0.048 ms
  VoxelMarchEmit   1 draw   42,680 primitives 128,040 vertices   0.099 ms
The quad path historically pushed 17,689,546 primitives through PrePass and
again through BasePass. Both are now zero and 960 respectively; the only
terrain-shaped geometry in the frame is the marcher's own tile quads.

2026-08-22  WAVE 1.1 -- THE MARCHER NO LONGER NEEDS THE WATER SIMULATION
================================================================================
capture-marcher-nofluid: voxel.Fluid.Enable 0, NO voxel.Fluid.Spawn, quads
retired, clipmap off, same acceptance pose as 00109.

  terrain renders (00113): 59,234 colours, 9.25 MB, mean 148.5/144.1/138.8
  fluids-on control (00109): 58,154 colours, 9.23 MB, mean 148.5/144.2/138.9
  frames=23145  emitFrames=23145  marchMs=3.592  indexEntries=98442
  declined noView=0 noVolume=0 noTextures=0 unsupported=0 noPool=0
  ZERO "Fluid perf [run]" lines in the entire log -- the solver never ran.

The two images are indistinguishable at the mean, which is the equivalence
control: the decoupling changed nothing, it merely stopped requiring something.

--- WHAT THE COUPLING ACTUALLY WAS, AND MY FIRST DESCRIPTION OF IT WAS WRONG ---
I wrote that "without fluids the march chunk index is never attached and every
pool removal is silently discarded". THAT IS FALSE for any Game/PIE session:
UVoxelShadowMarchSubsystem::OnWorldBeginPlay (VoxelShadowMarch.cpp:1267) already
attaches the index with no fluid dependency and says so in its own comment.

What a fluid-free run actually lost was THE EXTENSION ITSELF. Both the state and
the view extension were created inside VoxelMarchPublishSource, whose only caller
is the fluid subsystem, so with fluids off there was no marcher to decline.

Three strands, fixed separately:
 1. OWNERSHIP. VoxelMarchEnsureExtension() split out of VoxelMarchPublishSource
    and called from UVoxelWorldSubsystem -- the owner of the brick pool the
    marcher reads. The publish now only refreshes the OPTIONAL volume.
 2. LIVENESS. IsActiveThisFrame_Internal returned State->Volume.IsValid().
    Now source-aware: source 0 still requires a volume, source 1 does not.
    AND THE BASE-CLASS CALL WAS MISSING -- FWorldSceneViewExtension::
    IsActiveThisFrame_Internal was never called, and the volume check was
    masking it. Removing the volume requirement without adding the base call
    would have swapped a fluid coupling for a cross-world PIE bug: one world's
    bricks marched against another world's depth. FVoxelShadowMarchExtension
    makes that call and states the reason (VoxelShadowMarch.cpp:407-410).
 3. BINDING. VOXEL_FLUID_OCCUPANCY_PARAMETERS() rides the SHARED march and emit
    parameter structs, bound unconditionally on both sources by design. A null
    SRV there is an RDG assert, and the EMIT is the pass where it bites: it is
    added with a raw AddPass, so nothing calls ClearUnusedGraphResources. Added
    FVoxelFluidOccupancyVolume::BindNullShaderParameters.

TWO DETAILS IN THAT STAND-IN THAT ARE NOT COSMETIC:
  * FILLED 0xFFFFFFFF, NOT ZERO. The volume's convention is UNBUILT IS SOLID
    (vxc::kFluidVolumeUnbuiltWord). A zero-filled stand-in inverts it, so a
    dispatch that actually read occupancy would render an EMPTY WORLD with every
    counter healthy -- the exact silent-empty failure this file is full of.
    Filled solid, the same mistake draws a box at the camera.
  * DIM 1, NOT 0. Every reader takes FluidVolumeDimVoxels - 1 as a wrap mask
    (VoxelFluidCollision.ush:207, VoxelFluidOccupancy.usf:156) and both carry a
    lint suppression whose premise is that the dim "is never 0 ... and cannot
    underflow". Binding 0 would make that mask 0xFFFFFFFF -- precisely the
    underflow those notes say is impossible. 1 keeps the power-of-two invariant
    and every index those functions can form is 0, which one dword covers.

Also guarded: the frame-origin seed (Volume->GetOriginVoxel()), the ray origin,
and both verify kernels -- the latter by adding Volume.IsValid() to their
enclosing conditions rather than an early return, because those are plain blocks
in the middle of the hook and a return there would have skipped the depth
pre-emit and the entire emit setup below them.

--- THE HARNESS GUARD IS RETIRED, AND THAT IS PART OF THE FIX ---
tools/voxel-run-gpu-arm.ps1 REFUSED any leg that set voxel.March without
spawning fluid. That guard was correct when written and is now wrong: it would
refuse the correct configuration. Retired with the evidence above recorded in
place of it; -NoMarcherHookupGuard kept as an accepted no-op.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 1.2 -- brick-pool lifetime hooked into streaming (CODE, UNBUILT)
================================================================================

THE PLAN'S SITE MAPPING WAS WRONG AND THE CORRECTION IS THE FINDING.

The approved plan said to add FVoxelBrickPool::RemoveChunk inside
ReleaseChunkGeometry (VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp), covering its four callers.
Reading the callers shows that would have freed NOTHING in the shipping config
and would have freed the WRONG chunks in the others:

  1. ReleaseChunkGeometry is reached only from behind FChunkRecord::
     HoldsGeometry(), which is `Component.IsValid() || PoolSlot != INDEX_NONE`
     (:1319). Under -VoxelRetireQuads=1 a terrain record holds NEITHER, so the
     guard is false for every terrain chunk and the hook never runs. The file's
     own comment at :1321-1331 says exactly this about a different caller --
     "IS THERE DRAWN GEOMETRY" and "IS THERE TERRAIN HERE" are not the same
     question -- which is the confusion HoldsTerrain was added to fix. Hooking
     bricks to the geometry release is that same confusion arriving a second
     time, and it would have passed every quad-path test.

  2. The two pre-dispatch skip callers (:11326 buried, :11379 sky band) retire
     records for chunks that were NEVER DISPATCHED, so no brick was ever
     published under those keys. A RemoveChunk there returns false always.

  3. The ApplyMeshResult zero-quad caller (:13367) is a chunk that meshed to no
     visible faces -- a buried SOLID chunk is exactly that -- and its record
     STAYS in ChunkRecords. Its bricks are the ones the marcher needs most.
     Freeing them punches a hole in the volume that rays pass straight through.

WHAT WAS BUILT INSTEAD. A separate ReleaseChunkBricks(Key), called at the one
line that means "this chunk has left residency": immediately before
ChunkRecords.Remove(Key) in DrainUnloads, OUTSIDE the HoldsGeometry gate. Plus
EvictParkedKey, for the parked population.

THE PARKED EXCEPTION IS REAL. Adoption (:9389-9449) re-admits a parked chunk
with NO mesh job, so a parked chunk that lost its bricks at unload would come
back drawable and unmarchable. DrainUnloads therefore skips the brick release
when ParkChunkGeometry returned true, and EvictParkedKey frees them when the
park is given up. Checked the three EvictParkedKey callers: :9444 is the STALE
park (falls through to a normal admit, so a fresh mesh re-adds), :13292/:13345
are the caps, :14686 is the edit path -- all genuine give-ups. The successful
adopt branch does not call it. Parking also requires a pool slot, so it is
already inert under retirement; the split keeps the two mechanisms independent
rather than relying on that.

COUNTERS: released / absent, and ABSENT IS NOT AN ERROR HERE. The cover ring
(VoxelDetailAssetSubsystem.cpp:1593-1639) treats a false RemoveChunk as a
defect and is right to -- it keeps an explicit list of what it added. Terrain
has no such list, and the buried/sky skips legitimately retire records that
never had a brick, so counting those as errors would report tens of thousands
of defects on a healthy run. Read the pair as a ratio instead. New 5 s line:
  "Voxel brick lifetime (5s window): released= absent= | resident0= indexEntries=
   | cumulative released= evictions= writesDropped="
printed unconditionally, because "does anything ever leave this pool" was
answered "no" for the pool's whole existence and nothing said so.

INDEX SIDE ALREADY READY: FVoxelMarchChunkIndex::ApplyDelta applies Removed
before Added and validates slot + resident bit before clearing a cell
(VoxelMarchChunkIndex.cpp:433-488). Already exercised by the cover ring.

COST: FVoxelBrickPool::RemoveChunk is a hash erase, an arena free, a pending
clear and a pending index removal (:1117-1147). The plan's "full grid
rebuild-and-hash in MarkDirtyAndUpload" warning is about the QUAD pool's
RemoveChunk and does not apply.

STATUS: NOT BUILT, NOT MEASURED. Build.bat is blocked by the permission
classifier in this session. No leg has been run; nothing here is a measurement.
Pre-registered gate for when it can run (non-static leg -- walk, dig, place,
walk away past the unload ring, walk back):
  - released > 0 and rising with motion            (structurally zero before)
  - resident0 FALLS when the camera leaves covered ground
  - evictions stays 0                              (was heading for the 131,072 cap)
  - writesDropped small and explained
  - terrain still renders on the walk back         (adoption/re-admit not broken)

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 2 -- the plan's "biome tint is already in hand" is WRONG
================================================================================

The approved plan lists the biome tint face term as needing no plumbing:
"Its only inputs are face axis and sign ... and the marcher already derives
FaceClass". Both halves of that are true and the conclusion still does not
follow.

1. THE FACE TERM IS ALREADY APPLIED, BY A DIFFERENT MECHANISM. The marcher
   shades from VoxelPaletteFace[material * 3 + faceClass]
   (VoxelMaterialPalette.ush:36, VoxelMarch.usf:904) -- the palette is ALREADY
   indexed by face class, and MAT_GRASS carries green on top, darker green on
   the side and brown underneath. Adding BiomeTintForFace on top would apply
   the same geometric top/side/bottom distinction twice. ADR-0008 invariant 4
   ("face class is for MATERIAL difference, not for shading") is the rule that
   says which of the two is the right home, and the palette already is it.

2. WHAT THE QUAD PATH ACTUALLY HAS THAT THE MARCHER DOES NOT IS THE GATE, NOT
   THE TERM. VoxelQuadVertexFactory.ush:373 is
       TerrainTintR = bNearSurface ? FaceTint : 0.0f
   and bNearSurface (:337) is `PositionUU.z > SurfaceZHereRelUU - 200 UU`,
   evaluated against the chunk's fitted surface plane -- ChunkSurfaceZRelUU
   plus the 12+12 packed gradients, reconstructed per pixel. That gate is what
   stops a +Z face two hundred metres down a cave from being tinted as turf.
   The face term without it is not a partial win, it is the wrong answer
   underground.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE WAVE. There is no free half. Every term Wave 2 is missing
-- the surface-proximity gate, temperature and precipitation -- depends on the
SAME per-chunk ChunkParams float4 the quad path already carries
(VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp SampleChunkParamsForPool), so the plumbing is the
whole wave rather than a third of it. Verified by grep: VoxelMarch.usf contains
no occurrence of NearSurface, SurfaceZ, SurfaceGrad, Climate, Temperature or
Precip -- the marcher has none of these terms, and its shading today is
palette(material, faceClass) + per-voxel jitter + corner AO.

SIZING THE CARRIER, CORRECTED. The plan proposed spare dword 7 for temperature
and precipitation and said to extend the record to 12 dwords only "if gradients
are wanted". The gate makes them not optional: temp (1 B) + precip (1 B) +
SurfaceZRelUU (float, 4 B) + packed gradients (24 bits) is ~9 bytes, so dword 7
alone cannot carry Wave 2 and the record has to grow either way.

NOT IMPLEMENTED. Extending FVoxelBrickPool's record is a format change with
THREE writers that a gate byte-compares -- BuildChunkRecord (VoxelBrickPool.cpp
:555), BrickChunkRecordMain (VoxelBrickPoolWrite.usf:219) and
docs/brick-volume-format.md section 6, with voxel.GPU.VerifyBrickPack comparing
two of them -- plus BrickChunkClearMain's hardcoded 8-dword clear and the
`kChunkRecordDwords * 4 == 32` static_assert. Writing that blind, with no
compiler and no leg available this session, is how a silently-wrong format
lands. Specified here, deliberately not written.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 3 "MEASURE FIRST" -- terrain shadows: the answer, and a
                                     correction to what the marcher's 2.43x means
================================================================================

LEG: shadow-baseline-quad. 2560x1440, static flight, spawn -61440,-61440,
sun frozen 12:00 03-20, -VoxelNoClipmap -VoxelRiverRibbons=0, quad path
(marcher off, quads NOT retired), plus -VoxelShadowDistanceM=200.

ANSWER: TERRAIN CASTS SUN SHADOWS, AND IT IS THE MOST EXPENSIVE THING IN THE
GPU FRAME.

  ShadowDepths        3798 draws   20,860,656 primitives   8.337 ms   31.5%
    ShadowDepthPassParallel (4 cascades)
        6 draws       11,806 prim    0.017 ms
       85 draws      221,968 prim    0.116 ms
      793 draws    3,510,496 prim    1.641 ms
     2914 draws   17,116,386 prim    6.552 ms

  VoxelTerrainPool shadow eligibility: shadowView=1 ... castsDynamicShadow=1
  isShadowCast=1 dynRelevance=1 drawRelevance=1 mainPass=1
  VoxelTerrainPool cull: ... shadowGather=1   (alternating with the
  shadowGather=0 main-view cull, i.e. two culls per frame)

THIS RETIRES THE STANDING "shadowGathers=0" NOTE at VoxelGpuPoolComponent.cpp
:927-944, which reasoned from shadowGathers=0 that "GetDynamicMeshElements is
never REACHED with a shadow view". That was true, and it was a CONFIGURATION
effect exactly as VoxelSkySubsystem.cpp:2171-2183 predicted: with
DynamicShadowDistanceMovableLight at 0 there is no cascade to gather for. Pass
-VoxelShadowDistanceM= and the gather happens on the first frame. Nothing in
the proxy was wrong.

THE CORRECTION THAT MATTERS MORE. -VoxelShadowDistanceM= is NOT passed by
default, so IN THE SHIPPED DEFAULT CONFIGURATION TERRAIN CASTS NO SUN SHADOWS
AT ALL -- and every leg behind the marcher's 2.43x p50 headline was taken in
that state. NEITHER ARM PAID ANY SHADOW COST. So:

  - Wave 3 is a NEW CAPABILITY, not a regression to repair. There is no
    shadowing to preserve; there is none today.
  - The 2.43x is not diminished by shadows, but it is also not a comparison of
    two renderers that both cast. Whatever marched shadows cost is NEW cost on
    top of the marcher's current 14.72 ms, and the honest bar for them is the
    8.337 ms / 31.5% the quad path pays for the naive version at this pose.
  - 20.9M primitives for shadows against a main pass drawing ~10.9M says the
    shadow cull is far coarser than the camera cull (one cascade alone draws
    17.1M). A marched shadow has no such pass to be coarse in, which is the
    strongest performance argument for Wave 3 and is now quantified.

DO NOT COMPARE THIS LEG'S ABSOLUTE p50 (28.10 ms) TO THE STORED QUAD BASELINE
(35.71 ms). This leg carries NO fluid spawn; the stored baseline pair ran
voxel.Fluid.Spawn 40000 in both arms. Different configuration, not a faster
quad path. A matched no-shadow control at exactly this configuration is running
next, and the shadow cost must be read from that pair alone.
  this leg (shadows ON):  frames=4935 post-warmup p50=28.10 p95=30.34 max=296.87

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-22  RETRACTION, SAME HOUR: "terrain casts no sun shadows by default"
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WRONG. I wrote that above after ONE leg and the matched control overturned it
within the hour. Recorded rather than edited away, because the mistake is the
same shape this project keeps hitting: I read a single arm as if it were a
comparison.

WHAT -VoxelShadowDistanceM= ACTUALLY DOES IS CONSTRAIN THE CASCADE, NOT ENABLE
IT. Both legs render ShadowDepths; the arg makes it CHEAPER, not present.

  leg                       ShadowDepths                       p50      p95
  shadow-control-noshadow   6532 draws  42,045,362 prim        35.56    36.92
  (DEFAULT, no arg)         16.646 ms   48.1% of GPU frame
  shadow-baseline-quad      3798 draws  20,860,656 prim        28.10    30.34
  (-VoxelShadowDistanceM=200) 8.337 ms  31.5% of GPU frame

Same pose, same everything else, no fluids in either.

THE REAL FINDING IS MUCH LARGER THAN THE ONE I WENT LOOKING FOR. On the quad
path, SHADOW DEPTH RENDERING IS THE SINGLE LARGEST GPU COST IN THE FRAME --
16.6 ms, 48.1%, 42 MILLION primitives against a main pass drawing ~10.9M. The
shadow cull is drawing roughly four times what the camera cull draws. This has
never been measured on this project; the standing note at
VoxelGpuPoolComponent.cpp:927-944 records ShadowDepths passes drawing ZERO
primitives as of 2026-08-19, and that is no longer true.

AND IT PUTS THE 2.43x HEADLINE IN QUESTION. In the stored A/B the quad arm did
NOT retire quads (so it paid this shadow cost) and the marcher arm DID (so its
terrain pool was empty, nothing was gathered for any cascade, and it paid
none). So an unknown but large share of "35.71 -> 14.72 ms" is not the marcher
being faster at drawing terrain -- it is TERRAIN NO LONGER CASTING SHADOWS,
which is a visual regression, not a win.

  If that is what it is, the marched-shadow work in Wave 3 does not ADD cost to
  a 14.72 ms frame -- it gives back a cost the marcher was silently not paying,
  and the flip decision in Wave 4 has to be made against the shadowed number.

NEXT LEG (running): quad path, same pose, no fluids, r.ShadowQuality 0. If p50
falls from 35.56 toward the marcher's 14.72, the headline is mostly shadows and
must be re-taken with both arms casting. If it barely moves, the 2.43x stands
and the shadow cost is being absorbed elsewhere. Either way the current
headline cannot be quoted until this pair is read.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  THE 2.43x HEADLINE IS MOSTLY SHADOWS. Confirmed from the stored A/B
            legs themselves -- no new leg needed to establish it.
================================================================================

Re-read the ProfileGPU tables in the four legs that produced the 2.43x. They
were in the logs the whole time and nobody looked at the ShadowDepths row.

  leg                       ShadowDepths draws / primitives / ms / % of frame   p50
  perf-quad-baseline        6715   42,045,940   15.911 ms   46.6%             35.69
  perf-quad-baseline-r2     6649   42,045,496   15.9   ms   46.3%             35.73
  perf-marcher                 0            0    0.015 ms    0.1%             15.10
  perf-marcher-r2              0            0    0.000 ms    0.0%             14.33

THE QUAD ARM RENDERS 42 MILLION SHADOW PRIMITIVES PER FRAME. THE MARCHER ARM
RENDERS ZERO. The quad arm's shadow pass alone (15.9 ms) is LARGER THAN THE
MARCHER ARM'S ENTIRE GPU FRAME.

Why: the marcher arm runs -VoxelRetireQuads=1, so the terrain pool is empty, so
nothing is gathered for any cascade. Terrain stops casting sun shadows
ENTIRELY. That is not a rendering efficiency; it is a feature being switched
off, and the frame got faster because of it.

ARITHMETIC. The measured p50 gap is 35.71 - 14.72 = 20.99 ms. Of that, 15.9 ms
is a shadow pass the marcher does not run -- roughly 76% of the entire
"improvement". Subtracting it leaves the quad path at ~19.8 ms against the
marcher's 14.72, i.e. of order 1.3x rather than 2.43x. That subtraction assumes
the pass is cleanly removable and the frame is GPU-bound, which is why a direct
r.ShadowQuality 0 leg is running rather than resting on the arithmetic.

WHAT THIS CHANGES.

  1. THE HEADLINE CANNOT BE QUOTED AS IT STANDS. [[voxelsim-marcher-ships-4x]]
     already carries one retraction (4.3x -> 2.4x) for measuring a renderer
     that was discarding its output. This is the second confound in the same
     number, and it is bigger: the two arms are not rendering the same picture.
     An A/B is only an A/B if both arms draw the same scene.

  2. WAVE 3 IS NO LONGER INDEPENDENT AND IS NO LONGER OPTIONAL. The plan orders
     it third and calls marched shadows "a new capability". They are not: they
     are the thing that has to exist before the marcher can be flipped without
     silently deleting sun shadows from the game. Wave 3 should move ahead of
     Wave 4, and Wave 4's flip gate must require both arms to cast.

  3. IT ALSO EXPOSES A LARGE QUAD-PATH WIN NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR. 42M shadow
     primitives against ~10.9M camera primitives means the shadow cull admits
     about four times what the camera cull does. Whatever happens to the
     marcher, that is a ~16 ms pass on the shipped default path with an obvious
     suspect (shadow-view culling is far coarser than frustum culling), and
     -VoxelShadowDistanceM=200 already halves it to 8.337 ms.

  4. p95 IS NOW EXPLICABLE. p95 moved only 1.15x while p50 moved 2.43x. If most
     of the p50 gain is a GPU pass being skipped, and the tail is game-thread
     bound (renderMs=4.7 on hitch frames), then the two numbers were never
     measuring the same thing and the tail was never going to move. That is
     consistent with everything already recorded and needed no new mechanism.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  MEASURED DIRECTLY: the shadow pass is 75% of the marcher's headline
================================================================================

LEG shadow-off-quad. Identical to shadow-control-noshadow in every respect
except `r.ShadowQuality 0`. Quad path, no fluids, static, spawn -61440,-61440,
2560x1440, -VoxelNoClipmap -VoxelRiverRibbons=0.

  quad path, shadows ON (default)   p50 35.56   p95 36.92   ShadowDepths 16.646 ms
  quad path, shadows OFF            p50 19.78   p95 22.68   ShadowDepths (no row)

  delta from the shadow pass alone: 15.78 ms of p50.

The arithmetic in the previous entry predicted ~19.8 ms by subtraction. Measured
19.78 ms. That is close enough to treat the subtraction as sound and the pass as
cleanly removable.

WHERE THAT LEAVES THE HEADLINE. Against the stored marcher p50 of 14.72 ms, a
quad path that is not casting shadows is 19.78 ms -- about 1.34x, against the
2.43x on record. The shadow pass accounts for roughly 75% of the gap.

STILL CONFOUNDED, AND SAYING SO RATHER THAN QUOTING 1.34x. The stored marcher
legs ran voxel.Fluid.Spawn 40000; these quad legs run no fluids at all. 1.34x
compares across that difference and is therefore an estimate, not a result. A
matched marcher arm (no fluids, r.ShadowQuality 0, same pose, same 60/90 s
timings) is running now, two legs, and the number to quote comes from that pair
against a second shadow-off quad leg. Nothing here is a finding until both arms
have two legs.

THE MECHANISM BEHIND THE 42M PRIMITIVES, AND IT IS A BUG-SHAPED THING.
UVoxelGpuPoolComponent's cull emits
  "EMPTY (nothing visible) ... -- falling back to the FULL POOL draw, which is
   the conservative answer"
and in shadow-control-noshadow that line fires 100 times on shadow gathers
against 48 on camera gathers. The camera cull reports visibleQuads of 0.2% or
less for a shadow view and the fallback then submits the WHOLE 46.8M-quad pool
into the cascade. So the shadow pass is not drawing what the shadow frustum
contains; it is drawing everything, every time the cull comes back empty.
"Conservative" is the right instinct for a camera view that must not lose
geometry, and it is the wrong one here -- an empty shadow cull is very often
correct. This is a ~16 ms pass on the shipped default path with a named cause,
independent of anything the marcher does, and it is not on any wave in the plan.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-22  The shadow fallback, read properly before blaming it
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The cull is NOT naively testing shadow casters against the camera frustum --
that trap is already known and already handled. VoxelGpuPoolComponent.cpp
:1850-1874 takes View.GetDynamicMeshElementsShadowCullFrustum(), normalises
each plane and removes GetPreShadowTranslation() before testing, with a comment
naming the exact failure it avoids. That code looks right.

So the sequence is: the shadow cull runs against the correct frustum, rejects
every run (frustumSkipped=2214 of 2214), reports VisibleQuads==0, and the
three-state empty case at :2234 classifies it NothingVisible (not AllCapped,
since SkippedShadowLevel==0) and submits THE ENTIRE POOL.

TWO READINGS, AND I AM NOT PICKING ONE WITHOUT THE LEG THAT SEPARATES THEM:

  (a) The empty result is CORRECT -- this cascade really contains no caster --
      and the fallback is simply the wrong policy for a shadow view. Fix is one
      branch: NothingVisible on a shadow gather draws nothing.

  (b) The empty result is WRONG -- something about the shadow frustum test is
      rejecting geometry it should keep -- and the fallback has been MASKING
      that bug by drawing everything, which produces a correct picture at 16 ms.

(b) is the reading to fear and the one this codebase has been bitten by before:
a conservative fallback that makes a broken cull invisible in the picture. Note
that some shadow gathers DO cull successfully in the same leg
(visibleQuads=110903, ranges=85), which is evidence against a wholesale
coordinate error and for (a) plus a genuinely empty cascade -- but 100 fallback
gathers against 48 camera ones is too many to wave through.

THE SEPARATOR IS CHEAP and needs no new code: the per-level Pool[] / Visible[]
breakdown already logged on the same line says whether the rejected runs were
anywhere near the cascade. If Visible[] is zero at every level while Pool[] is
large AND the camera is looking at lit ground, that is (b).

NOT ON ANY WAVE. This is ~16 ms on the shipped default path, found while
measuring something else, and it belongs in the plan wherever the owner wants
it -- it is independent of the marcher and pays off even if the marcher never
ships.

================================================================================

            WRONG. Matched, the marcher is BETTER than 2.43x, not worse.

I made the same mistake twice in one session: reasoned about a ratio after

THE 2.43x ON RECORD WAS ROUGHLY RIGHT BY ACCIDENT: two large confounds pulling
the same way partly cancelled. Removing both makes the marcher look BETTER

WAVE 4.1 IS ANSWERED, AND IT IS THE FLUID SIM. The plan lists "attribute the p95

as the frame rate the game will see.

ONE LEG PER ARM SO FAR. Leg 2 of each is running. Not a finding until both land.

2026-08-22  Two plan claims confirmed live, from the marcher leg's own log
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. THE GI ANCHOR REALLY IS THE EMPTY STAND-IN POOL. drawpath-marcher-noshadow-1
   logs, under full retirement:
     "VoxelGI volume: ANCHORED to the TERRAIN pool 'VoxelTerrainPool' at
      (-6151040, -6167680, 210560); 1 registered pool component(s) in this
      world, 0 declined by identity."
   with terrainPoolUsedQuads=0 and residentQuads=0 in the same window. So the
   pool exists solely to be found by GI, exactly as the plan states, and Wave
   4.3 cannot delete GetOrCreateGpuPool until GI has another origin. The
   replacement is straightforward -- VoxelGI wants Pool->GetComponentLocation(),
   and UVoxelWorldSubsystem sets that location from GpuPoolRebase =
   FirstChunkOrigin (VoxelWorldSubsystem.cpp:12516-12517), so the anchor can
   come from the subsystem directly. NOT WRITTEN: GpuPoolRebase is ZeroVector
   until the first chunk lands, so a direct read needs a validity flag that does
   not exist yet; without one GI would silently anchor at the world origin
   during startup, which is the "healthy counters, wrong world" failure this
   project keeps meeting.

2. THE MARCHER'S WHOLE TERRAIN DRAW COST, from the same leg's ProfileGPU:
     VoxelMarch                 3.204 ms
       VoxelMarch.March(...)    3.169 ms
       VoxelMarch.CompactTiles  0.007 ms
       VoxelMarch.DepthPreEmit  0.023 ms
     VoxelMarchEmit             0.116 ms
       VoxelMarch.Emit(scene)   0.113 ms
   ~3.3 ms for all terrain, against the quad path's BasePass plus a 15.8 ms
   shadow pass.

3. NO WATER AT THIS POSE. Zero water-pool cull lines in any of today's legs, so
   the matched pair is uncontaminated by water rendering -- and Wave 5's
   "measure the water pool's cost" leg CANNOT use this spawn. It needs a
   low-altitude water pose; water only meshes within ~25 m of the camera. The
   river trunk at -160632,-85613, alt 10 m (tools/bv12-river-captures.ps1
   'ladder-10m') is the candidate.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-22  WAVE 1.2 completeness check: is DrainUnloads the ONLY leak point?
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Yes. ChunkRecords is emptied at exactly two sites:

  :14616  DrainUnloads               -- hooked by Wave 1.2
  :9896   the bounded-admission drop -- CANNOT hold bricks

The second is already guarded by `!Rec->HoldsTerrain(Entry.Key)`, which queries
the brick pool rather than the record's drawn geometry, with a comment saying
why: "a chunk resident in the brick volume but holding no quads is NOT
droppable -- dropping it would re-admit it next tick, forever. Under quad
retirement that is every chunk." So a record reaching that Remove has no brick
by construction and there is nothing to free.

That makes the Wave 1.2 hook complete rather than merely plausible, and it is
worth stating because "I found the obvious site" and "I found every site" have
been different answers on this codebase before.
