--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-22  RETRACTION: the full-pool fallback is NOT what costs 15.8 ms
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Settled from data already on disk, with no new leg, exactly as the previous
entry said it could be. The answer is neither (a) nor (b): the fallback is a
STARTUP TRANSIENT and is not running during the measured window at all.

  All 100 "EMPTY (nothing visible) shadowGather=1" lines in
  shadow-control-noshadow fall inside 04:05:12.631 - 04:05:13.762 -- a 1.1
  SECOND window, ~2.5 minutes before the leg's post-warmup period ends at
  04:07:51. The one such gather that carries a level breakdown shows pool
  L0=4 L1=32257, i.e. a world that had barely begun streaming. An empty cull
  there is correct.

  In steady state the shadow cull works and reports real numbers:
    04:07:42  visibleQuads=611,836   (1.3%)  ranges=361
    04:07:46  visibleQuads=611,836   (1.3%)  ranges=361
    04:07:51  visibleQuads=5,722,657 (12.2%) ranges=2064

So the 42M shadow primitives are LEGITIMATE WORK, not a fallback artefact: four
cascades over a 400 m shadow distance, each admitting up to 12.2% of a
46.9M-quad pool, is simply a lot of geometry. 5.72M quads is ~11.4M triangles
for ONE cascade, and four of them reach the observed total.

MY EARLIER FRAMING WAS WRONG and is withdrawn. I counted 100 fallback lines
against 48, saw a big number in ProfileGPU, and joined them without checking
WHEN either happened. The counting was right; the causal link was invented.
Third time today that reading one number and inferring a mechanism produced a
wrong answer -- and the third time the data needed to refute it was already in
the log.

WHAT THE REAL LEVERS ARE, then:
  - CASCADE DISTANCE. -VoxelShadowDistanceM=200 against the 400 m default takes
    the pass 16.646 -> 8.337 ms. Measured, halves it, costs nothing but shadow
    reach.
  - SHADOW LEVEL CAP. voxel.Stream.GPUShadowMaxLevel already exists and read 4
    in these legs with cappedRuns=0, so it is not currently removing anything.
    Coarse rings casting into far cascades is the obvious candidate and it is
    already instrumented.
  - The fallback branch is still worth a look for correctness, but it is a
    boot-window behaviour and NOT worth 15.8 ms of anyone's attention.

2026-08-22  WAVE 5 "measure first" -- ATTEMPTED, NO MEASUREMENT. Pose problem.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

LEG water-cost-control: marcher arm, r.ShadowQuality 0, 'surface' flight at the
river trunk -160632,-85613 (tools/bv12-river-captures.ps1 'ladder-10m'), river
ribbons left ON deliberately.

  post-warmup p50=7.73 p95=71.88 max=314.25 frames=5586
  water pool cull lines: ZERO. No UVoxelGpuPoolComponent for water was ever
  created.

NOT A TILE PROBLEM -- the fine tier reports resident=1 tile, loaded=1,
absentOnDisk=0, blockingLoads=0, and the water subsystem is armed and enabled
("BathyField: armed", "RippleField: armed", "Baked water tier ENABLED", "Basin
ledger ENABLED"). Terrain streamed correctly. There simply was no water within
meshing reach at any point on this flight.

That is consistent with [[voxelsim-near-field-water-reach]]: water only meshes
within about +/-25.6 m of the camera, so "fly near a river on the map" is not
the same as "have water quads in the frame". The 'surface' flight mode's
altitude was never verified against that reach before spending the leg.

SO WAVE 5 STILL HAS NO COST MEASUREMENT, and the plan's claim that there is "no
measured frame cost for the water pool anywhere in the repo" still stands. What
this leg DID establish is the prerequisite nobody had written down: THE POSE
MUST BE VERIFIED TO PRODUCE WATER POOL COMPONENTS BEFORE THE A/B IS WORTH
RUNNING. The cheap check is one capture at the candidate pose grepped for a
water-pool cull line -- not a 3-minute perf leg.

Also noted, unrelated but real: p95=71.88 ms on a MOVING flight against 9.2-9.6
ms on the static legs at -61440. The tail on this project is dominated by
camera motion (streaming/apply), which is consistent with max frames not moving
when shadows or fluids were removed.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-22  The shadow level cap is NOT the lever. Checked before recommending.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The previous entry named voxel.Stream.GPUShadowMaxLevel as "the obvious
candidate" for cutting the 15.8 ms shadow pass, on the reasoning that coarse
rings casting into far cascades would be waste. The per-level breakdown already
on the same log line says otherwise:

  visiblePreCap: L0=611,836    L1=0        L2..L5=0
  visiblePreCap: L0=611,836    L1=0        L2..L5=0
  visiblePreCap: L0=5,122,276  L1=600,381  L2..L5=0

SHADOW-VISIBLE GEOMETRY IS ESSENTIALLY ALL LEVEL 0. L2 through L5 contribute
NOTHING to any shadow gather in the leg, and L1 only on the largest one. The
cap already reads 4 with cappedRuns=0, and lowering it to 1 -- or even 0 --
would remove at most the 600k L1 quads out of 5.7M. The coarse rings are not
casting, so there is nothing there to stop.

So the 15.8 ms is level-0 quads inside a 400 m cascade, and the levers are only:
  - CASCADE DISTANCE (measured: 400 m -> 200 m halves the pass), or
  - FEWER LEVEL-0 QUADS, which is what the marcher does by not having quads.

Recorded because "the obvious candidate" was mine, was wrong, and the data that
refuted it was on the line I had already quoted twice.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  CAPTURE SET for the owner: what the flip actually looks like
================================================================================

Three captures, IDENTICAL pose and sun, for the owner to judge. Conditions
stated; no verdict offered here.

  pose    spawn -61440,-61440, +40 m, pitch -20, yaw 45
  shutter cam loc=(-6143981,-6143981,222831) rot=(pitch -20.0 yaw 45.0)
          -- logged identically in all arms, so this is the same frame
  sun     FROZEN 08:00 on day-of-year 78 (epoch 37200.000 s), chosen LOW so
          that shadow presence/absence is visible at all; the standard 12:00
          acceptance pose casts short shadows and would understate it
  common   2560x1440, -VoxelNoClipmap, -VoxelRiverRibbons=0, 150 s settle,
          fully settled in every arm (jobsInFlight=0 pendingJobs=0,
          peak loaded=50591)

  A  VoxelVerify00115  QUAD path, shadows ON (today's default)
                       571 shadow gathers, last one visibleQuads=4,378,397 (9.3%)
  B  VoxelVerify00117  MARCHER, retired=1 terrainPoolUsedQuads=0
                       residentQuads=0 brickPacks=95,216 -- casts no shadows
  C  VoxelVerify00118  QUAD path, r.ShadowQuality 0 -- the separator

WHY C EXISTS. A vs B changes TWO things at once (the renderer AND the presence
of sun shadows), and imgdiff says so plainly: 84.74% of pixels differ, mean
delta 55.64/255, CONCENTRATION 0.11 against 0.10 for "perfectly diffuse". By
that tool's own doctrine a diffuse difference is the signature of a global
change and cannot be attributed to one variable. C splits the pair:

  A vs C = the shadow contribution alone (same renderer)
  C vs B = the renderer contribution alone (neither casting)

  RESULTS OF THE SPLIT (imgdiff, threshold 8/255):

    A vs C  SHADOW ALONE, same renderer
            98.38% of pixels differ, mean max-channel delta 86.01/255
            CONCENTRATION 0.10 (perfectly diffuse)
    C vs B  RENDERER ALONE, neither casting
            89.79% of pixels differ, mean max-channel delta 45.58/255
            CONCENTRATION 0.11
    A vs B  both variables together
            84.74% differ, mean delta 55.64/255, CONCENTRATION 0.11

  TONE (mean RGB over the whole frame, and unique colour count):

    A  quad + shadows      colours 10,180   mean  39.8 / 43.4 / 52.5
    C  quad, no shadows    colours 38,239   mean 125.8 / 111.4 / 96.6
    B  MARCHER             colours 51,869   mean  90.4 / 79.3 / 67.9

READ IT AS FOLLOWS, AND NO FURTHER -- the look is the owner's call.

  * LOSING SUN SHADOWS IS THE LARGEST SINGLE VISUAL CHANGE MEASURED HERE:
    98.4% of pixels, mean delta 86/255, and the frame mean goes from 39.8 to
    125.8. At an 08:00 sun most of this scene is in shadow, so the shadowed
    frame is dark and blue and the unshadowed one is bright and warm. Whatever
    the marcher's merits, shipping it today removes THAT.
  * THE RENDERER DIFFERENCE ON ITS OWN IS ALSO LARGE (89.8%, mean delta 45.6)
    and is Wave 2 in a picture: the marcher's shading is not at parity. Note
    the marcher sits BETWEEN the two quad arms in tone (90.4 vs 125.8 with the
    same lighting and no shadows in either), i.e. the marcher is rendering the
    same unshadowed scene meaningfully DARKER than the quad path does.
  * BOTH diffs are diffuse (0.10-0.11 against 0.10 for "perfectly diffuse"), so
    neither is a localised artefact and neither can be attributed further from
    these images alone. That is expected here -- both variables are global -- but
    it does mean these numbers size the change without diagnosing it.
  * The marcher's 51,869 unique colours against the quad path's 38,239 at the
    same lighting is consistent with ADR-0008 invariant 3 (per-voxel hue and
    lightness jitter) being present and working.

2026-08-22  WAVE 2's ORDER-CRITICAL DEPTH GATE -- attempted at the cascade
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

LEG depthgate-cascade: voxel.March 2, HTileProbe 0, Source 1, Rings 1,
SkipLevels 2, StepBudget 3328, VerifyDepth 64, Stats 1; quads NOT retired (the
gate needs raster depth as its reference, which is exactly why this can never
run after the flip); -VoxelBrickPack=1 -VoxelBrickPackOnCpu=1 -VoxelNoClipmap
-VoxelRiverRibbons=0, static, -61440,-61440.

Mode 2 is confirmed live -- the scratch GBuffer targets are allocated in the log
(VoxelMarch.Scratch.GBufferA..E, Velocity, 1552x880). But the gate line reads

  "Voxel march depth gate: NOT RUN (voxel.March.VerifyDepth 0, or no readback
   has landed yet). This is not a pass."

and did so on the only stats block emitted during the run.

NOT CALLED A RESULT EITHER WAY. Two candidate explanations and I did not
separate them before the session ended:
  - the stats block prints on its own cadence and simply had not emitted again
    after the 64-frame window closed, or
  - the readback never lands -- which would put VerifyDepth in the same class as
    the already-open "voxel.March.VerifySource readback never lands" item, and
    would mean this gate has silently not been runnable for some time.

The second would matter a great deal, because the plan leans on this gate as
the ONE check that cannot be deferred past the flip. Worth an hour before Wave 2
starts, not after. The instrument's own text is the thing to trust here: it
already refuses to call an absent readback a pass, which is the right design and
is why this is a clean "unknown" rather than a false green.

  REFINEMENT after the leg completed: the stats block was emitted EXACTLY ONCE
  in the whole 90 s run (one "mode2frame" line, one gate line). So the gate
  never had a second opportunity to publish after its 64-frame window closed,
  and the single reading available is from before it could have. That does not
  prove the readback lands -- but it does mean this leg CANNOT distinguish the
  two explanations, and the immediate obstacle is the stats cadence rather than
  the gate itself. Whoever picks this up should force repeated stats output (or
  shorten VerifyDepth's window) before concluding anything about the readback.

THE absent COUNTER VINDICATES THE DESIGN CHOICE. released:absent runs about
20:1 (13,100 : 647). Had I copied the cover ring's rule that a false RemoveChunk
is a defect, this leg would have reported 647 "errors" in five seconds and
several thousand over the run, all of them correct behaviour -- records the
buried/sky skips retired without ever publishing a brick. The pair is a ratio,
as designed.

NOTE ON PREFLIGHT: released is exactly 0 for every pinned window and non-zero in
the first window after motion begins. That is the falsifier working in both
directions -- a counter that read non-zero on a pinned camera would mean
something was being freed that should not be.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 1.2b -- load-before-unload never ran for the marcher.
            TWO gates, one assumption, and only the far one was visible.
================================================================================

FOUND BY THE OWNER WATCHING A LEG, not by an instrument: "the moving camera
quickly outpaces the terrain streaming ... chunks are missing and it falls
behind ... performance was better with streaming with the previous quad
renderer."

CONFIRMED IMMEDIATELY FROM THE LEG'S OWN LOG. wave12-brick-lifetime-1, 150 s of
LINE flight with voxel.Stream.LodRetentionMs configured (retentionMs=10000):

  held=0  covRelSettled=0  covRelAbsent=0  capRel=0

Not small -- never reached, once, in the whole run. On the quad path that line
is busy. The coverage gate PR #160 added to fix ring-gap holes has therefore
NEVER EXECUTED for the marcher.

WHY IT ONLY SURFACED NOW. Nothing ever freed from the brick pool, so a chunk's
bricks stayed resident forever once visited and the marcher kept drawing
footprints streaming had long since dropped. The leak was an unbounded
accidental cache and it hid the missing gate completely. Wave 1.2 made removal
exact, and the gap became holes. WAVE 1.2 DID NOT CAUSE THIS; IT STOPPED HIDING
IT.

THERE WERE TWO GATES AND FIXING THE OBVIOUS ONE CHANGED NOTHING.

  GATE 1 (DrainUnloads, :14465)  `if (Rec->HoldsGeometry())` -- the retention
      verdict, the unload budget and the geometry release all sit inside it.
      Fixed first: split into bHoldsGeom / bHoldsTerrain, so retention applies
      to brick-holding records while the BUDGET stays geometry-only (a brick
      free is a hash erase batched into the tick's Flush; charging it an unload
      slot would throttle removal to MaxUnloads=4 per frame against the
      ~2,600/s the marcher retires, and the pool would grow again).
      RESULT: held/capRel/covRelSettled STILL EXACTLY 0. Leg
      wave12b-retention-1. The fix was correct and insufficient.

  GATE 2 (RecomputeDesiredSet, :9076)  `if (bLodTransition && Rec.HoldsGeometry()
      && Rec.LastQuadCount > 0)` -- this is where RetainReplaceDir is STAMPED.
      Both halves are quad-shaped: HoldsGeometry() is false under retirement and
      LastQuadCount is 0 for every terrain chunk on that path. So no eviction was
      ever MARKED as an LOD transition, and DrainUnloads' verdict was
      NotRetained regardless of which gate guarded it.

The site comment justified the LastQuadCount test with "a zero-quad chunk shows
nothing, so there is no hole to bridge". Under the marcher that is precisely
inverted: a chunk with zero quads and resident bricks IS drawn, and dropping it
is the hole.

FIX: `Rec.HoldsGeometry() ? (Rec.LastQuadCount > 0) : Rec.HoldsTerrain(LevelKey)`
-- quad semantics preserved byte-for-byte, brick case added only where the
record holds no drawn geometry.

THE LESSON, WHICH IS THE THIRD INSTANCE THIS SESSION. "Is there a drawn
primitive here" and "is there terrain here" are different questions, HoldsTerrain
exists because of that, and the codebase still had two more places asking the
wrong one. Grepping HoldsGeometry() is now the cheap way to find the rest.

GATE FOR THE VERIFYING LEG (wave12c-retention-1): held / covRelSettled /
covRelAbsent / capRel must go NON-ZERO under motion. They have been exactly 0
across two prior legs, so this is falsifiable in the direction that matters.

  WAVE 1.2b RESULT (leg wave12c-retention-1, both gates fixed, LINE flight):

    retention counters, previously EXACTLY ZERO on every marcher leg ever taken:
      held=1024  covRelSettled=620  covRelAbsent=1842  capRel=0
    The mechanism is live for the first time.

    coverage verifier, same flight, same pose:
      before (wave12b, gate 2 unfixed)   holes 20,456 / 20,690 / 19,829 / 18,883
      after  (wave12c, both fixed)       holes 11,995 / 12,743 / 13,899 / 14,638
      scanned ~23,880 in both

    ROUGHLY A THIRD OF THE HOLES ARE GONE. That is a real improvement and it is
    NOT the whole story -- the static legs report holes=6 (marcher) and holes=0
    (quad), so ~13,000 of ~23,880 columns are still uncovered under motion.

MOTION IS THE VARIABLE, AND IT PREDATES WAVE 1.2. Hole counts by flight mode:
    drawpath-marcher-noshadow-1   STATIC   holes=6      (marcher)
    shadow-off-quad               STATIC   holes=0      (quad)
    water-cost-control            SURFACE  holes=6,773  (marcher, BEFORE Wave 1.2 built)
    wave12b-retention-1           LINE     holes=18,883 (marcher, after Wave 1.2)
    wave12c-retention-1           LINE     holes~13,300 (marcher, retention live)
  So holes under motion existed before any of this wave; exact brick freeing
  made them worse, and retention has taken a third of that back. The residue is
  a THROUGHPUT problem -- the camera outrunning chunk generation -- which is the
  known ~600 chunks/s plateau and is not a rendering defect.

CAVEAT ON THE COVERAGE VERIFIER ITSELF, which must be stated before anyone
quotes these numbers further. Its test (b) -- "a coarser stand-in is still on
screen" -- reads Flags.bAnyGeomNonDeep, which is
`Pair.Value.HoldsGeometry()` (:10445). That is FALSE for every terrain record
under retirement, so test (b) can never pass and the verifier OVER-REPORTS holes
on the marcher. Test (a) still works via bAnySettledNonDeep (bMeshSettled is set
on the brick path), which is why the number is not simply 100%. The before/after
comparison above is sound because both legs share the same bias; the ABSOLUTE
rate is not, and this counter is a THIRD instance of the HoldsGeometry-vs-
HoldsTerrain confusion. Two more benign instances at :6668 and :15620 are
reporting-only ("loaded = has a live component"), already known to read 0 under
retirement.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-22  RETRACTION: "roughly a third of the holes are gone" was a windowing
                        error. Retention buys ~5-10%, and costs p95.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I compared wave12c's EARLY-CLIMB windows against wave12b's LATE-PLATEAU windows
and reported the difference as an effect. Same mistake as the shadow/fluid
confounds earlier today, in a fourth costume: comparing unlike things. The full
series, matched position for position:

  window #    22     23     24     25     26     27     28  ...  plateau   final
  12b (off)  10697  12029  13229  14009  14820  15594  16297 ... 19.5-20.7k 18883
  12c (live)  9027  10344  11368  11995  12743  13899  14638 ... 18.9-20.0k 18113

Both legs are identical in shape: high during the cold fill, down to 7-14 while
the camera is PINNED through preflight, then a steady climb the moment the
flight starts, plateauing at ~19-20k of ~23,900 columns.

RETENTION'S REAL EFFECT: about 1,000-1,700 fewer holes at matched points during
the climb -- roughly 5-10% -- and a PLATEAU THAT IS ESSENTIALLY UNCHANGED
(19.5-20.7k vs 18.9-20.0k). It does not fix what the owner saw.

AND IT IS NOT FREE:
    p50   8.18 -> 7.93 ms   (marginally better)
    p95  72.76 -> 90.71 ms  (25% WORSE)
Holding stand-ins keeps more records resident and more work in flight. That is
a real cost for a small benefit.

THE CODEBASE ALREADY SAID WHY, AT THE RETENTION SITE ITSELF: "capRel large =>
the replacement is genuinely not streaming in inside LodRetentionMs, which is a
THROUGHPUT problem (the ADR-0006 funnel), not a retention bug: RETENTION CAN
ONLY DELAY A HOLE, NEVER MANUFACTURE A CHUNK." That is exactly this. The holes
climb monotonically from the instant the camera moves and plateau where
generation throughput balances camera speed -- the known ~600 chunks/s ceiling.

WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT CHANGE:
  * Wave 1.2b is still CORRECT and stays. Load-before-unload was dead code for
    the marcher, that was a genuine defect, and the counters now prove it runs
    (held=1024 covRelSettled=620 covRelAbsent=1842 against exactly 0 before).
    A mechanism that is supposed to exist should exist.
  * But it must NOT be presented as the fix for "the camera outpaces streaming".
    It is not. The owner's observation is the streaming throughput plateau, and
    no amount of eviction policy will close it.
  * The p95 regression is worth one more leg before this is called done: if
    LodRetentionMs is the knob, a shorter retention may keep the small hole
    benefit without the tail cost.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 3 (S2) -- the light-function injection is ALREADY BUILT
================================================================================

Discovered while planning the wave, not by writing it: docs/shadow-march-design-
2026-08-20.md section 9 records S2 as "BUILT, awaiting material + build + legs",
and all three preconditions are already met on this box:

  * VoxelShadowMarch.cpp mode 2 is REAL, not a stub -- it loads the parent
    material, creates the persistent PF_R8 render target, wires an MID via
    SetLightFunctionMaterial and sets DisabledBrightness = 1. Verified by
    reading :1424-1502, not by trusting the doc.
  * The checked-in material EXISTS: ue-project/Content/Voxel/M_VoxelSunShadowLF
    .uasset, 7,691 bytes, dated 2026-08-20.
  * It is in the built binary: VoxelShadowMarch.cpp is 2026-08-20 07:13 and
    UnrealEditor-VoxelEarthShaders.dll postdates it.

So Wave 3 is not a build, it is a LEG SEQUENCE.

AND THE CVAR HELP WAS LYING. voxel.Shadow.March's own description said
"2 = reserved for S2 (light-function injection). NOT BUILT: runs as mode 1 and
warns once". That text was written when mode 2 was a stub and never revisited
when the injection landed, so the ONE PLACE an operator looks to find out what
the switch does was telling them the feature did not exist. Anyone planning this
wave from the cvar help -- which is exactly what a reasonable person does --
would have concluded there was a renderer to write. Fixed 2026-08-22; the
replacement text says what it does and records that it was stale, because a
switch whose whole purpose is to be set by someone reading it cannot have
decorative documentation.

LEG ORDER, taken from the design doc and NOT reordered for convenience:
  I0   mode 2 + voxel.Shadow.MarchMutateInjection 1. Fills the target with 0.5
       instead of the mask, so DIRECT SUN MUST READ HALF-DARK EVERYWHERE. Run
       FIRST: no capture is trusted before the chain is proven able to fail.
  Cal-A/Cal-B  voxel.Shadow.March 0, cascades on, two captures at one pose --
       the raster's own replicate noise, which is the bar V1 is judged against.
       Run, not assumed.
  V1   mode 2 + voxel.Stream.GPUShadowMaxLevel -1 -- the replacement look.
       Captures + conditions to the owner, no verdict from me.

STATED LIMITATIONS OF THIS STAGE (from the design doc, to accompany any V1
capture -- these are the design's own scope, not defects): 64 m reach, so
casters beyond it cast nothing; hard edges, no penumbra until cone jitter lands;
water, translucency and volumetric fog receive no marched shadows because those
are atlas-only light-function systems; props still cast via CSM unchanged.

  I0 (MUTATION ARM) -- the injection chain is LIVE. Census from
  capture-s2-I0-mutation.log:

    "INJECTION LIVE: sun light function = MID of
     /Game/Voxel/M_VoxelSunShadowLF.M_VoxelSunShadowLF, param
     'VoxelSunShadowMask', target 1552x880 PF_R8. Prove it with
     voxel.Shadow.MarchMutateInjection 1 before trusting any capture."

    "shadowinject: copies=0 mutated=602 declinedNoTarget=1
     [MUTATION ARM ACTIVE -- direct sun must read HALF-DARK everywhere; a
      normal-looking frame means the injection is NOT live]"

  copies=0 with mutated=602 is exactly right for this arm: the mutation
  REPLACES the copy, so a non-zero copies here would mean the real mask was
  also landing and the arm was not isolating anything. declinedNoTarget=1 is
  the single startup frame before the render target existed.

  The material loaded, the MID is wired onto the sun, the parameter name
  matched (SetTextureParameterValue on a missing name is a silent no-op, which
  is why the census prints the name it actually used), and the target is PF_R8
  as the copy's format match requires. Every link in the chain is confirmed by
  something other than "it did not crash".

  The image is the remaining half of I0 and is judged on ONE question: is
  direct sun half-dark everywhere? That is a mutation proof, not a look
  judgement -- if the frame looks normal, the injection is not live whatever
  the counters say, and no later capture in this wave could be trusted.

  I0 VERDICT: PASS. VoxelVerify00121, same pose and sun as every other capture
  in this set, fully settled (jobsInFlight=0 pendingJobs=0).

    marcher, NO shadow march (00117)   colours 51,869   mean 90.4 / 79.3 / 67.9
    I0, mask forced to 0.5   (00121)   colours 33,846   mean 63.8 / 57.4 / 52.9

  A large, global darkening in the direction the mutation predicts. It is not
  exactly half the frame mean and should not be: the light function scales the
  DIRECT SUN term only, while sky, ambient and the tonemapper are untouched, so
  a 50% cut to one term lands as a ~29% cut to the frame. The falsifier was
  "a normal-looking frame means the injection is NOT live"; the frame is
  emphatically not normal.

  Together with the census (INJECTION LIVE naming the material, the parameter
  and the PF_R8 target; copies=0 mutated=599 declinedNoTarget=0) every link is
  now confirmed by something other than absence of a crash. Captures taken
  later in this wave can be trusted to be showing the injection.

  NEXT, IN ORDER: Cal-B (a second raster capture at the identical config to
  00115, giving the replicate-noise bar V1 must be judged against -- run, not
  assumed), then V1 (mode 2, real mask, voxel.Stream.GPUShadowMaxLevel -1).

  SHADOW MARCH CENSUS from the same I0 leg, and the headline is the cost:

    shadowmarch: mode=2 reach=64.0m budget=2048 offsetVox=0.50 frames=600
      gpuMs mean=0.409 max=0.559 n=440
      per-frame: considered=1,354,896  sky=867  far=840,288  backface=263,826
                 offbox=0  marched=249,915
      hit%=100.0  inside/f=124.32 (near=0 mid=0 far=124)  exhausted/f=0.00
      steps/marched mean=3.4  p95edge<=8  max=368
      idxEntries=98,452  declines nv=0 ns=0 np=0 ni=0 nt=0

  MARCHED SUN SHADOWS COST 0.41 ms. The quad path's ShadowDepths pass costs
  15.8 ms at 2560x1440 for the same sun. That is a ~38x difference, and it is
  the strongest performance argument this project has produced for the marcher
  -- stronger than the draw-path ratio, because it is a like-for-like feature
  comparison rather than one arm not doing the work at all. Every decline
  counter is zero and the step budget never binds (exhausted/f=0.00, steps
  mean 3.4 against a budget of 2048), so this is not a cheap number bought by
  quietly refusing rays.

  BUT READ far=840,288 BEFORE CELEBRATING. 62% of the frame is beyond the 64 m
  reach and is skipped, counted 'far', and READ LIT. Only 249,915 pixels (18%)
  are actually marched; 263,826 (19%) are backfacing and shadowed trivially.
  At this pose -- 40 m up, pitch -20 -- the ground inside 64 m is a foreground
  strip, so V1 here will show marched shadows over a small part of the frame
  and conventional lighting everywhere else. That is the design's stated v0
  limitation, not a defect, and the 0.41 ms is a cost measured over that 18%.

  THE REACH CANNOT SIMPLY BE RAISED. The cvar clamps to [1, 512] m, but
  VoxelShadowMarch.cpp:118-126 states the 64 m default "keeps the box inside
  R0's level-0 residency (128 m radius), because this walk is level-0-only
  until the hierarchy lands". Marching past residency finds no bricks and reads
  LIT -- fail-lit, so it degrades safely rather than going black, but it would
  silently under-shadow. Extending reach is gated on taking the hierarchy, and
  that is the real remaining work in this wave.

  ONE NUMBER TO CHECK RATHER THAN TRUST: hit%=100.0. Every marched ray
  reporting a hit is a suspicious exactness -- it is the shape a stuck
  instrument makes. It may be sound at an 08:00 sun where the marched set is
  the near-field ground and grazing rays hit almost everything, and inside/f=124
  is small against 249,915 marched. Flagged for the V1 read; if V1's picture
  shows plausible shadow shapes rather than a uniformly dark near field, the
  counter is describing geometry and not a defect.

  CAL-A / CAL-B -- THE REPLICATE-NOISE BAR, RUN RATHER THAN ASSUMED.
  Two raster captures (quad path, cascades on, no shadow march) at the identical
  pose and frozen sun: VoxelVerify00115 and VoxelVerify00123.

    0.37% of 3,686,400 pixels differ by more than 8/255
    mean max-channel delta 1.58/255
    CONCENTRATION 0.42

  THE RASTER PATH REPRODUCES ITSELF TO 0.37%. That is the bar every judgement in
  this capture set is measured against, and it is far tighter than this project's
  history would suggest -- imgdiff.py's own header warns that "two images that
  look identical routinely differ by double digits" and records a control pair
  that differed by 85% because the exposure had moved between runs. It did not
  move here: settle, pose and sun are all pinned and logged.

  IT ALSO RETROACTIVELY SETTLES TODAY'S EARLIER COMPARISONS. Against a 0.37%
  floor:
    shadows alone      98.38%  (mean delta 86.01)  = ~266x the noise bar
    renderer alone     89.79%  (mean delta 45.58)  = ~243x
    both together      84.74%  (mean delta 55.64)
  None of those is ambiguous, and I had quoted them before this control existed.
  Having the bar does not change the numbers; it changes what may be claimed
  from them, which is the point of running it.

                      frames=0 emitFrames=0 | indexEntries=0

frames=0 emitFrames=0 indexEntries=0 at frame 0. Nothing had rendered. Of course

("Voxel march emit bindings (mode 2, scratch 1)"), and the mode-gate refusal at

The readback path was verified intact end to end by reading it: 3-slot ring

in VoxelMarch.usf:1153 -- and that is exactly the CPU/GPU word-count join class

THE FIX IS SCHEDULING, AND THE SANCTIONED HARNESS ALREADY DOES IT.

  voxel.DeferExec 200 voxel.Brick.Stats, voxel.DeferExec 205 voxel.March.Stats,
  voxel.DeferExec 235 voxel.March.Stats
-- two reads, deliberately, so convergence between them can be checked (:188-195),

VERIFYSOURCE IS A DIFFERENT AND REAL DEFECT -- "readback never lands" was my
wrong framing; the DISPATCH IS NEVER ARMED. bVolumeSettled (:4319) ANDs two
terms against the same SettleFrames=60, but they are in DIFFERENT UNITS:
  State->FramesSinceIndexChanged >= 60   counted per RENDER FRAME
  GStreamConvergedFrames >= 60           incremented in VoxelMarchPublishStreaming
                                         State, called from MaybeLogCounters,
                                         which runs ONCE PER -VoxelPerfLogInterval
                                         (default 5 s)
So the second term needs 60 x 5 s = ~5 MINUTES of continuously idle streaming.
Observed in downray-diag.log: "index quiet 4274 of the 60 required" (71x over)
while "streaming converged for 58 frames" crawls, and the process exits. The
cvar's help documents only the frame-counted term. Three further conditions on
that path (Volume.IsValid, bSourceCompareInFlight, VoxelMarchBindPool==false)
have NO decline log at all, so a source-1 leg with fluids off disarms the
comparator silently. Separately, under Source 1 bFrameIsVolumeFrame is forced

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 1.2b REVERTED. It violated Wave 1.2's own pre-registered gate.
================================================================================

FOUND BY AUDIT, not by the leg I ran to verify it -- I checked the counters the
change was ABOUT (retention) and not the counter the WAVE was gated on.

  leg                        resident0   evictions   cumulative released
  wave12b (retention off)       25,700           0               346,591
  wave12c (retention live)     125,941     151,657                55,359
                               ^^^^^^^^ 96% of the 131,072 pool

Wave 1.2's pre-registered gate was "evictions stays 0 -- eviction is what reads
as holes". wave12c violates it by 151,657, and the mechanism is direct:
retention defers the unload, the deferral keeps the record, the record keeps its
bricks, releases collapse 6x, the pool fills to 96%, and it begins evicting
farthest-from-focus. THAT IS THE EXACT FAILURE WAVE 1.2 EXISTS TO PREVENT --
eviction drops bricks the march index is still naming. Also measured on the same
change: tracked 28,021 -> 271,549 and pendingUnload frozen at 246,229, which is
what feeds the 25 ms exitScanMs.

FULL LEDGER OF THE CHANGE, and it loses on every line that matters:
  holes           19.5-20.7k -> 18.9-20.0k plateau   ~5-10%, matched windows
  p95             72.76 -> 90.71 ms                  +25%
  brick releases  346,591 -> 55,359                  6x fewer
  resident0       25,700 -> 125,941 of 131,072       96% full
  evictions       0 -> 151,657                       GATE VIOLATED

REVERTED, both gates, back to HoldsGeometry(). The observation that motivated it
STILL STANDS and is recorded at both sites: load-before-unload is dead code for
the marcher, and that is a real defect. But the fix is not "switch it on":
a mechanism sized for a few thousand quad records does not transfer to 271,549
tracked ones, and the retention site says why it could never have worked here
anyway -- "retention can only delay a hole, never manufacture a chunk". The
holes are the camera outrunning generation. Any retry needs a pool-pressure cap
and a much shorter LodRetentionMs than the quad path's 10 s, and must re-read
`evictions` as its gate.

THE PROCESS LESSON, WHICH IS THE POINT. I verified the change against the
counters it was about and reported it as working. The counter that condemned it
was on the SAME LOG LINE I had already quoted twice that night -- `evictions=0`
sits directly beside `released` and `resident0` in the brick-lifetime line I
added myself. Pre-registering a gate is worthless if the verification step only
reads the numbers the change was designed to move.

  REVERT VERIFIED (leg wave12-revert-verify, same LINE flight, same config):
    released=26,557 absent=1,729 | resident0=22,251 | evictions=0 writesDropped=0
  Against wave12c's resident0=125,941 / evictions=151,657. The pool is healthy
  again and Wave 1.2's gate holds. Wave 1.2 (brick freeing) stands; only 1.2b
  (retention for brick records) is gone.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 2 STEP 0 -- the verify gate now compares the WHOLE record
================================================================================

Done BEFORE the format grows, deliberately, because a gate converted in the same
change it is meant to catch is a gate nobody has tested.

voxel.GPU.VerifyBrickPack did not compare the record. It extracted five named
fields -- origin xyz, LevelAndFlags, BrickBase, the L1 mask -- and compared each
(VoxelGpuMeshAsyncVerify.cpp:1946-1976). DWORD 7 WAS NEVER READ. That was
survivable only while dword 7 was reserved-and-zero.

It stops being survivable the moment the record carries per-chunk shading,
because those fields are written by TWO INDEPENDENT PRODUCERS -- BuildChunkRecord
on the CPU arm and BrickChunkRecordMain on the GPU fork -- and a disagreement
between them has no error, no null and no counter. The fork takes a small share
of chunks and the worker the rest, so the symptom is patchy colour whose
distribution follows queue saturation. IT WOULD LOOK EXACTLY LIKE A BIOME.

Replaced with: build `Want[kChunkRecordDwords]` through
FVoxelBrickPool::BuildChunkRecord -- the same function the CPU upload path calls
-- and compare every dword. Now every field is gated, including every field
added after this line was written, for free and forever. The error names the
offending dword and prints both records, because "the record differs" is not
actionable on a record about to carry three unrelated field groups. This is the
shape VoxelCoverVerify.cpp:230-249 already used.

GATE FOR THIS STEP: voxel.GPU.VerifyBrickPack must still report recordFail=0
guardFail=0 on the UNCHANGED 8-dword format. If it now fails, the old gate was
wrong about something it never checked -- which is worth knowing before the
format moves, not after.

  STEP 0 RESULT: PASS, on the unchanged 8-dword format.
    "voxel.GPU.VerifyBrickPack: PASS -- the resident volume is byte-identical to
     the CPU reference -- 16/16 chunks checked against packChunkBricksCanonical.
     scratchDescFail=0 poolDescFail=0 occFail=0 matFail=0 recordFail=0
     guardFail=0 failed=0 sizeMismatch=0 doubleDelivered=0."
  The converted gate agrees with the old one on the format the old one was
  written for. That is what makes it usable as the gate for the format change.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 2 STEP 1 -- the record is 16 dwords. STRIDE ONLY, NO PAYLOAD.
================================================================================

Deliberately split from the field addition so that a stride mistake dies with
nothing else in the frame to confuse it. Every record is byte-identical to the
old format's 32 bytes followed by 32 zero bytes, so BOTH gates apply unchanged:
VerifyBrickPack must still be byte-identical, and the marcher must still render.

  kChunkRecordDwords 8 -> 16 (VoxelBrickPool.h:665 and the .cpp detail namespace)
  static_assert(... * 4 == 32) -> == 64                 VoxelBrickPool.cpp:559
  VOXEL_MARCH_CHUNK_RECORD_DWORDS 8u -> 16u             VoxelBrickTraverse.ush:1286
  BuildChunkRecord zeroes [7..N-1] with a LOOP bounded by the constant, not
    seven assignments -- so adding a field below cannot leave a later dword
    carrying a reused slot's previous tenant.
  VoxelBrickPoolWrite.usf: new `uint ChunkRecordDwords` parameter; both
    ChunkSlot*8u strides replaced by it; the record kernel zero-fills [7..N-1].
  BrickChunkClearMain: [numthreads(8,1,1)] + `tid.x >= 8u` -> [numthreads(64,1,1)]
    + `tid.x >= ChunkRecordDwords`. THE 8 MEANT TWO THINGS -- group size AND
    record length -- and that conflation is precisely how a grown record gets
    half-cleared on every eviction, leaving a re-allocated slot carrying the
    previous tenant's fields with no error anywhere.
  Both C++ parameter structs and both dispatch sites fill the stride from
    FVoxelBrickPool::kChunkRecordDwords (VoxelGpuWorldGen.cpp:600,617,1706,1732),
    so the kernels and the C++ writer read ONE constant.

WHY 16 AND NOT 10 (the minimum the payload needs): only 8 and 16 divide a 64 B
cache line. The traversal fetches dwords 0..6 for every chunk a ray enters and
the shading fetch wants 7..9 from the same line; at 10 or 12 most records
straddle two lines. Cost at the 131,072-chunk default is 4 MiB -> 8 MiB, ~1% of
the pool's committed total.

WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE YET: no producer passes shading, nothing reads
dwords 7..9, and VoxelMarch.usf is untouched. Those are steps 2-4.

  STEP 1 FIRST RUN: FAIL, recordFail=6 guardFail=1 -- AND THAT IS THE STEP
  WORKING. The gate converted in step 0 immediately caught a stride literal the
  old field-by-field gate could never have seen:

    RECORD differs at dword 0 -- got 98c4fc20 want ffffffc0.
    Full record got: [98c4fc20 0000026e 00000000 00040004
                      98c4fb50 0000026e 00000000 00040004]
    GUARD BAND: chunk record 16 dword 0 is 01520153 and belongs to no chunk.

  The "got" record is two repeated 4-dword groups of descriptor payload -- the
  signature of reading a buffer at the wrong stride -- and the guard band hit a
  slot past the region that was actually copied.

  CAUSE: TWO hardcoded 32-byte strides in the READBACK, not in the format.
    VoxelGpuMeshAsyncVerify.cpp:1744  AddEnqueueCopyPass(..., InChunkSlots * 8u * 4u)
    VoxelGpuMeshAsyncVerify.cpp:1806  Copy(..., ChunkSlots * 8u * 4u)
  Both fixed to kChunkRecordDwords.

  WHY THIS PAIR SURVIVED EVERY PREVIOUS CHECK: they agreed with each other at 8,
  and with the old gate's `ChunkSlot * 8` indexing. THREE literals, mutually
  consistent, all wrong together the moment the format moved -- so nothing could
  detect them until one of the three became a constant. This is the argument for
  parameterising a value everywhere rather than "where it matters": the sites
  that agree with each other are exactly the ones no test can separate.

  It also vindicates doing step 0 (convert the gate) BEFORE step 1 (move the
  format) rather than in one change. Had both landed together, this failure
  would have been ambiguous between "the new format is wrong" and "the new gate
  is wrong", and the natural response to an ambiguous gate failure is to distrust
  the gate.

  ALSO ADDED IN STEP 1 -- THE READER-SIDE STRIDE CROSS-CHECK, designed to empty
  the world rather than to warn.

  VoxelBrickTraverse.ush carries VOXEL_MARCH_CHUNK_RECORD_DWORDS as a #define
  while the pool's C++ owns kChunkRecordDwords. Growing the record means moving
  both, and if only one moves every lookup lands at the wrong offset -- not a
  crash and not a null: it reads a neighbouring record's fields, and enough of
  them survive the origin validation to draw SOMETHING. Terrain in the wrong
  place with no error is the worst outcome available on this path.

  So the stride is now BOUND (VOXEL_BRICK_POOL_PARAMETERS() gains
  VoxelBrickChunkRecordDwords, filled from kChunkRecordDwords in
  BindShaderParameters), and VoxelMarchLookupChunk compares the bound value
  against the #define immediately after the slot bounds check:

      if (VoxelBrickChunkRecordDwords != VOXEL_MARCH_CHUNK_RECORD_DWORDS)
      { return C; }

  One compare per lookup, and it converts BOTH remaining stride failures into a
  completely empty world: a stale #define, and a pass that forgot to bind the
  parameter (an unbound loose uint reads 0, which would otherwise collapse every
  lookup onto slot 0 and draw ONE CHUNK EVERYWHERE). Neither can be mistaken for
  terrain, which is the whole design goal -- this feature's history is failures
  that looked plausible.

  STEP 1 RESULT: PASS at 16 dwords.
    "voxel.GPU.VerifyBrickPack: PASS -- the resident volume is byte-identical to
     the CPU reference -- 16/16 chunks checked. recordFail=0 guardFail=0
     failed=0 sizeMismatch=0 doubleDelivered=0"
  taken with the MARCHER LIVE in the same leg (retired=1, brickPacks=83,113), so
  the CPU writer, the GPU kernel, the clear kernel and the reader all agree at
  the new stride. The remaining half of the step's gate -- a capture that matches
  the previous binary within the 0.37% replicate bar -- is queued behind the box.

  HARNESS NOTE, MY ERROR: the capture was launched while the previous leg was
  still in its linger and voxel-capture.ps1 refused it -- "REFUSING TO START:
  1 editor process(es) already running". The guard did its job; the one-editor
  rule is not advisory and a leg's linger counts.

  STEP 1 FULLY VERIFIED. Both halves of its gate:
    voxel.GPU.VerifyBrickPack   PASS, recordFail=0 guardFail=0, marcher live in
                                the same leg (retired=1, brickPacks=83,113)
    render                      VoxelVerify00127 vs the 8-dword binary's
                                VoxelVerify00117: 0.00% OF PIXELS DIFFER
                                (mean max-channel delta 1.01/255)
  Pixel-identical, against a measured replicate bar of 0.37%. The stride moved
  and nothing else did.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 2 STEP 2 -- the shading fields exist. Producers still neutral.
================================================================================

FVoxelBrickChunkShading (VoxelBrickPool.h): temperature u8, precipitation u8,
SurfaceZRelUU float, SurfaceGradPacked uint32, plus Neutral()/IsNeutral()/Pack().

THREE DESIGN POINTS THAT ARE NOT ARBITRARY:

1. SHADING IS A REQUIRED PARAMETER OF BuildChunkRecord AND BOTH AddChunkFrom*,
   NOT A DEFAULTED ONE. A default would let a producer that was never plumbed
   compile, link, run, and ship a flat mid-range climate with the surface gate
   disabled -- which renders as completely plausible terrain. Callers with no
   shading now say FVoxelBrickChunkShading::Neutral() explicitly, and that is
   greppable. Cover says it and says why (50 mm vegetation, no biome, no gate).

2. THE BIT LAYOUT LIVES IN ONE FUNCTION. Pack() produces the three dwords, and
   BOTH writers consume its output -- BuildChunkRecord assigns them directly,
   and the GPU kernel receives them as already-packed shader parameters and does
   no arithmetic at all. There is nothing left in HLSL that can lay these bits
   out differently from the C++.

3. THE GRADIENT PACKING IS MIRRORED, NOT RE-DERIVED. [12:23] is QX and [0:11] is
   QY because VoxelClimate::PackSurfaceGradients returns QX*4096 + QY and
   VoxelQuadVertexFactory.ush already decodes (bits>>12)&0xFFF as dz/dx. A
   second encoding of the same quantity is exactly how two renderers come to
   disagree about where the ground is.
   SurfaceZRelUU stays float32 because -1.0e30f (kNoSurfaceGate) is a legal
   in-band value meaning "no gate", written by three existing producers; any
   fixed-point form would need a sentinel four orders of magnitude outside the
   data range.

GATE FOR THIS STEP, and it is the same pair as step 1 BY CONSTRUCTION: every
producer passes Neutral(), so both writers pack identical bits and nothing reads
dwords 7..9 yet. VerifyBrickPack must still be byte-identical AND the render
must still be pixel-identical. If either moves, the fields are being written
differently by the two producers -- which is the exact failure the whole-record
gate was converted to catch, arriving on schedule.

  STEP 2 RESULT: PASS. VerifyBrickPack byte-identical at 16 dwords with the
  shading fields present and every producer passing Neutral(). The two writers
  agree on the new dwords.

  THIRD SAFEGUARD ADDED (the plan's, and it is the one that catches the failure
  neither of the other two can): GetChunksAddedWithNeutralShading(), incremented
  in both AddChunkFrom* when the argument IsNeutral(), and printed on the
  "Voxel brick lifetime (5s window)" line as neutralShading=.

  WHY IT IS ON THAT LINE AND NOT BEHIND A COMMAND: the whole-record gate proves
  the two WRITERS agree, and the stride cross-check proves the READER agrees --
  but neither can see a PRODUCER that was never plumbed. That producer compiles,
  links, runs, and ships a flat mid-range climate with the gate disabled, which
  renders as entirely plausible terrain. In a healthy leg this counter must
  equal the cover-chunk count (cover is 50 mm vegetation, legitimately neutral)
  and nothing more; a terrain producer appearing in it is the plumbing having
  silently not landed. This project has shipped that exact shape twice --
  -VoxelBrickPack=1 with a fork carrying zero traffic, and a weathering pass
  that removed 20 voxels of 90,000 for months.

  HARNESS NOTE, MY SECOND ONE TONIGHT: a build attempted while the leg's editor
  was still alive failed with LNK1104 on both DLLs. Builds and legs cannot
  overlap in EITHER direction -- the leg holds the binaries the build wants to
  write, exactly as the build would steal the cores the leg is measuring.

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 2 STEP 3 (CPU arm) -- real climate and surface plane in the
                                       record. GPU fork deliberately left behind.
================================================================================

Split from the GPU arm on purpose, so the neutralShading counter added in step 2
gets to demonstrate itself: after this change it should read cover chunks PLUS
the GPU fork's share, and after the GPU arm lands it should fall to cover alone.
An instrument whose first reading is its expected steady state has never been
shown to move.

  ShadingFromChunkParams(FVector4f) -- a CONVERSION of SampleChunkParamsForPool's
  output, not a second sampler. The quad path and the marcher must agree about
  where the ground is and what the climate is; two samplers is how they stop.
  The byte round trip is exact: the sampler emits k/255.0f for integer k, and
  round(x * 255.0f) recovers k for all 256 values in float32.

  VoxelBrickCpuArm::Publish now TAKES the shading rather than sampling it. That
  namespace is defined ~10,000 lines before the sampler, and more to the point
  the sampler needs a scene Root only the caller holds -- and both callers
  already hold it for the quad path's identical call.

A FRAME BUG I WROTE AND CAUGHT BEFORE BUILDING. My first version passed
`ChunkOriginWorldForLevel(...) - GpuPoolRebase`. The sampler adds
Root.GetComponentLocation() itself, so its argument is in the ROOT frame; the
quad path passes ChunkOriginWorldForLevel with NO rebase (:13333, :13617).
Subtracting the rebase would have sampled the climate and the surface plane at
the wrong place on the map -- and the result would still have looked like
terrain, which is the only reason it is worth writing down. Checked against the
existing caller rather than reasoned about.

GATE: VerifyBrickPack must still PASS (the harness publishes and references with
Neutral() on both sides, so it is unaffected), the render must still be
pixel-identical (nothing reads dwords 7..9 until step 4), and neutralShading
must be NON-ZERO and roughly cover + GPU-fork chunks.

  STEP 3 (CPU ARM) RESULT: PASS, and the new counter accounted for itself
  EXACTLY.

    VerifyBrickPack: PASS (byte-identical)
    neutralShading = 3220
    brickPacks     = 94020   brickFromGpu = 3220

  neutralShading EQUALS brickFromGpu, to the chunk. Every remaining
  neutral-shaded chunk is a GPU-fork chunk, and all 90,800 CPU-arm chunks now
  carry real climate and a real surface plane. That is a stronger statement than
  "the counter is non-zero": it says precisely WHICH producer is still
  unplumbed, without anyone having to guess.

  This is why the CPU and GPU arms were split across two steps. Had they landed
  together, neutralShading would have gone from 0 to 0 -- the correct final
  reading, and completely uninformative about whether the counter works at all.
  An instrument whose first observation is its expected steady state has never
  been shown to move.

  ALSO LANDED (the plan's second blocker): GpuPoolRoot is now assigned every
  tick in TickStreaming, not only inside GetOrCreateGpuPool. That constructor is
  a QUAD-PATH one, and under retirement the only thing keeping it alive is the
  deliberately-empty stand-in pool GI needs to find -- so the GPU fork's climate
  sampling would have depended on a component Phase 5 intends to delete, and the
  failure would have been silent (null root -> neutral -> plausible terrain).

  GATE FOR STEP 3b: neutralShading must FALL from 3,220 to essentially zero
  (only cover chunks may remain), with VerifyBrickPack still PASS and the render
  still pixel-identical.

  STEP 3b (GPU ARM) RESULT: neutralShading = 0.

  From 3,220 to zero. Every chunk admitted to the brick pool now carries real
  per-chunk climate and a real fitted surface plane, from all three producers --
  the CPU worker arm, the game-thread edit re-mesh, and the GPU fork -- sampled
  through ONE function with one Root and one origin convention.

  The counter did the job it was added for, in both directions: it went non-zero
  when a producer was unplumbed, named which one by matching brickFromGpu
  exactly, and went to zero when that producer was plumbed. A gate that can only
  read zero proves nothing; this one has now been observed at 3,220 and at 0.

  WHAT REMAINS IN WAVE 2 IS STEP 4, AND IT IS THE ONLY STEP THAT CHANGES A PIXEL:
  VoxelMarch.usf must READ dwords 7..9 and apply them -- the surface-proximity
  gate first (that is the term whose absence is actually wrong underground), then
  climate. Everything up to here has been deliberately invisible, which is what
  made each step verifiable against "byte-identical and pixel-identical".

================================================================================
2026-08-22  WAVE 2 STEP 4 -- the surface-proximity gate. The first pixel change.
================================================================================

WHAT WAS ACTUALLY MISSING, restated because the obvious answer is wrong and I
believed it for part of the night.

The obvious answer is "the marcher has no biome tint". But BiomeTintForFace
IGNORES the material id and returns 255 for a sky-facing Z face, 0 for a ceiling
and 140 for a side -- a purely geometric top/side/bottom distinction. The
marcher already has that: VoxelPaletteFace is indexed [material * 3 + faceClass]
and MAT_GRASS already carries green on top, darker green on the side and brown
underneath. Applying the tint here TOO would apply the same distinction twice,
against ADR-0008 invariant 4.

What the quad path has and the marcher does not is the GATE:
    TerrainTintR = bNearSurface ? FaceTint : 0      (VoxelQuadVertexFactory:373)
Without it, a +Z face two hundred metres down a cave is tinted as turf. That is
not a missing refinement, it is a wrong answer underground, which is why it
lands before any climate work.

IMPLEMENTATION, AND ONE DESIGN CHOICE WORTH DEFENDING.

  reader   VoxelBrickTraverse.ush: FVoxelMarchChunkCache gains ClimatePacked,
           SurfaceGradPacked and SurfaceZRelUU, read from dwords 7..9 of the
           line the lookup has already paid for -- which is the entire reason
           the record is 16 dwords and not 10.
           VoxelMarchNoChunk() initialises them NEUTRAL, not zero: a zeroed
           SurfaceZRelUU would claim a real surface plane at the chunk's own
           origin, so an absent chunk would gate as though the ground were at
           z = 0. -1e30 (kNoSurfaceGate) is what "nothing here" should mean.

  plane    VoxelMarchSurfaceZRelAt TRANSCRIBES VoxelQuadVertexFactory.ush:326-338
           -- the 12+12 split, the 2048 bias, the 256 scale. Not re-derived. A
           second derivation of the same plane is exactly how two renderers come
           to disagree about where the ground is.

  apply    THE GATE CHANGES THE FACE-CLASS CHOICE, NOT THE PIXEL'S BRIGHTNESS.
           ADR-0008 invariant 4 says face class is for MATERIAL difference, and
           the palette already honours it -- [mat + TOP] is the sky-facing
           appearance, [mat + SIDE] the exposed one. Below the band a Z face is
           not sky-facing in any meaningful sense; it is a cave roof or floor.
           So the honest correction is to stop selecting the TOP entry, NOT to
           darken the pixel. Written as a lerp toward the SIDE entry (no
           divergence) and gated on FaceClass == TOP so a genuine ceiling keeps
           its own entry. The per-voxel jitter is carried across as a ratio so
           invariant 3 survives.

  A chunk with kNoSurfaceGate is ALWAYS surface, so the gate can never darken
  terrain it has no information about -- fail-open, in the direction that
  preserves today's appearance.

EXPECTED: this is the FIRST step in the wave that moves a pixel, so unlike
steps 1-3 the render must NOT be identical. The change should be confined to
+Z faces well below the fitted surface -- cave roofs and deep overhangs -- and
absent from open ground. Captures to the owner; no verdict from me.

  STEP 4 SECOND FAILURE, AND IT IS AN ARCHITECTURAL ONE WORTH RECORDING.

    Fatal: FVoxelMarchEmitPS's required shader parameter
           FVoxelMarchEmitParameters::MarchChunkIndex was not set.

  FVoxelMarchChunkIndex::Register hands back the STAGED buffer once and clears
  bStagedValid; the march pass, earlier in the frame, consumes it. The pooled
  copy it queues for extraction is not valid until that graph executes -- so the
  emit pass's Register in the same frame returns nullptr and leaves
  MarchChunkIndex UNSET.

  THAT WAS HARMLESS FOR AS LONG AS THE EMIT NEVER REFERENCED THE INDEX. An unset
  binding for an unreferenced parameter is not required, so the hole sat there
  invisibly. The surface gate references it, and UE fatals immediately.

  FIXED IN THE EMIT PASS ONLY, NOT IN VoxelMarchBindPool, and the asymmetry is
  the point. Register's own comment says binding null reads as zeros, zero means
  "not resident", and a march against that draws an EMPTY WORLD with no error.
  True -- for the MARCH, which traverses the index. The EMIT uses it for exactly
  one thing: the hit chunk's fitted surface plane. A miss there leaves
  SurfaceBandFade at 1.0, which is precisely the appearance before this wave. So
  the fallback is fail-open in the direction that preserves the picture, and
  putting it in the shared binder would have converted the march's loud fatal
  into a silent empty world -- trading the one failure mode this project can
  actually see for the one it cannot.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-08-22  THROUGHPUT: the "~600 chunks/s" figure this plan quotes is STALE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Measured on tonight's own legs, from the "Voxel tick budget (5s window)" line:

  wave12b   ticks=96  tickMs=3646.5 (72.93% of wall) | dispatch=1927.4
            brickFlush=1509.8  brickPacks=8845  (1,756/s)
  wave12c   ticks=87  tickMs=4198.9 (83.98% of wall) | dispatch=1863.8
            brickFlush=1363.1  brickPacks=8213  (1,629/s)

1,629-1,756 chunks/s, not 600. docs/backlog.md records the 600 figure CLOSED at
797/s weeks ago. Planning a fix against 600 would size the wrong thing, and
marcher-plan-addendum-2026-08-22.md repeats it -- corrected there.

AND THE HEADLINE THROUGHPUT COUNTER IS DEAD ON THIS ARM. `chunksPerSec=0.0` on
the job-flow line: it derives from TotalChunksLoaded, which is keyed to GEOMETRY
PUBLICATION, so under retirement it is zero forever. Anyone quoting "chunks/s"
from a marcher log is quoting a constant. USE brickPacks (N/s) ON THE TICK-BUDGET
LINE. This is the same class as `chunksLoaded=0` already recorded in
[[voxelsim-marcher-ships-4x]] -- a counter that is correct by its own definition
and useless for the question being asked.

WHERE THE CEILING ACTUALLY IS, on this evidence: throughput = MaxJobsInFlight x
frame rate, and both legs land on that identity exactly (96 slots x fps, against
96.2 and 98.4 dispatched per tick). The frame rate is set by the streaming tick
itself, which is 73-84% of wall clock, and the largest single item inside it is
DispatchJobs at ~0.21 ms of game thread per dispatched chunk -- reproducible
across two legs with tracked counts an order of magnitude apart, so it is not
O(tracked). brickFlush is the second at 32-41% of the tick.

NOT ACTED ON, AND SAYING SO. The candidate fix -- caching
FootprintSurfaceUpperBoundMm, the one uncached worldgen computation on the
dispatch path, whose sibling FootprintSolidFloorMmCached twenty lines away IS
cached -- is a hypothesis from reading, not a profile. DispatchJobs has no
per-stage brackets (recompute has exitScanMs/sortMs/entryMs; dispatch has
nothing). The honest first move is to add those brackets, not to write the cache.

  STEP 4 CAPTURE: 0.00% OF PIXELS CHANGED. VoxelVerify00129 against
  VoxelVerify00127 (same binary lineage, gate off), mean max-channel delta
  1.04/255, frame mean identical to 0.1 (90.4 / 79.3 / 67.9 both).

  THAT IS THE EXPECTED RESULT AT THIS POSE AND IT IS NOT YET EVIDENCE OF
  ANYTHING. The camera is 40 m above open ground at pitch -20; every +Z face in
  frame IS near the fitted surface, so a correct gate does nothing here. But "a
  change that does nothing observable" is exactly the silent-no-op class this
  project has been bitten by repeatedly -- the weathering pass that removed 20
  voxels of 90,000, the fork that carried zero traffic. A correct gate and a gate
  that never fires are indistinguishable from this capture.

  SO IT IS BEING MUTATION-PROVED BEFORE IT IS BELIEVED. The comparison is
  temporarily INVERTED (bNearSurface := !bNearSurface), which must repaint every
  sky-facing face on open ground with the SIDE palette entry -- a large, obvious
  change at exactly this pose. If the mutation arm also renders 0.00%, the gate
  is not wired, the plane data is not arriving, or the lookup is missing, and the
  "correct no-op" reading above would have been a fiction.

  MUTATION ARM RESULT: 0.00% CHANGED. THE GATE WAS A SILENT NO-OP.

  VoxelVerify00131 (inverted) against VoxelVerify00129 (correct): 0.00% of
  pixels differ, frame mean identical to 0.1 in all three channels. An INVERTED
  surface gate must repaint every sky-facing face on open ground; it repainted
  nothing. So bValid was false on every pixel and the gate never fired.

  AND THE UNMUTATED CAPTURE READ AS A CLEAN PASS. 0.00% against the ungated
  build is exactly what a CORRECT gate produces at a pose with no sub-surface +Z
  faces in view. The two readings are identical and mean opposite things. Had I
  stopped at the first one -- which looked like a textbook "no regression"
  result -- this wave would have shipped a feature that does nothing, and the
  next person would have found it by wondering why caves were still tinted turf.

  ROOT CAUSE: I used V.LocalVoxel where the lookup needs the ABSOLUTE chunk
  coordinate. V.LocalVoxel is relative to the march's frame origin; the chunk
  index and the records are keyed on world chunk coords -- which is precisely
  why the line above my block computes WorldVoxel at all, with a comment saying
  "adding the level-0 frame origin back". Every lookup missed. Fixed to
  WorldVoxel >> 5, and LocalInChunkUU with it.

  THIS IS THE THIRD COORDINATE-FRAME DEFECT TONIGHT: the GpuPoolRebase one I
  caught by reading before building, this one caught only by mutation, and both
  sit next to the marcher's own long-standing warning that "THE ORIGIN MUST BE
  TAKEN AT THE HIT'S OWN LEVEL". Frames are the recurring hazard on this path;
  every new consumer of V.LocalVoxel should be read against WorldVoxel before it
  is trusted.

  MUTATION AFTER THE FRAME FIX: THE GATE FIRES, BUT ONLY JUST.

    inverted gate vs ungated   0.39% of pixels, mean delta 2.49/255
                               frame mean 90.4/79.3/67.9 -> 88.6/77.7/66.4
                               CONCENTRATION 0.33 (localised, not diffuse)
    (before the frame fix, the same mutation gave 0.00% and no mean shift)

  So the mechanism is WIRED: lookups now succeed, the fitted plane is real data,
  and inverting the comparison moves the picture. That is a genuine advance on
  the silent no-op.

  BUT 0.39% SITS ALMOST ON THE 0.37% REPLICATE-NOISE BAR, and an inverted
  surface gate ought to repaint every sky-facing face on open ground -- a large
  fraction of this frame, not a third of a percent. So the honest statement is:
  THE GATE IS WIRED AND FIRES SOMEWHERE; IT HAS NOT BEEN SHOWN TO FIRE BROADLY.
  The mean did shift in all three channels (which noise does not do), so this is
  not purely the floor -- but it is not the demonstration I wanted either.

  NOT CHASED FURTHER TONIGHT, AND NOT DRESSED UP AS DONE. The candidates I have
  not separated: most visible faces at this pose may be SIDE rather than TOP (the
  gate only touches TOP); the emit pass may still be taking the zero-index
  fallback on many frames; or the band may genuinely be satisfied nearly
  everywhere here, which is what a correct gate would do.

  THE RIGHT NEXT INSTRUMENT IS A COUNTER, NOT ANOTHER CAPTURE: a per-frame tally
  of pixels where the lookup succeeded and where the gate actually changed the
  face class. Two numbers, and they separate all three candidates at once. A
  capture cannot, which is the same lesson as the 0.00%-means-two-things pair
  above.

  SHIPPED AS-IS DELIBERATELY. The gate is fail-open -- a failed lookup or
  kNoSurfaceGate leaves SurfaceBandFade at 1.0 -- and the correct build measured
  0.00% against the ungated one, so it cannot regress the picture. It is in the
  tree, doing at most what it should, pending the counter.
